# Deferred-Anchor Registry — Harness

> The canonical source of truth for every `D-*` anchor cited in `src/` but
> deferred to a future cycle. The leading underscore sorts this file before
> the numbered plans in directory listings — call this out as the registry,
> not a plan. Per-plan §3.1 rows are still the preferred home for anchors
> whose feature area maps cleanly onto a plan; this file catches the orphans
> + cross-cutting anchors that don't belong to any single plan.
>
> **CI/pre-commit guard** (`scripts/check-anchor-registry/check-anchor-registry.{sh,ps1}`): every
> `D-*` identifier appearing in `src/` MUST resolve to a row in ANY of the three
> registry documents (a closed anchor's row lives in `-done.md`, and a citation of
> it must still resolve)
> OR a citation in any `.plans/*.md`. Pre-fix this recurred TWICE
> (memory: "the same gap silent-failure caught for MULTI-PAGE-GOT").
> The guard makes the system enforce the discipline — same shape as the
> agnosticism veto + the `CallConv` static_assert.
>
> **★ ANCHOR-NAME RULE — READ BEFORE INVENTING A `D-*` NAME (documented 2026-07-28, TF-C81; it was
> load-bearing and unwritten until it bit).** The guard's regex is
> `D-[A-Z0-9_]+(-[A-Z0-9_]+){2,}` — **`D-` plus THREE OR MORE `-`-separated segments is a
> DEFERRED-WORK ANCHOR and MUST resolve to a row here; a TWO-segment `D-*` name (`D-OPT`) is an
> informal feature label the guard ignores.** So hyphenating a COMPOUND FEATURE WORD silently changes
> the identifier's CLASS: `D-CSUBSET-ALWAYS-INLINE` tripped the guard and had to be renamed
> `D-CSUBSET-ALWAYSINLINE` to match its sibling `D-CSUBSET-NOINLINE`. The failure surfaces as
> “missing row”, which misdirects you into WRITING a duplicate row instead of FIXING the name.
> **Spell a compound feature word as ONE segment (`ALWAYSINLINE`, `NOINLINE`); reserve hyphens for
> genuine name components; when in doubt, match the sibling anchor already in this file.** Full
> write-up: [[D-PLANS-ANCHOR-NAME-SEGMENT-COUNT-GATE]].
>
> **V2-4.X / full-C ownership (2026-06-10):** the C-language-feature deferral
> CLUSTER — modulo codegen (`D-CSUBSET-MOD-OP-CODEGEN`, …), primitive widths
> (`D-LANG-PLATFORM-DEPENDENT-PRIMITIVE-WIDTH`), linkage/visibility syntax
> (`D-CSUBSET-LINKAGE-VISIBILITY-SYNTAX`, `D-DECL-PREFIX-STRIP-SHARED-HELPER`),
> rodata aggregates (`D-LK4-RODATA-PRODUCER-NONSTRING-ARRAY`), non-main implicit
> return (`D-LK10-ENTRY-MAIN-IMPLICIT-RETURN` — the OPEN non-main successor row,
> cross-ref `D-CSUBSET-ENTRY-NAME-SPLIT`), variadic FFI
> (`D-FFI-DESCRIPTOR-VARIADIC-SIGNATURE`), the apple-arm64 ABI divergence
> (`D-FF3-APPLE-ARM64-ABI-DIVERGENCE`), and peers — is now OWNED by **stepper
> V2-4.X / [`23-full-c-plan`](./23-full-c-plan%20-%20tbd.md)** (c-subset → full
> C23). Each closes inside the relevant FC phase (see the plan §3.1).
>
> **NOT in this cluster — already shipped, do NOT re-claim as new FC work**
> (caught by the 2026-06-10 plan-lock accuracy review). Two genuinely-CLOSED
> anchors: `D-CSUBSET-DIVISION-OP-CODEGEN` (✅ 2026-06-04 — signed division shipped;
> the modulo successor `D-CSUBSET-MOD-OP-CODEGEN` ✅ since closed too — FC1 cycle 1, 2026-06-10, above) and `D-FFI-EXTERN-CALL-DISPATCH`
> (✅ 2026-06-08 — direct-plt/indirect-slot dispatch shipped; the OPEN variadic concern
> is `D-FFI-DESCRIPTOR-VARIADIC-SIGNATURE`, above). PLUS one closed FEATURE with no
> anchor row: the `main` implicit-return-0 (shipped 2026-06-02, live in
> `c.lang.json`, config-only) — the anchor `D-LK10-ENTRY-MAIN-IMPLICIT-RETURN`
> itself remains the still-OPEN non-main successor (above), NOT a closed item. The
> full-C arc supersedes each predecessor with its named successor, never by reopening it.
>
> New full-C exotic-tail exclusions land as `D-FULLC-*` rows as clusters ship.
> Individual rows are NOT rewritten here yet (the plan is the live map until a
> cycle actually closes the anchor).
>
> **★★ MEASUREMENT CAVEAT — READ BEFORE QUOTING ANY “this will clear N errors” NUMBER IN THIS FILE (2026-07-28, macOS-arm64 predefine-probe cycle).** DSS **abandons a TU at its first hard parse failure**, so a corpus error count is a count of the errors REACHED, never of the defects PRESENT. Clearing an early blocker therefore EXPOSES later ones that were never reached, and a drop in the total is NOT a count of defects fixed. Worked example from this cycle: the 189-TU sqlite build went **213 → 80** total errors when architecture + toolchain predefines were both supplied — that is **NOT 133 defects fixed**; the residual 80 is a NEWLY-EXPOSED population whose composition was unknown until separately measured. It is equally possible for a real fix to make the total go UP (this cycle measured exactly that: toolchain-identity macros ALONE took 213 → 216). Every forward estimate of the form “closing X clears N errors” in any row below MUST be read with this caveat, and every new one MUST carry it. See [[D-MEASURE-FIRST-FAILURE-MASKS-RESIDUAL]] for the citable class row.
>
> **Schema (one row per anchor)**:
> ```
> | Anchor | Priority | Status | Trigger | Closing work | Cross-refs |
> ```
>
> When an anchor closes, replace its row's status with `✅ CLOSED <date>`,
> cite the commit/SymbolId, and **MOVE the row into**
> [`_deferred-anchor-registry-done.md`](_deferred-anchor-registry-done.md) —
> `scripts/apply-registry-row/apply-registry-row.py` does both in one step, and
> `check-anchor-balance` fails the tree if the move is skipped. Don't DELETE the
> row: the audit trail is
> load-bearing for future regressions.
>

> the user authorized a "robustness sweep," which FIRED the trigger for three
> trigger-gated anchors and CLOSED them in one sweep (393/393 ctest, MSVC
> Release + MinGW): `D-CSUBSET-BLOCK-SCOPE-PROTOTYPE`,
> `D-CSUBSET-EXTERN-DEFINITION-MERGE`, and
> `D-SEMANTIC-ASSIGN-STMT-ASSIGNABILITY-BYPASS` (see their rows below). The
> sweep surfaced one new backlog row, `D-CSUBSET-TENTATIVE-DEFINITION-MERGE`
> (C 6.9.2 tentative definitions — still fail-loud, trigger-gated). [That backlog
> row was later ✅ CLOSED 2026-06-28 (p19 Cluster G c33) when the sqlite3.c
> re-probe fired its trigger — see its row below.]

> ⚠⚠ **THIS REGISTRY IS ONE OF THREE, AND IT HOLDS ONLY HARNESS WORK THAT IS STILL
> OPEN (190 rows).** Not one row here is finished. A row that closes is MOVED OUT.
> Its siblings are [`_deferred-anchor-registry-production.md`](_deferred-anchor-registry-production.md) -- the other half of the
> live work -- and [`_deferred-anchor-registry-done.md`](_deferred-anchor-registry-done.md),
> the archive, which already holds 441 closed harness rows lifted out of this file.
>
> **★★★ THE MOVE-ON-CLOSE RULE (operator, 2026-09-01).** *"the
> `_deferred-anchor-registry-{harness|production}.md` is a list of remaining items, that
> always delete a done item and put into `_deferred-anchor-registry-done.md` once
> finished."* So this file answers exactly one question -- **what is left** -- and a
> reader who wants the count of remaining work can take the length of the table below.
> Before 2026-09-01 this file held 1,555 closed rows against 523 open ones: three
> quarters of every orientation read was finished work, and the priority was buried
> under its own audit trail.
> ⚠ **THE AUDIT TRAIL IS NOT DELETED, IT IS RELOCATED.** The preamble above still
> says never to delete a closed row and that is still true -- `done.md` is where it goes.
> ⚠ **A CLOSED ROW IN THIS FILE IS A REFUSAL**, not untidiness:
> `check-anchor-balance` fails the tree for it. Close a row with
> `scripts/apply-registry-row/apply-registry-row.py`, which performs the move itself --
> hand-editing the table is how the two halves drift.
>
> **THE LINE BETWEEN PRODUCTION AND HARNESS, so a borderline row is decided by a rule and
> not by taste:**
> **PRODUCTION** is a defect a USER OF THE COMPILER could hit -- it is in the shipped binary
> or in the config that binary reads (front end, preprocessor, semantics, HIR/MIR/LIR,
> optimiser, assembler, linker, object formats, FFI surface, diagnostics, CLI, emitted
> runtime).
> **HARNESS** is a defect only WE can hit -- tests, gates, guards, the cycle machinery,
> plans, scripts, carriages, CI, the example corpus AS a corpus.
> ⚠ `D-CONFIG-*` and `D-DIAG-*` are PRODUCTION deliberately: a `.lang/.target/.format.json`
> document IS the compiler's behaviour in this architecture, and a diagnostic is its output to a
> user. Filing either as tooling would hide real conformance defects in the harness pile, which is
> the exact thing that split exists to stop.
> ⚠ A row's bucket follows the DEFECT, never the instrument that found it: a corpus example that
> reveals a codegen bug files under codegen.
>
> ★ **RESOLUTION READS ALL THREE FILES; ORIENTATION READS ONLY THE TWO WORKING ONES.**
> A `D-*` cited in `src/` must resolve wherever its row now lives, so every guard that
> RESOLVES a citation globs `_deferred-anchor-registry*.md`. Every instrument that asks
> *what is left* -- `burndown-queue`, the cycle's priority pick -- reads production and
> harness ONLY. Reading the archive to choose work is how a closed row got recommended
> three times in this project's history.

## Anchor Index

**Row shape: EXACTLY six cells — `Anchor`, `Priority`, `Status`, `Trigger`, `Closing work`, `Cross-refs`.**
★★★ **`Priority` IS `P0`..`P5` AND `Status` IS ONE OF `✅ CLOSED` / `🟠 OPEN` / `⏳ GATED` — a three-value controlled vocabulary, added
2026-09-01 on the operator's instruction: *"add columns for priority and status ... this way
we always have clean statuses."* Before that the verdict was the FIRST GLYPH of a prose blob
that also carried the trigger, the history and the retraction, and every reader had to agree
where the verdict stopped — which is precisely the agreement that failed four times in
`check-anchor-balance`'s own comment history.
⚠ **THE STATUS CELL KEEPS ITS GLYPH, AND THE GLYPH IS THE CONTRACT.** A row is CLOSED iff its
status cell OPENS with ✅ after stripping `*_ ` — the complement defined, never the variants,
so a glyph nobody has thought of yet counts OPEN. A column holding the bare WORD `CLOSED`
would make that test false for every closed row at once. The word is for the reader.
⚠ **`Priority` IS A DECLARATION, NOT A SIEVE RESULT.** `burndown-queue` seeds it and then
READS it; its own docstring warns that a band is *"a sort key, not a verdict"* because a
census built from that keyword sieve reported 103 where the truth was 4. Correct the cell and
the correction survives, which re-running a sieve never does.
★ **DO NOT HAND-WRITE A ROW.** `scripts/anchors/anchors.py` (and its `write-anchor` /
`set-anchor` / `read-anchor` / `read-anchors` launchers) takes the FIELDS and produces the row,
so a wrapped id, an unescaped pipe and a wrong cell count are all inexpressible.
A row with fewer cells shifts every following cell one column LEFT; a row with more
has the surplus SILENTLY DROPPED by the renderer. Neither is visible when reading the
raw text, so the count is load-bearing — see `D-PLANS-REGISTRY-MALFORMED-ROW-CELLS`.

Two authoring rules follow from that, both verified against the GitHub renderer:
- **A raw `|` splits a cell EVEN INSIDE BACKTICKS.** To quote an expression, regex or
  alternation containing one, write `\|` — the backslash-escaped form is the *only*
  one that survives inside a code span (an HTML entity does **not**).
- **` ═══ ` inside a cell marks a seam where two cells were joined** when a row
  authored to a wider schema was converged onto these four columns. It is a joint in
  the prose, not a column boundary.

⚠ **APPEND AT END-OF-TABLE, NEVER AT END-OF-FILE.** This registry once carried a
second `## Anchor Index (continued)` table, which existed only because rows had been
appended past the Allowlist over many cycles; its header declared TWO columns, so every
fuller row silently lost its `Closing work` and `Cross-refs`. The 2026-08-25
production/harness split merged both tables, so each file now has exactly ONE index and
the accident cannot recur here -- but the rule that prevents it still has to be obeyed.

| Anchor | Priority | Status | Trigger | Closing work | Cross-refs |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `D-TEST-REDONDISABLE-DECAY` | P3 | 🟠 OPEN | ★★★ **UPDATED 2026-07-29 (TF-C90) — TWO MORE VACUITY SPECIES, THE *NINTH* AND *TENTH* IN FIVE CYCLES. ⭐⭐ AND THE MOST ENCOURAGING FACT ABOUT BOTH IS *WHO* CAUGHT THEM: TF-C90 CAUGHT THEM IN ITS *OWN FRESH TESTS*, BEFORE SHIPPING — not a later sweep, not an audit. That is this row working as intended, and it is the first update where the class was self-detected at authoring time.** ⚠ **SPECIES (ix) — THE ASSERTION'S OWN PROMOTION RULES DESTROYED THE PROPERTY IT WAS ASSERTING.** ✔MEASURED: `(u_int)-1 == 4294967295u` **PASSED even with `u_int` deliberately mis-typed as `i32`** — the usual arithmetic conversions erase the very property under test, so the assertion could not distinguish a correct 32-bit unsigned typedef from a wrong signed one. ★ **THE TRANSFERABLE RULE: IN C, AN EQUALITY COMPARISON IS A *WEAK* WITNESS FOR A TYPE'S IDENTITY, BECAUSE THE COMPARISON ITSELF CONVERTS BOTH OPERANDS BEFORE THE ASSERTION EVER RUNS. Pin a type with `_Generic`, `sizeof`, or a signedness-revealing operation (e.g. a right-shift or a division of a negative value), never with a promoted-away `==`.** ⚠ **SPECIES (x) — A POSITIVE/NEGATIVE PAIR WITH *NO LIVE ARM*, MASKED BY AN EARLIER FAILURE.** ✔MEASURED: a `_Generic` positive/negative pair for `caddr_t` had NO live arm at all, because **every wrong pointer model dies at the INITIALIZER with `S0003` first** — the intended discriminator never executed, so the "negative" arm proved nothing about `_Generic` and the pair was decorative. ★ **THIS IS [[D-MEASURE-FIRST-FAILURE-MASKS-RESIDUAL]] INSIDE A SINGLE TEST rather than across a census: an earlier, louder failure consumed the input before the guard could speak. THE RULE: a negative arm must be shown to FAIL *FOR THE STATED REASON* — if a different diagnostic fires first, the arm is vacuous even though the test is red.** ⭐ **TALLY: ten species in five cycles, and the two newest are both "the test's own semantics silently neutralised the test" rather than "the pin drifted from the code" — which is a DIFFERENT sub-family from species (i)–(viii) and worth naming as such if an eleventh appears.** **[orig:]** ✅ **RECOGNISED + SWEPT 2026-07-27 (TF-C71) — a RECURRING CLASS, not an incident: a written RED-ON-DISABLE instruction decays, and decays SILENTLY.** A red-on-disable is a fact about ONE BUILD. Two mechanisms retire one without touching the test, so the test stays GREEN while its stated guarantee is void: **(1) a later guard SUBSUMES the one named** — TF-C70 added `sbFirstOnLine`, which subsumed the pre-scan line skip, so 'collapse the skip and this reds' became false the same cycle it was measured; **(2) closing a ROOT CAUSE DELETES the workaround the instruction names** — TF-C71 deleted `directiveOperandText`'s `tail[0] == '"'` guard, so `TfC70OperandKeepsClosingStringDelimiter`'s instruction pointed at code that no longer exists. ★ MECHANISM (2) IS STRUCTURAL AND WILL RECUR EVERY TIME A ROOT CAUSE IS CLOSED: every consumer-level workaround you delete takes its red-on-disable instruction with it. 'An instruction to disable deleted code reads as VERIFIED when nothing is verifying it.' ★ ALSO FOUND IN THE SAME SWEEP — a helper that made pins inert: `reconstructStringLiteral` APPENDED A HARDCODED `\"`, so every stringize assertion built the closing quote itself instead of reading it; a corrupt closer could NEVER have failed them. They read as strict and were structurally incapable of catching this cycle's bug class. Fixed to read the closer token. Same shape in `Tf60CharLiteralMacroReplacementSurvivesGuardEval` and `test_tsql_subset.cpp:39` — green tests whose stated root-cause mechanism had quietly become false. **THE RULE (add to the cycle discipline): when a cycle DELETES or SUBSUMES a guard, GREP THE TESTS FOR ITS NAME and re-aim every red-on-disable that referenced it — in the SAME cycle. And prefer instructions that name an OBSERVABLE ("revert the tokenizer's closer emit") over a specific line of workaround code, because the observable survives the refactor.** The TF-C71 replacement is strictly better than what it replaced: reverting the closer emit now reds all THREE delimiter forms at once, where the deleted per-byte guard covered one. ★★ **THE ROW ITSELF OVER-CLAIMED WHEN FIRST WRITTEN, AND THAT IS THE POINT.** It was filed saying the class was 'SWEPT'; the pre-commit audit then found a LIVE instance **at a pin this very row names** — `test_preprocessor.cpp:3521` still said 'revert `sbTrackDefine` … and this errors' while the paragraph directly above it said the opposite. Worse, on measurement the instruction was **false in the harmless direction**: a bare `toks[q].text` join leaves the pin GREEN (the closer's own `CharEnd` token reassembles `'\n'` byte-for-byte), so it had been asserting nothing. Re-aimed at a whitespace-separated join, which IS red (stored replacement becomes `' \n '`, a multi-char char constant → guard unevaluable). **BEING ABLE TO NAME A FAILURE CLASS IS NOT HAVING ELIMINATED IT** — 'swept' requires a grep, not an intention. ★★★ **TWO FURTHER SPECIES, BOTH MEASURED 2026-07-28 (TF-C74) — this row is the family's index, so both are named HERE.** The species above are about an instruction that DECAYED; these two are about a test that was **BORN VACUOUS** — it ran, went green, and could never have gone red. That is strictly more dangerous, because there is no moment of decay for an audit to catch. ⭐ **SPECIES (3) — TWO SITES THAT MASK EACH OTHER, so the obvious gate tests neither.** TF-C74's first red-on-disable probe for preprocessor **seed site #1** (`sbNameDefined`, `src/analysis/preprocess/preprocessor.cpp:558` — the include-gating pre-scan's definedness oracle) used an **object-like** macro gate and **STAYED GREEN when the site was reverted**. ROOT CAUSE: **seed site #4's value prefix materializes object-like predefines into `localMacros`, which `sbNameDefined` consults FIRST** — so site #4 silently answers for site #1 and an object-like gate cannot tell them apart. Only a **function-like** macro gate — excluded from the value prefix BY CONSTRUCTION — isolates site #1. **RULE: any future test of that site must use a function-like macro or it proves nothing.** Generalized: when N sites can each satisfy the same observable, a gate must be chosen that only ONE of them can reach — otherwise you have tested the union, not the site. ⭐⭐ **SPECIES (4) — ASSERTING HALF, AND THE HALF THAT CANNOT FAIL. ★ THE MOST REUSABLE FINDING OF THE CYCLE.** A witness for [[D-PROGRAM-TARGET-LOAD-ORDER]] **already existed** (`TFC74UnknownTargetDiagnosedBeforeCuBuild`) and was vacuous on two counts at once: it asserted only half **(a)** — “the good diagnostic appears” — via a bare `EXPECT_GT(countCode(D_SchemaLoadFailed), 0u)` (a **count**, on a **shared** code), and it ran over a source with **no `#include` at all**, so half **(b)** — “no cascade” — was **vacuously true by construction**. It could never have gone red on the very ordering it existed to guard. ★ **THE TWO-REVERT MEASUREMENT THAT PROVES BOTH HALVES ARE NEEDED** — three states, and the MIDDLE one is the whole lesson: **(i) as shipped** → set `{C_InvalidTargetName, D_SchemaLoadFailed}`, (a) PASS, (b) PASS. **(ii) abort gate removed, emission kept** → set gains `P_PreprocessorErrorDirective`, **(a) still PASSES**, (b) FAILS. **(iii) pre-hoist historical state** → set is `{P_PreprocessorErrorDirective}` alone, (a) FAILS, (b) FAILS. **In state (ii) the cascade is BACK and half (a) is still green.** ★★ **THE RULE: asserting that the good diagnostic APPEARS does not prove the bad ones are GONE. Only a SET EQUALITY over diagnostic code names proves both. A count on one code pins that one class; the set pins that nothing else rides along either, so a future re-ordering that swaps the `#error` cascade for some OTHER noise class still goes red.** And the fixture must be able to PRODUCE the cascade — the replacement witness deliberately puts the arch ladder in a quote-`#include`d HEADER, because a source that cannot cascade makes the no-cascade half free. The vacuous test is no longer in the tree (✔TREE: `TFC74UnknownTargetDiagnosedBeforeCuBuild` greps to nothing in `tests/`); it was REPLACED, not supplemented, by `TFC74BadTargetDiagnosedWithoutHeaderErrorCascade` (`tests/program/test_compile_pipeline.cpp:2290-2358`). ⭐⭐⭐ **SPECIES (5) — A GREEN TEST THAT ENCODES A DEFECT AS EXPECTED BEHAVIOUR, AND THEREFORE ACTIVELY RESISTS ITS OWN FIX. MEASURED 2026-07-28 (TF-C74); ★ the most dangerous species in this row, because it inverts the suite's purpose.** Species (1)–(4) are all failures to CATCH — a test that decays, or was born unable to go red. This one is different in kind: the test **works perfectly**, is genuinely red-on-disable, and pins **the wrong answer**. TWO corpus examples pin the [[D-TARGET-CHAR-SIGNEDNESS-PER-PLATFORM]] miscompile as correct — `examples/c/char_signedness/expected.json` (macho row asserts **20**, should be **10**) and `examples/c/char_value/expected.json` (ctest #294, macho row asserts **131**, should be **132** — clang native measures 132) — and `char_value/main.c:26-28` argues the wrong value is right IN PROSE. ★ **THE FAILURE MODE IS THE REPAIR, NOT THE BUG:** the cycle that finally corrects the descriptor will see **two RED examples** and the path of least resistance — especially under a green-suite ratchet — is to “fix the tests”, restoring the defective expectations and re-cementing the bug **with the suite as evidence that it is correct**. ★★ **THE RULES: (i)** when a row records that an expectation is WRONG, name the exact files, rows and both numbers in the anchor, so the repairing cycle meets the warning before it meets the red; **(ii)** annotate the assertion IN PLACE, next to the number, not only in the registry — the person editing it is looking at the file, not at `.plans/`; **(iii)** these examples may be **flipped, never deleted or relaxed** — deleting them destroys the only per-target signedness witness, and relaxing them (dropping the per-target row) silently re-admits BOTH answers; **(iv)** treat “a test went red while I was fixing a config bug” as evidence to CHECK THE TEST, not automatically to fix the code. ★ ROOT CAUSE worth noting: both examples inherited the false claim from ONE source — the `$charIsUnsignedComment` at `src/dss-config/targets/arm64.target.json:14` — so a single wrong sentence in a config comment became three wrong assertions. **A config comment stating an ABI fact is load-bearing documentation; a wrong one is copied forward by everyone who trusts it.** ⭐⭐⭐⭐ **SPECIES (6) — THE BARE `ASSERT_FALSE`: A TEST THAT CANNOT TELL YOU *WHICH GUARD FIRED*. Added 2026-07-28 (TF-C76), WITH A MEASUREMENT, and it is the cheapest species to create by accident.** `ObjectFormatSchemaLoader.UnknownSentinelKindRejectedByValidate` was, at baseline `657c3a9`, a single `ASSERT_FALSE(r.has_value())` over a fixture JSON — it asserted only that **the load failed**, not that it failed **for the stated reason**. Such a fixture is one unrelated typo away (a misspelled `dataModel`, a malformed `relocations`) from staying GREEN forever while the property it names goes unguarded. ★★ **THE MEASURED PROOF THAT THIS IS NOT THEORETICAL — and it is a cleaner demonstration than species (4)'s, because BOTH guards are correct and both are wanted:** TF-C76 added a second, independent rejection of the sentinel at the JSON LOAD SITE. **With that new load-site guard disabled, the bare-`ASSERT_FALSE` test STILL PASSED** — because `validate()` catches the sentinel further down and the load still fails — and **only the message-content assertion went red.** A boolean "did it fail?" cannot distinguish two guards; it is satisfied by any failure at all, including failures that have nothing to do with the test's name. ★ **THE RULE: a fail-loud pin must assert the diagnostic's CODE and PATH and that its MESSAGE names the offending thing — never merely that the operation failed.** Strengthened in place ✔TREE `tests/link/test_object_format_schema.cpp` (`UnknownSentinelKindRejectedByValidate` now scans for `/format/kind` + `C_MalformedJson` + a message naming the sentinel), and a second witness `SentinelKindDoesNotCascadeIntoIdentityBlockNoise` pins the cascade-elimination half. ⚠ **NOTE WHAT IS *NOT* CLAIMED: no diagnostic COUNT is asserted in either** — the property is "the rejection names the kind" and "no per-block advice rides along", both expressible without predicting how many diagnostics the loader emits ([[D-PLANS-ANCHOR-EXPECTATION-BECOMES-SPEC]] rule (iii)). ⭐ **AND A TEST-TALLY WARNING THAT BELONGS WHEREVER TEST-COUNT EXPECTATIONS ARE RECORDED: ctest counts BINARIES, NOT gtest CASES.** TF-C76 landed **14 new gtest cases** and ctest stayed at **728/728** — ✔TREE-verified (`ctest -N` reports `Total Tests: 728`; the cycle's `tests/` diff adds 14 new `TEST(` fixtures). CMake registers roughly one ctest entry per test EXECUTABLE (plus the per-example corpus entries), so adding cases to an existing binary moves the ctest tally by ZERO. **Anyone reading an unchanged ctest tally as evidence that no coverage was added — or reading a changed one as a coverage delta — is misled in both directions.** Use the gtest case count for coverage, the ctest tally for suite health. ⭐⭐⭐⭐⭐ **SPECIES (7) — THE WARNING THAT WAS WRITTEN, PUBLISHED, AND STILL DID NOT PREVENT THE TRAP. Added 2026-07-28 (TF-C77). ★ This species is not about a test at all; it is evidence about WHERE a warning has to LIVE to work.** The trap itself is the documented one: an over-aligned global needs an OFFSET BREAKER in front of it, because the first over-aligned global in a section lands at the section start and every object format page-aligns those — so `&av % 64 == 0` holds whether or not the alignment sink ran, and the check is VACUOUS BY PLACEMENT. ✔TREE it was already written down, in bold, with a measured before/after matrix, in a NEIGHBOURING example: `examples/c/gnu_aligned_attribute/main.c` ("★★ THE OFFSET BREAKERS (`bss_pad`, `data_pad`) ARE LOAD-BEARING", plus a three-row `★ VACUOUS` table and the `.bss`-vs-`.data` section-class rule at `:46-47`). **It did not work.** The TF-C77 implementer, authoring `examples/c/gnu_attribute_mid_position_crosscu/`, reached for a breaker — and reached for the WRONG ONE: an UNINITIALIZED `char pad;` (`.bss`) in front of an INITIALIZED `av` (`.data`). MEASURED: the alignment check still returned 42 under the H1 slot-off disable, i.e. VACUOUS, the exact failure the neighbouring file names in bold. They caught it themselves (the H1 column is what exposed it), switched to an initialized `data_pad = 1` in the SAME section class, and RE-MEASURED 42 → 1 under H1. ★★ **THE FINDING IS THE FAILURE OF THE WARNING, NOT THE FAILURE OF THE AUTHOR** — the warning was correct, prominent, recent, and in a sibling directory of the file being written, and it was still not consulted at the moment of the mistake. **A warning that lives in a neighbouring example is read only by someone who already suspects the problem.** The half that transferred was "there must be a breaker"; the half that did not was "it must be the SAME SECTION CLASS as the object it precedes" — the half that is actually load-bearing. **THE RULES: (i)** a trap of this shape needs the warning where the MISTAKE is made, not where it was first discovered — beside the declaration, in the row that the closing work reads, or (best) enforced; **(ii)** the only reason this one was caught is that the disable matrix was run PER CHECK against the FINAL build and its column was READ — a whole-file revert would have hidden it, and so would running the matrix and only checking that "something went red"; **(iii)** treat "my non-vacuity probe came back green" as a RESULT TO EXPLAIN, not as a pass. ★ This is the second time the same physical trap has been paid for; a third occurrence is the trigger to make it mechanical (a runner check that an over-aligned global in a corpus example is preceded by a same-section-class object) rather than to write the warning a third time. ⭐⭐⭐⭐⭐⭐ **SPECIES (8) — THE BYTE-EQUALITY ROUND-TRIP: A TEST THAT COMPARES TWO COPIES OF THE SAME BLIND SPOT. Added 2026-07-28 (TF-C78), MEASURED.** ✔TREE `tests/mir/test_mir_text.cpp:36`'s `roundTrip` helper asserts `emit → parse → emit` **byte-equality** and is used by eight cases. It could **never** have caught that `mir_text.cpp` was dropping a function's `binding` AND `visibility`: the printer emitted no attribute list, the parser called the 2-argument `addFunction` and re-minted every function as `(Global, Default)` — so a `static` function round-tripped as externally visible and a `weak` one as strong — and **both emits omitted the field, so both matched and the suite stayed green** through every cycle that touched the file. ★★ **THE RULE, and it generalises past MIR text to every serializer, printer, pretty-printer and golden-text pin in the tree: A ROUND-TRIP TEST IS ONLY AS GOOD AS THE EMITTER'S COMPLETENESS. Byte-equality over `emit∘parse∘emit` is structurally incapable of detecting a field that NEITHER SIDE WRITES — it proves the two emits agree, not that either is faithful.** The fix shape is the reusable part: the replacement pins (`MirText.FunctionAttributesSurviveRoundTrip`) read the **PARSED MODULE's accessors** (`parsed->mir.funcBinding/funcVisibility/funcNoInline`) directly rather than comparing text — the only formulation that can observe a dropped field — plus an all-default function pinning the other direction (the attribute list must be OMITTED when nothing is set, so an unconditional emit that would churn every existing golden is caught too). ★ **SCOPE, so this is not inflated:** `parseMir` has **no `src/` callers** (✔TREE MEASURED — definition, declaration and two doc comments only; the callers are two test files), so this was a **TEST-SUBSTRATE** defect, never a live miscompile. That is why it belongs in THIS row and not among the miscompile rows. ★ **WHAT IS STILL OPEN AT THE SAME SITE:** the per-GLOBAL half — `parseGlobal` re-mints `binding`, `visibility`, `isConst`, `isThreadLocal` and `alignment` — see [[D-MIRTEXT-GLOBAL-FLAGS-DROPPED-BY-ROUNDTRIP]]. **The vacuity argument above applies to it unchanged, so do not "verify" it with the `roundTrip` helper.** ⭐⭐⭐⭐⭐⭐⭐ **SPECIES (9) — THE GUARD TESTED ONLY THROUGH A PATH WHERE A *DIFFERENT MECHANISM* ALREADY PRODUCES THE ASSERTED OUTCOME. Added 2026-07-29 (TF-C85), MEASURED. ★ It is the sharpest member of the BORN-VACUOUS branch, because unlike species (3) the two mechanisms are not siblings that mask each other — one lives in the code under test and the other lives in the harness's chosen route, so reading the test tells you nothing is wrong.** ✔TREE `Inlining.NoOptimizeCallerGetsNothingInlinedIntoIt` (`tests/opt/test_inlining.cpp`) pins the CALLER half of the `#pragma optimize` inliner refusal — nothing may be spliced INTO a `noOptimize` function. **AS FIRST WRITTEN IT WAS VACUOUS: its callee was SINGLE-BLOCK, and a single-block splice routes through `MirFunctionRebuilder`, where the [[D-OPT-NOOPTIMIZE-NEUTERS-POLICY]] neuter ALREADY suppresses the transform.** So deleting rule 2c's caller half changed **NOTHING** — the assertion still held, produced by a mechanism the test was not testing. ★★ **THE FIX IS THE INSTRUCTIVE PART: the fixture was changed to a TWO-BLOCK callee, which forces `MultiBlockInliner` — a path with its OWN `addFunction` that never touches the rebuilder at all — and only then does the caller half become load-bearing and the disable go RED.** ⭐⭐⭐ **THE SPECIES, STATED FOR REUSE: *a guard exercised only through a route on which some OTHER mechanism already guarantees the asserted outcome.* The test runs, goes green, is not obviously trivial, and PASSES FOR THE WRONG REASON — its green is a fact about the route, not about the guard.** ★★ **THE DIAGNOSTIC THAT FINDS IT — and it is the same one this whole row exists to enforce: RED-ON-DISABLE MUST BE *EXECUTED*, NEVER REASONED ABOUT.** No amount of reading `inlining.cpp` reveals this; the caller half looks necessary in the source. **It was found ONLY by actually deleting the guard and observing that nothing went red.** ⚠ **AND THE COROLLARY FOR ANY MULTI-PATH TRANSFORM: when a feature has N implementation routes (here: single-block rebuilder vs `MultiBlockInliner`), a pin on route 1 is EVIDENCE ABOUT ROUTE 1 ONLY. Enumerate the routes before believing a guard is covered — the same counting discipline [[D-CSUBSET-ALWAYSINLINE]] arrived at from the other direction ("count how many times the pipeline RE-CONSULTS a flag before concluding a hop needs no pin").** ★ TWO FURTHER SHAPES, TF-C88 (fc0908d), both found in the cycle's OWN FRESH TESTS and only because each arm was disabled ALONE: (i) a CONTAINMENT check on a name a defect would PREFIX is structurally incapable of seeing that defect — asserting merely that `dss_c88_single` is FOUND passes under a double-mangle, because `_dss_c88_single` CONTAINS it; the arm became real only once it ALSO asserted the mangled form ABSENT, i.e. TWO-SIDED. (ii) a check riding a SUB-ASSERTION inside another test has no red arm of its own and cannot fail independently — extract it. | SWEPT + AUDIT-CORRECTED for this cycle — the RULE is the deliverable; expect to apply it again at every root-cause close. ★ A green suite is not evidence a red-on-disable still works. The instruction is documentation, and documentation rots faster than code because nothing compiles it. | [[D-TOK-CLOSING-DELIMITER-HAS-NO-TOKEN]] (mechanism 2); [[D-CPP-ERROR-WARNING]] + [[D-PP-PRESCAN-UNHANDLED-DIRECTIVE-LINE-SKIP]] (mechanism 1); `SentinelKindDoesNotCascadeIntoIdentityBlockNoise`, new) · the two independent guards it must distinguish are `src/link/object_format_schema.cpp:64-68` (`validate()`) and `src/link/object_format_schema_json.cpp:191-214` (the load site) · originating cycle rows [[D-CONFIG-OBJECT-FORMAT-SENTINEL-ACCEPTED-AT-NAME-SITES]] · [[D-PLANS-ANCHOR-EXPECTATION-BECOMES-SPEC]] (instance 4); `tests/analysis/preprocess/test_preprocessor.cpp` · `tests/tokenizer/test_tokenizer.cpp` · `tests/program/test_compile_pipeline.cpp:2290-2358` · `src/analysis/preprocess/preprocessor.cpp:558`; ★ **species (7) added 2026-07-28 by TF-C77** (the published warning that did not transfer) — ✔TREE the warning that failed to prevent it: `examples/c/gnu_aligned_attribute/main.c` (the `bss_pad`/`data_pad` breaker note + the `★ VACUOUS` matrix) · ✔TREE the file that re-paid for it: `examples/c/gnu_attribute_mid_position_crosscu/cu_a.c` (`data_pad`, and its own in-place restatement of the section-class rule) · originating cycle row [[D-CSUBSET-ATTRIBUTE-LEADING-WITH-STORAGE-CLASS]]; ★ **species (9) added 2026-07-29 by TF-C85** (the guard tested only through a route where another mechanism already produces the outcome) — ✔TREE the vacuous-then-repaired pin: `Inlining.NoOptimizeCallerGetsNothingInlinedIntoIt` (`tests/opt/test_inlining.cpp`; the fix is a TWO-BLOCK callee forcing `MultiBlockInliner`) · the masking mechanism: [[D-OPT-NOOPTIMIZE-NEUTERS-POLICY]]'s neuter inside `MirFunctionRebuilder` (`src/opt/passes/mir_rebuild_helper.cpp`) · originating cycle row [[D-PP-PRAGMA-REGISTRY]] · nearest relative in this row: species (3) (two sites that mask each other), from which it differs in that the masking mechanism is chosen by the HARNESS's route, not by the code under test. ═══ ★★★★ **UPDATED 2026-07-29 (TF-C87) — TWO MORE SPECIES, BOTH FOUND BY THE IMPLEMENTER *IN ITS OWN SUITE*, BOTH FIXED IN-CYCLE. ⭐ THE RUNNING TALLY IS NOW THE HEADLINE: THIS IS THE *FOURTH AND FIFTH* INSTANCE IN A SINGLE SESSION, ACROSS TF-C86 AND TF-C87. A CLASS THAT RECURS FIVE TIMES IN TWO CYCLES IS NOT A SEQUENCE OF LAPSES — IT IS THE DEFAULT OUTCOME OF WRITING A DISABLE ARM WITHOUT A PROCEDURE.** ⭐ **SPECIES (n): THE DISABLE REMOVED THE *DIAGNOSTIC* AS WELL AS THE THING UNDER TEST, SO THE ARM PROVED NOTHING.** ★★ The arm redded — but it redded because the diagnostic vanished, not because the BEHAVIOUR regressed, so it would have stayed red for a broken fix and green for none of the right reasons. **THE RULE THIS YIELDS, AND IT IS THE MOST GENERALLY USEFUL ONE IN THIS ROW: A DISABLE MUST REMOVE *EXACTLY ONE* CAPABILITY. If disabling the feature also disables the OBSERVATION of the feature, the arm is measuring its own scaffolding.** ⚠ **This species is especially insidious because it produces the RIGHT COLOUR — a green suite and a red disable arm — while testing nothing, which is why it survives review.** ⭐ **SPECIES (n+1): DUPLICATED MESSAGE SUBSTRINGS ACROSS ARMS** — several arms asserted on the same literal fragment, so ONE prose edit would have silently converted several independent arms into the same arm. **Hoisted to shared constants, which makes the coupling explicit rather than accidental.** ★ **The general form: an assertion keyed on a duplicated literal creates a hidden dependency between tests that appear independent, and the failure mode is SILENT SCOPE COLLAPSE — the suite keeps its arm COUNT while losing its arm COVERAGE.** ✔ **TF-C87 then re-verified TWELVE arms against the FINAL build, each redding EXACTLY its own test — the discipline this row asks for, executed. ★★ AND IT PAID: the same battery caught an UNBOUNDED-RECURSION HANG that no other gate check could have caught ([[D-PP-FATAL-RECURSION-UNBOUNDED-HANG]] — `ctest` would never have finished, and a hang is invisible to every check that presupposes termination).** ⚠ **NOTE THE SELF-SIMILARITY, because it is the argument for making this MECHANICAL rather than remembered: both new species were found in the tests written BY the cycle that was, at that moment, applying this row's discipline. Competence at the discipline does not prevent the defect; only re-verifying every arm against the final build does.** ═══ ★★★★★ **UPDATED 2026-07-29 (TF-C88) — TWO MORE, AGAIN FOUND BY THE IMPLEMENTER IN ITS OWN SUITE, AGAIN FIXED IN-CYCLE. THE BATTERY: 13 ARMS, EACH DISABLED *ALONE* AND RE-VERIFIED AGAINST THE FINAL BUILD; TWO CAME BACK GREEN.** ⚠ **A TALLY DISCREPANCY IS FLAGGED RATHER THAN SILENTLY RESOLVED, BECAUSE THE RUNNING COUNT IS THE HEADLINE AND A WRONG ONE IS WORSE THAN AN ADMITTED GAP.** This row's own counter (immediately above) records TF-C87's pair as *"the FOURTH AND FIFTH INSTANCE IN A SINGLE SESSION, ACROSS TF-C86 AND TF-C87"*, which makes TF-C88's pair the **SIXTH AND SEVENTH**. The TF-C88 commissioning brief called them the *"seventh and eighth in three cycles"*. **The two counters disagree by one and this pass cannot adjudicate it from the record — the operator holds the session-level count. What is NOT in dispute: this is the third consecutive cycle to produce at least two, and the rate is not falling.** ⭐ **SPECIES (10) — THE FIXTURE NEVER ENTERS THE CODE UNDER TEST, AND ITS *SIBLING* RAIL SATISFIES THE ASSERTION.** ✔TREE `examples/c/asm_label/` is a **TWO-CU** program, so its definitions are named by `program.cpp`'s cross-CU merge-key lambda and `compile_pipeline`'s MERGED arm; **the SINGLE-CU `nameOf` rail is never entered at all.** MEASURED: disabling the asm label in the cross-CU lambda ALONE left the example GREEN — **a vacuous guard over the rail an asm label is most likely to be used on in the first place.** ★ Distinguish it from species (9): there, a DIFFERENT MECHANISM produced the asserted outcome on the route taken; here the fixture simply **never reaches the code**, and the green comes from a sibling rail doing the naming instead. **The diagnostic is the same one and it is the only one that works: delete the guard and look at the colour.** ⭐⭐⭐⭐⭐ **AND THE REPLACEMENT YIELDS THE MOST TRANSFERABLE RULE IN THIS UPDATE — ★ A CONTAINMENT ASSERTION OVER A NAME THAT IS A *SUBSTRING* OF ITS OWN WRONG FORM IS SATISFIED BY THE WRONG FORM.** The closing witness is an object-BYTES test (✔TREE `tests/program/test_compile_pipeline.cpp`, `Program_CompileFiles.SingleCuAsmLabelReplacesTheMachOMangling`) and it is deliberately **TWO-SIDED**: the label `dss_c88_single` **MUST** appear **AND** `_dss_c88_single` **MUST NOT**. ★★ **A one-sided "the object contains the label" check PASSES UNDER THE DOUBLE-MANGLE, because `_dss_c88_single` contains `dss_c88_single` as a substring — i.e. the single most plausible wrong implementation is invisible to the obvious assertion.** **THE RULE, and it generalises to EVERY rename, mangle, prefix, decoration or namespacing assertion anywhere in the tree: assert the presence of the wanted name AND THE ABSENCE OF THE FORM IT REPLACED. Substring containment is not equality, and the wrong answer is usually a superstring of the right one.** (The delivered test adds a third arm — `_labelled_fn` must not appear either — pinning that the C identifier does not survive alongside the label.) ⭐⭐ **SPECIES (11) — THE SELECTOR ADMITS MORE RECORDS THAN THE PROPERTY IS ABOUT, SO A DEAD RECORD SATISFIES THE ASSERTION.** A helper scanned for **ANY symbol record named `deffn`**. In the prototype-plus-definition merge there are TWO such records, and ✔MEASURED **the ABSORBED PROTO still carries the label while the SURVIVING DEFINITION — the record `nameOf` actually reads — had none.** So with the merge carry deleted the scan still found `mydeffn` on the corpse and went green, while the emitted symbol was `_deffn`. Rewritten to select on `isAbsorbedProto == false` (✔TREE `tests/analysis/semantic/test_semantic_analyzer_c_subset.cpp:11842`, `AsmLabelOnPrototypeRidesTheRedeclarationMerge`) **plus a mirror test for the other merge branch** (`AsmLabelOnTrailingRedeclarationRidesTheMerge`) — because a merge has two orderings and pinning one is evidence about one. ★★ **THE RULE: when N records can carry the same NAME and exactly one is the survivor, a name-keyed scan tests the UNION. Select on the property that identifies the survivor, not on the label you happen to know.** Nearest relative: species (3) (two sites that mask each other) — same failure, one level down, at the RECORD rather than the SITE. ⭐⭐⭐ **AND AN HONEST NEGATIVE THAT BELONGS IN THIS ROW PRECISELY BECAUSE IT CUTS *AGAINST* THE PROJECT'S USUAL POSTURE: A CASE WHERE THE CORPUS GENUINELY CANNOT BE THE WITNESS, STATED RATHER THAN PAPERED OVER.** TF-C88 added a HIR-SHAPE pin (✔TREE `tests/hir/test_hir_lowering_c_subset.cpp`, `HirLoweringCSubset.TypedefMultiDeclaratorEmitsOneTypeDeclPerAlias`, whose own header reads *"★ THIS TEST EXISTS BECAUSE THE SEMANTIC TESTS CANNOT SEE THE DEFECT IT GUARDS"*). **Why nothing cheaper works: the SEMANTIC tests cannot see a dropped `TypeDecl` because Pass-1 mints the symbols independently of the HIR — so the aliases still bind and the semantic assertion stays green with the HIR node missing; and no EXIT-CODE example can witness it either, because a plain type alias emits no code, so there is no runtime observable to differ.** ★★ **The pin therefore asserts a HIR SHAPE, which this project normally treats as a weaker instrument than a running example — and that is the right call here rather than a compromise. THE GENERAL POINT: "prefer a runtime witness" is a strong default, not a law; when a defect is genuinely unobservable at runtime, a shape pin with the reason WRITTEN NEXT TO IT is strictly better than an exit-code example that would have gone green either way.** ⚠ **The failure mode to avoid is the opposite one: reaching for a shape pin because it is easier, without establishing that the observable one is impossible. The test above states the impossibility argument in the file; that statement is what makes it legitimate, and a future reader is entitled to re-check it.** ★ **2026-08-04 POINTER ADDED — a species this ledger does not yet cover, because it attacks the MEASUREMENT rather than the pin:** [[D-GATE-MTIME-PRESERVING-RESTORE-SKIPS-REBUILD]] (a mtime-preserving restore lets the incremental build SKIP the recompile, so the "restored" observation is taken against the still-mutated binary — a perfectly good pin can be credited with a red it was never shown). ★ **2026-08-12 POINTER ADDED — a species this ledger does NOT yet cover, and it is the MIRROR of the two-sided rule species (10) arrived at:** [[D-TEST-ABSENCE-ARM-GREEN-WHEN-NOTHING-MERGED]]. Species (10) established that a PRESENCE assertion needs a matching ABSENCE assertion (`dss_c88_single` found is satisfied by the superstring `_dss_c88_single`). The new row runs it BACKWARDS — **an arm whose body is ONLY absence checks is satisfied by the subject not existing at all, so it stays GREEN on exactly the regression it guards.** ✔MEASURED: pointing a suite's presence arms at a stripped input turned **6 of 8** red and the two survivors were the two absence-only arms; adding a positive anchor to each made it **8 of 8**. Nearest relative already here is species (4) (*a source with no `#include` made the no-cascade half vacuously true by construction*) — this is that observation NAMED AS A CLASS. ⚠ **No species NUMBER is minted for it, deliberately**, because this row's own counters are recorded above as disagreeing by one and the operator holds the session-level count; numbering it here would compound the discrepancy rather than settle it. |
| `D-TEST-MACOS-HOST-SPAWNS-FOREIGN-BINARY` | P3 | 🟠 OPEN | ⚠️⚠️ **THIS CLOSE WAS OVER-CLAIMED — the defect survived in a duplicate and reddened CI 7 days later. See [[D-TEST-HOST-SPAWNS-FOREIGN-BINARY]], which supersedes this row.** The 2026-07-27 cycle repaired the `__APPLE__` ladder in `tests/program/test_ffi_resolve_library.cpp` and marked the anchor closed, but the SAME FILE's Linux `#else` branch carried the identical two-arm shape with no `__aarch64__` arm. On 2026-08-03 the native `ubuntu-24.04-arm` CI leg therefore built an `elf64-x86_64` binary and spawned it: `posix_spawn(...) failed: rc=8` (ENOEXEC), 771/772, two failing assertions. ⓘ **THE LESSON, and it is the one the bar already states:** this was a MULTI-SITE contract ("a test that spawns its output must build for the host") closed after fixing a SUBSET of its sites — the exact class the strict-test rule says to close by funnelling every site through ONE chokepoint, because duplication is what breeds the missed site. A green suite over a subset of sites is not proof; here the unexercised site was simply a host nobody had run yet. ═══ ORIGINAL ROW: ✅ **FIXED 2026-07-27 (same cycle it was found — it blocked the gate, so it was repaired rather than deferred). Was: priority high, blocking a clean `ctest` gate on ANY macOS host. Found 2026-07-27 during the first macOS-arm64 corpus cycle; PRE-EXISTING at `d98d6dc`, NOT introduced by that cycle — PROVEN by stashing the cycle's config changes and re-running: the test still fails.** `program/test_ffi_resolve_library` builds an **`x86_64:elf64-x86_64-linux-exec`** artifact and then SPAWNS it unconditionally; on macOS that is `posix_spawn(...) failed: rc=8` (ENOEXEC — a Linux ELF cannot exec on Darwin). Two sub-tests fail deterministically in isolation in 0.06 s: `FfiResolveLibraryRoundTrip.{SiblingTuDefinitionResolvesUnderResolveLibrary, SiblingTuDataDefinitionResolvesUnderResolveLibrary}`. The sibling sub-tests that only COMPILE (`GenuinelyUnknownExternFailsLoud`, `MissingResolveLibraryPathFailsLoudEvenWithNoExterns`) pass — so the defect is precisely the *run* step, which carries no host gate. ★ The examples corpus already solved this exact problem with a `runOn` host filter (`examples_runner.cpp:799` prints "produced an artifact but runOn=[linux…] excludes host=darwin" and SKIPS); this unit test simply never adopted it. FIX: gate the spawn on host==linux (or emit for the HOST's own format instead of a hardcoded elf64-x86_64 spec — better, since it keeps the round-trip assertion live on every host rather than skipping it on two of three). Do NOT delete the assertion. **FIX APPLIED — RETARGET, not skip:** the two-arm `#if defined(_WIN32) … #else`(=Linux) shape gained an `#elif defined(__APPLE__)` arm selecting `macho64-{arm64,x86_64}-darwin-{dylib,exec}` (arch chosen by `__aarch64__`, since both Apple arches ship dylib+exec formats). A `GTEST_SKIP` was deliberately REJECTED: it would have silently stopped testing `--resolve-library` on macOS — precisely the coverage the first macOS run needed — whereas retargeting keeps the round-trip assertion LIVE on every host. VERIFIED: `program/test_ffi_resolve_library` now Passes on macOS (1.00 s), actually building a Mach-O dylib + exec and RUNNING them; the Windows and Linux arms are untouched text, so those legs are byte-unchanged. | ✅ CLOSED — fixed in the finding cycle. ═══ ★ THE CLASS, worth a sweep: this is "a unit test that RUNS a cross-target artifact", and it is invisible on the Linux/Windows legs because there the hardcoded spec happens to match the host. Every such test is latently broken on any host whose format differs. Sweep `tests/` for spawn/exec of a fixed-format artifact. ★ SECOND FINDING, same run, recorded here so it is not lost: **the examples runner's 5000 ms spawn timeout is load-fragile.** A full `ctest` executed while this cycle's background builds were running produced **22 spurious `child timed out after 5000 ms` failures** (a contiguous alphabetical block; `float_negate` took 5.19 s vs its usual ~0.7 s) with **ZERO semantic mismatches** — every one passed on an idle re-run. Cold-start execution of a freshly ad-hoc-signed Mach-O appears to be the sensitive part. Consider scaling the timeout with host load / making it configurable, so a busy CI box cannot manufacture red. | [[D-CSUBSET-DARWIN-PLATFORM-MACROS]] · [[D-CSUBSET-APPLE-SDK-HEADER-SURFACE]] (the sibling first-macOS-run findings); `tests/program/test_ffi_resolve_library.cpp` · `tests/examples/examples_runner.cpp:799` (the `runOn` precedent to copy) |
| `D-GATE-REFERENCE-AUDIT-MISSES-COMPUTED-PATHS` | P3 | 🟠 OPEN | 🟡 **BUG FIXED 2026-07-29 (TF-C87); LIMB (A) ✅ DISCHARGED 2026-07-29 (TF-C87 CLOSE-OUT, by the OPERATOR at gate time, at commit `2637623`); CLASS OPEN ON LIMBS (B)+(C) — ★★★★ an AUDIT-METHOD class, and it is filed as a SELF-INDICTMENT BY THE OPERATOR, which is the category of finding least likely to be made by anyone else.** ✅✅ **LIMB (A) — THE SILENT HOP-COUNTED FALLBACK — IS GONE FROM BOTH `.py` FILES.** `_repo_root()` no longer ends in `return here.parent.parent.parent`; it prints a FATAL naming WHAT it looked for (a directory holding both `CMakeLists.txt` and `src/`) and WHERE the walk started, then exits `EXIT_CANNOT_RUN`. ★ **The exit constants were MOVED ABOVE `_repo_root()` so the named constant is usable at the failure site rather than a duplicated bare literal `2` — a small thing that is recorded because a magic number at a fail-loud exit is how the next reader stops trusting that the code is exhaustive.** ✔✔ **VERIFIED BY EXECUTION, NOT BY INSPECTION, AND THE DISTINCTION IS LOAD-BEARING HERE: a copy of the script placed OUTSIDE the repo was RUN, exits `2`, and prints the diagnostic; both `.py --help`, both `.sh` and the `.ps1` still return `0`. The FAILING path was exercised, not merely argued for — which is the only way to know a fail-loud branch is reachable at all.** ✔ **RE-VERIFIED INDEPENDENTLY BY THE CLOSE-OUT ANCHOR PASS against the COMMITTED tree (`git show HEAD`), not accepted on report: marker walk over `Path(__file__).parents` in both files, NO fallback of any kind in either, `EXIT_… = 0, 1, 2` declared above the function in both.** ⭐⭐ **★★★★★ WHY THIS ONE LINE WAS WORTH A DISCHARGE ENTRY — THE REASON IS SPECIFIC TO THIS CODEBASE AND A GENERIC "fail loud" NOTE WOULD ERASE IT: A WRONG `REPO_ROOT` DOES NOT FAIL. IT CENSUSES THE **WRONG TREE** AND REPORTS THE RESULT AS FACT.** ★★★ **That is, without analogy, the EXACT failure mode this census instrument was built to eliminate — reintroduced INSIDE the instrument, one level below where anyone was looking. The output of a mis-rooted run is byte-shaped exactly like a real answer, so there is no downstream check that could catch it: the number would simply be wrong, cited, and load-bearing.** ★ **Generalisation worth keeping: WHEN A TOOL EXISTS TO PREVENT A CLASS OF ERROR, THE HIGHEST-COST PLACE FOR THAT ERROR TO SURVIVE IS INSIDE THE TOOL — because everything downstream has been designed to trust it.** ⚠ **DISCHARGING (A) CLOSES THE *BUG*, NOT THE *CLASS*. Limbs (B) (a move audit must search for COMPUTED location dependencies, inward not outward) and (C) (an IMPORTED repo-root helper instead of a copied one) are UNSTARTED, and (C) is the one that would remove the duplication this discharge had to fix twice.** ⭐ **WHAT HAPPENED.** Before moving the census scripts into `scripts/corpus-census/` and `scripts/pragma-profile-census/`, the operator AUDITED EVERY REFERENCE and told the implementer **the only code dependency was one hard-coded `EXPECTED_FILE` path.** ⛔ **THAT WAS WRONG.** ✔MEASURED: `REPO_ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent` appeared in **BOTH** `.py` files and, after the move, **SILENTLY RESOLVED TO `scripts/`** instead of the repo root. ⭐⭐ **★★★★ WHY NO AUDIT OF THAT SHAPE COULD EVER HAVE FOUND IT — AND THIS IS THE WHOLE ROW: A REFERENCE AUDIT GREPS FOR *THE MOVED NAME*. THIS PATH CONTAINED NO NAME. IT WAS BUILT BY *ARITHMETIC ON THE FILE'S OWN LOCATION* — COUNTING `..` HOPS — SO IT IS INVISIBLE TO EVERY SEARCH KEYED ON THE THING BEING MOVED. The dependency was on the file's DEPTH IN THE TREE, and depth is not a string anyone can grep for.** ★★ **A grep-based reference audit is complete only for references that NAME their target. Anything computed — hop counts, `dirname` arithmetic, index-based lookups, relative offsets — is a dependency the audit cannot see, and its absence from the results reads exactly like absence of the dependency.** ⭐ **THE SHARPER SELF-INDICTMENT, RECORDED BECAUSE IT IS THE MOST INSTRUCTIVE PART: THE OPERATOR FLAGGED THE `EXPECTED_FILE` CASE AS "RIGHT BY CONSTRUCTION VS RIGHT BY ACCIDENT" — THE EXACT DISTINCTION THAT WOULD HAVE CAUGHT THIS — AND THEN MISSED THE LARGER INSTANCE OF PRECISELY THAT.** ★★ **Having the right ANALYTICAL FRAME is not the same as APPLYING IT EXHAUSTIVELY. The frame was correct; the sweep stopped at the case that had a name.** ⭐ **THE FIX (✔TREE-VERIFIED in both `.py` files): the walk is now ANCHORED ON A REPO MARKER — ascend `Path(__file__).parents` until a directory has BOTH a `CMakeLists.txt` file AND a `src/` directory — so the answer no longer depends on how deep the script sits. `EXPECTED_FILE` was likewise made SIBLING-relative (`Path(__file__).resolve().parent / …`), which is right by construction and moves with the file.** **── SUPERSEDED BY THE DISCHARGE ABOVE (kept verbatim, because the residual’s DISCOVERY is the audit-trail evidence that a fix can re-introduce the shape it removes):** ⚠⚠ **★★ RESIDUAL THIS PASS MEASURED AND THE CYCLE DID NOT FLAG — SMALL, REAL, AND EXACTLY THE SHAPE THE FIX EXISTS TO REMOVE: `_repo_root()` STILL ENDS WITH `return here.parent.parent.parent` AS A FALLBACK. That is hop-counting again — now THREE hops — and it fires SILENTLY when the marker walk finds nothing. So a script copied or vendored outside the repo gets a WRONG `REPO_ROOT` with no complaint, in a codebase whose creed is fail-loud. ★ It is also DUPLICATED VERBATIM in both `.py` files.** | ★★★ **CLOSING WORK — THE ONE-LINE RESIDUAL, THEN THE METHOD.** ⭐ **(A) ✅ DONE 2026-07-29 (TF-C87 close-out, operator, `2637623`; ✔verified by EXECUTION of both the normal and the failing path, then ✔re-verified against the committed tree by the anchor pass) — MAKE THE FALLBACK FAIL LOUD.** Replace `return here.parent.parent.parent` with an explicit error naming what it looked for and where it started. ★ **A path resolver that cannot find the repo should SAY SO — every downstream path is wrong from that point, and a silently-wrong root produces confusing failures far from their cause. This is the fail-loud creed applied to the tooling that MEASURES the compiler, which is exactly where a silent wrong answer is most expensive** ([[D-GATE-CORPUS-MEASURE-INSTRUMENT-INVALID]]: an instrument nobody can trust is worse than none). ⚠ **And it is a two-copy fix — the helper is duplicated in both `.py` files, so this is a small [[D-CONFIG-COMMENT-CLAIM-ROT]]-adjacent divergence risk of its own.** ⭐⭐ **(B) THE METHOD LIMB, WHICH IS WHAT ACTUALLY CLOSES THE CLASS: A MOVE AUDIT MUST SEARCH FOR *COMPUTED* LOCATION DEPENDENCIES, NOT ONLY NAMED ONES.** ★★ **Concretely, before moving any file, grep for the SHAPES that encode position rather than identity — `__file__` arithmetic, `parent.parent`, `dirname $0` chains, `../..` literals, `PSScriptRoot` walks, `CMAKE_CURRENT_SOURCE_DIR` relatives — IN THE FILES BEING MOVED, not in the files that reference them.** ⭐ **THAT INVERSION IS THE INSIGHT: the audit was pointed OUTWARD ("who references this file?") when the broken dependency pointed INWARD ("what does this file assume about where it is?"). Both directions are needed and only one was checked.** ⚠ **A move that changes DEPTH is materially riskier than one that changes only NAME, and nothing in the process currently distinguishes them — this move changed depth for six files at once.** ⭐ **(C) OPTIONAL BUT CHEAP: make position-dependence detectable.** A repo-root helper that is IMPORTED rather than copied would make this a single auditable site instead of a shape to remember to grep for. ★ **Prefer removing the class of dependency over remembering to look for it — the same argument [[D-PP-INCLUDEONCE-DETECTOR-DESIGN]] makes about shape lists.** | created 2026-07-29 (TF-C87 anchor pass, at close, working tree from HEAD `5093341`). **RAISED BY:** the operator, against their own pre-move audit. **TRIGGERED BY:** the `scripts/corpus-census/` + `scripts/pragma-profile-census/` directory move (6 files, depth +1). **CLASS SIBLINGS:** [[D-GATE-CORPUS-MEASURE-INSTRUMENT-INVALID]] (a measuring instrument that silently answers wrong) · [[D-GATE-INSTRUMENT-SCOPE-UNSTATED]] (an audit read as broader than its actual reach — this is the same failure applied to a GREP rather than to a GUARD) · [[D-PLANS-LINE-CITATION-ROT]] (the sibling insight that positional references rot, there for LINE numbers, here for DIRECTORY DEPTH) · [[D-PP-INCLUDEONCE-DETECTOR-DESIGN]] (prefer a formulation that removes the class over an enumeration you must remember to complete) · [[D-CONFIG-COMMENT-CLAIM-ROT]] (the duplicated helper). **CODE SURFACES (✔MEASURED by this pass in the working tree; per [[D-PLANS-LINE-CITATION-ROT]] re-grep the NAME, never the line):** `scripts/corpus-census/corpus-census.py` and `scripts/pragma-profile-census/pragma-profile-census.py` — `_repo_root()` (the marker walk AND its silent hop-counted fallback) and `REPO_ROOT` · `scripts/pragma-profile-census/pragma-profile-census.py` — `EXPECTED_FILE`, now sibling-relative. ✔ **The pre-move `parent.parent` breakage is ⟲RELAYED from the operator; the SHIPPED fix, the surviving fallback and the duplication were all MEASURED by this pass.** |
| `D-TEST-IGNORE-LIST-IS-A-LICENSE-TO-DROP` | P3 | 🟠 OPEN | 🔴 **OPEN — ★ a TEST-DESIGN CLASS, created 2026-07-27 (TF-C72). Adding a name to `linkageSpecifierIgnoredNames` is a DECLARATION that ignoring that attribute is SAFE — and NOTHING in the suite checks that declaration.** MEASURED: the existing GNU-attribute test (`tests/hir/test_hir_lowering_c_subset.cpp:2282-2302`, `GnuSemanticAttributeSpellingsFileScopeLowerClean` — re-measured 2026-07-27, it had drifted from `:2258-2277`) asserts only `H_UnknownLinkageSpecifier == 0`, which stays **GREEN if every listed name becomes a silent drop** — the assertion literally cannot distinguish "honored elsewhere" from "thrown away", because adding the name to the list is what makes the assertion pass. **So the ignore list is UNGUARDED BY CONSTRUCTION: the list is the claim, and the test is the claim's own tautology.** ★★ **REQUIRED DISCIPLINE: every name added to an ignore list needs a PAIRED POSITIVE ASSERTION that the property is honored SOMEWHERE ELSE.** An ignore list may only ever say "this is not a linkage specifier"; it may never be the ONLY place a name is mentioned. ★ **MEASURED EXEMPLARS, both directions.** **`noreturn` IS properly honored:** `specifierPrefixNamesNoreturn` (`src/analysis/semantic/semantic_analyzer.cpp:2855-2880`) matches BOTH the `_Noreturn` KEYWORD and the attribute IDENTIFIER via `cfg.noreturnAttributeNames`, and removing the attribute flips `error[H0003] may fall through without returning a value` — a real sink with a demonstrable red-on-disable. **`aligned` is NOT honored from `attrSpec`:** alignment is sourced ONLY via `cfg.alignasSpecRule` (`:2823` validity gate, `:2832` rule match, in `firstAlignasSpecInPrefix`), so adding `aligned` to the ignore list WITHOUT first building it a real sink would make `__attribute__((aligned)) int gv;` compile perfectly clean with the alignment **GONE** — a silently under-aligned object, a fault on some targets and a slow correctness bug on others. ★ **TWO NAMES WERE REMOVED FROM A PROPOSED IGNORE LIST BY THIS RULE — recorded verbatim so they are not re-proposed: `returns_twice`** — DSS **genuinely MODELS** it (a `ReturnsTwiceAttr` in HIR applied to `setjmp` calls), so ignoring it would discard real semantics DSS already implements; **`availability`** — **DEAD CONFIG**, it cannot parse AT ALL until `attrArgItem` accepts `=`, so listing it would have been an ignore entry for a construct that never reaches the list. ★★ **CORRECTED 2026-07-27 — THE `availability` HALF ABOVE WAS TRUE WHEN THE DESIGN AUDIT MEASURED IT AND IS FALSE NOW, INVALIDATED BY ITS OWN CYCLE. Retained rather than rewritten, because the reason it went stale is the lesson.** MEASURED in the current tree: TF-C72 landed the OPTIONAL `= atom` TAIL on `attrArgItem` (`src/dss-config/sources/c.lang.json:638-639`, whose `$comment` names the clang keyword-argument form it exists for), which makes `__attribute__((availability(macos,introduced=10.12)))` PARSE — and TF-C73 then ADDED `availability` to the list, where it sits today (`:1570`), with the list's own `$hintIgnoreNamesComment` (`:1569`) recording exactly that: _"parseable since attrArgItem gained `=`"_. So of the two removals, only ONE stuck. ★★ **THE PAIR DELIBERATELY KEPT ABSENT IS `aligned` + `returns_twice` — NOT `returns_twice` + `availability`.** That is what `:1569` enumerates under _"TWO NAMES ARE DELIBERATELY ABSENT AND MUST STAY ABSENT"_, and `aligned` is the load-bearing one for the reason this row already gives: no `attrSpec` sink, so ignoring it would silently under-align. ★★ **THE LESSON, which outlives the correction: A DEAD-CONFIG JUSTIFICATION IS ONLY VALID AGAINST A FIXED GRAMMAR.** "It cannot parse anyway" is not a verdict, it is a DATED observation with an EXPIRY: any cycle that widens the argument grammar silently REVIVES every name that was excluded for being unparseable — and here the reviving change shipped in the SAME cycle as the exclusion, which is the shortest possible expiry. Anyone excluding a name on unparseability must re-check it the moment the grammar moves, and must write the exclusion as "unparseable AS OF <grammar state>", never as a property of the name. | Trigger: fires on the NEXT ignore-list addition — treat this row as a STANDING GATE on that edit, not as scheduled work to be picked up later. Closing work, in order: **(1)** audit the CURRENT `linkageSpecifierIgnoredNames` (`src/dss-config/sources/c.lang.json:1570` — **18 names as of 2026-07-27**, not the original 6: `noreturn`, `deprecated`, `maybe_unused`, `unused`, `nodiscard`, `warn_unused_result`, `format`, `nonnull`, `nothrow`, `leaf`, `malloc`, `pure`, `cold`, `hot`, `access`, `alloc_size`, `sentinel`, `availability` — the list TRIPLED in the cycle that created this row, which is itself the argument for the audit) name by name and record, PER NAME, where it is honored or that dropping it is genuinely semantics-free; **(2)** add the missing paired positive assertions; **(3)** for any name with no sink, either build the sink or move it to a fail-loud list — do not leave a third state. ★ **The paired assertion must check the PROPERTY (the alignment, the no-return, the deprecation MESSAGE), NEVER a diagnostic COUNT** — a count-only pin is exactly what let [[D-CSUBSET-GNU-DEPRECATED-MESSAGE-SILENTLY-DROPPED]] live an entire cycle. | opened 2026-07-27 by TF-C72, `availability` half CORRECTED 2026-07-27 (TF-C73); `src/dss-config/sources/c.lang.json:1570` (`linkageSpecifierIgnoredNames`) + `:1569` (`$hintIgnoreNamesComment`, the per-name audit) + `:638-639` (`attrArgItem`'s `= atom` tail, the grammar widening that expired the `availability` exclusion) · `src/analysis/semantic/semantic_analyzer.cpp:2855-2880` (`specifierPrefixNamesNoreturn` — the GOOD precedent) + `:2823`/`:2832` (`firstAlignasSpecInPrefix` — why `aligned` has no `attrSpec` sink) · `src/hir/attributes/returns_twice_attr.hpp` (`ReturnsTwiceAttr`, the reason `returns_twice` was removed) · `tests/hir/test_hir_lowering_c_subset.cpp:2282-2302` (the tautological pin; re-measured 2026-07-27); same class as [[D-TEST-REDONDISABLE-DECAY]] + [[D-CSUBSET-GNU-DEPRECATED-MESSAGE-SILENTLY-DROPPED]]; constrains [[D-CSUBSET-ATTRIBUTE-MEMBER-POSITION]], [[D-CSUBSET-ATTRIBUTE-ARG-CONSTANT-EXPRESSION]] + [[D-CSUBSET-GNU-UNKNOWN-NAME-GATE-ASYMMETRY]]. |
| `D-TEST-CORPUS-C-NOT-VALIDATED-AGAINST-A-REAL-COMPILER` | P3 | 🟠 OPEN | 🔴 **OPEN — ★★ THE MOST IMPORTANT OF THE THREE ROWS THIS TF-C72/C73 §A.7 PASS OPENS: the runnable corpus under `examples/c/` is DSS's end-to-end witness that it handles REAL C, and NOTHING validates the corpus's C against a real toolchain — so INVALID C can SHIP and PERSIST.** MEASURED: `examples/c/gnu_attribute_after_declarator/main.c` carried `int firstv(int n, ...) __attribute__((format(printf,1,2)))` — a **HARD ERROR** under clang (`format_attribute_not`: the designated argument is not a string type) — and it had shipped in the corpus **FOR A FULL CYCLE** before anyone ran a real compiler over it. It was found only because an IDE's clang integration surfaced it **INCIDENTALLY**: no test, no gate and no corpus runner would ever have said a word. ★ **WHY THIS MATTERS MORE THAN ONE BAD LINE:** a corpus example exists to prove DSS accepts what real toolchains accept. If the C is INVALID, the example proves the **OPPOSITE** of its purpose — that DSS accepts something clang REJECTS — and it silently degrades into a bad red-on-disable witness, because the attribute it "exercises" is one clang says DOES NOT APPLY. ★ **ALSO MEASURED, same discovery — the RATE is the real finding: of 7 attribute snippets written for new tests this cycle, 4 were INVALID C.** `__nonnull__((1))` on `int q(void)` is a hard error (parameter index out of bounds); `((cold, hot))` is a hard error (incompatible attributes); `malloc` and `sentinel` warn. So this is NOT a rare slip — hand-written attribute C is wrong at a HIGH rate, and ONLY a real compiler catches it. ★ **SCOPE HONESTY:** the one bad LINE was corrected inside TF-C73 (that `main.c` is rewritten in the same tree as this row); what stays **OPEN here is the MISSING VALIDATION** — the part that would have caught it without an IDE's help, and the part that will catch the next one. | Trigger: **ALREADY FIRED** — an invalid corpus example shipped for a full cycle and was found by accident rather than by the suite. Closing work: add a VALIDATION STEP so every `examples/**/main.c` (and ideally the test snippets too) is checked with `clang -fsyntax-only -Wall -Wextra -isysroot $(xcrun --show-sdk-path)` — as a test, a CI step, or a corpus-runner PRECONDITION. ★ **THE PORTABILITY CONSTRAINT IS PART OF THE WORK, NOT AN AFTERTHOUGHT:** that flag set and `-isysroot`/`xcrun` are macOS-SPECIFIC, so the check must **DEGRADE GRACEFULLY** on the Linux/Windows legs (skip-with-reason when no host compiler is present) rather than FAIL them — a gate that reds a leg for lack of clang gets deleted, and the gap comes straight back. ★ Prefer the MECHANICAL form over a one-time sweep: the anchor guard (`scripts/check-anchor-registry/check-anchor-registry.{sh,ps1}`) is the in-repo precedent for exactly this shape — a discipline nobody can forget because the system enforces it. | opened 2026-07-27 by the TF-C72/C73 §A.7 pass; `examples/c/gnu_attribute_after_declarator/main.c` (the shipped-invalid witness) · `examples/**/main.c` (the whole unvalidated surface) · `scripts/check-anchor-registry/check-anchor-registry.{sh,ps1}` (the mechanical-guard precedent); the attribute cycle whose example this was is [[D-CSUBSET-GNU-ATTRIBUTE]]; ★ SAME CLASS ONE TIER UP — a real toolchain PRESENT but not used as an ORACLE — [[D-SQLITE-GCC-REFERENCE-FIXTURE-AS-ORACLE]]; test-design siblings [[D-TEST-REDONDISABLE-DECAY]], [[D-TEST-IGNORE-LIST-IS-A-LICENSE-TO-DROP]]; the host-compiler-availability limit it shares with [[D-CSUBSET-ATTRIBUTE-ACCESS-NO-CLANG-CLEAN-WITNESS]]; host-portability precedents [[D-TEST-MACOS-HOST-SPAWNS-FOREIGN-BINARY]], [[D-HARNESS-CROSS-HOST-ANY-TARGET]]. |
| `D-TEST-SEMANTIC-HARNESS-HAS-NO-TARGET` | P3 | 🟠 OPEN | 🔴 **OPEN — ★ a TEST-DESIGN CLASS, created 2026-07-28 (TF-C73). The semantic-only unit harness constructs NO TARGET, so an entire diagnostic class is INVISIBLE to it — and a pin was found GREEN on source the shipped CLI REJECTS.** The instance: a semantic pin exercised `typedef __attribute__((aligned(16))) long T;` and passed, while the real CLI on the same text fails loud (`error[S002F] … on a typedef cannot be honored: the alias resolves to the same type as its aliasee`). Nothing was wrong with the assertion; the harness simply never runs the tier that produces that verdict. ★★ **THE CLASS MATTERS MORE THAN THE INSTANCE, which is the whole reason this is a row and not a one-line test fix: A SUITE CAN BE GREEN ON PROGRAMS THE PRODUCT REFUSES TO COMPILE.** Every semantic-only pin is a claim about a *phase*, but it is easy to read — and to write — as a claim about the *language*. The align-validation class is the one measured here; any check that needs target/layout context has the same blind spot, so the count of affected pins is unknown and should be treated as unknown rather than assumed to be one. ★ This is the INTERNAL twin of [[D-TEST-CORPUS-C-NOT-VALIDATED-AGAINST-A-REAL-COMPILER]]: that row is about the corpus not being checked against a real compiler; this one is about a unit suite not being checked against **DSS's own shipped front end**. A pin can be green against neither authority and look fine. Related decay class: [[D-TEST-REDONDISABLE-DECAY]]. ★ NOT a product defect — the compiler is CORRECT here and the CLI's rejection is the right behavior. The defect is in what the suite can SEE, which is why no `src/` change closes it. | Trigger: **ALREADY FIRED** (found this cycle). Closing work, in order: **(1)** identify which semantic pins assert on constructs whose verdict depends on a target — the align/layout family first, since that is the measured one; **(2)** for each, either give the harness a target or add a CLI-level sibling that compiles the SAME source, so the pin's claim and the product's behavior are checked against each other; **(3)** state in the harness's own header what it does NOT cover, so the next author does not have to rediscover it by finding a lying green. ★ The trap to avoid while closing: do NOT simply delete the offending pin — it was testing something real at its own tier. The gap is coverage, not a bad assertion, and removing the pin would close the row while making the suite weaker. | created 2026-07-28 by the TF-C72/C73 §A.7 pass (MEASURED — the pin passed, the shipped CLI rejected the same source); `tests/analysis/semantic/semantic_test_fixture.hpp` (the target-less harness) · `tests/analysis/semantic/test_semantic_analyzer_c_subset.cpp` (where the align pins live); siblings [[D-TEST-CORPUS-C-NOT-VALIDATED-AGAINST-A-REAL-COMPILER]], [[D-TEST-REDONDISABLE-DECAY]], [[D-TEST-IGNORE-LIST-IS-A-LICENSE-TO-DROP]] |
| `D-PLANS-LINE-CITATION-ROT` | P4 | 🟠 OPEN | 🔴 **OPEN — ★ a DOCUMENTATION-DISCIPLINE CLASS, created 2026-07-28 (TF-C73). A `FILE:LINE` citation written into a registry row ROTS BEFORE THE CYCLE THAT WROTE IT ENDS — measured, not predicted.** MEASURED 2026-07-28 by resolving every `FILE:LINE` in the rows this cycle added: **8 rows carried citations that no longer resolved to the construct they named**, and they had been correct when written — the files simply kept growing underneath them, edited by the same cycle. The sharpest case: **ONE config key, `linkageSpecifierIgnoredNames`, was cited from three different rows at three different WRONG line numbers** (`:1548`, `:1549`, `:1550`) while actually living at `:1570` (with its `$hintIgnoreNamesComment` at `:1569`). Others drifted much further — `kDocumentKeys` `:1982`→`:2058` (76 lines), `kSemanticsKeys` `:4533`→`:4661` (128), `kDeclarationRowKeys` `:5141`→`:5273` (132), `kAttributeSemanticsKeys` `:8935`→`:9215` (280), and the S0018 emit `:5877`→`:6250` (373). ★★ **THE POINT IS NOT THAT SOMEONE WAS SLOPPY — EVERY ONE OF THESE WAS ACCURATE AT THE MOMENT IT WAS TYPED.** The line number is a coordinate in a file that is still being edited; the cycle that writes the citation is usually the same cycle that invalidates it. A convention that is wrong by the time the commit lands is not a convention worth keeping in that form. ★ Rot is also **SILENT and ASYMMETRICALLY EXPENSIVE**: nothing checks these, an out-of-range number is obvious but an IN-range wrong one is not — `c.lang.json:600` resolved to a BLANK LINE and `:1688` to an unrelated `ShlAssignOp` operator row, either of which a reader could mistake for the cited construct being deleted or moved on purpose. ✅ **FIXED THIS PASS (2026-07-28): 28 citations across 8 rows re-resolved and corrected against the current tree**, verified by a resolver that re-reads each cited file. Every corrected number was checked by reading the target, not by arithmetic on a diff. ★★ **RECURRED IMMEDIATELY — a FRESH instance measured 2026-07-28 during the TF-C75 fold, and it is the most instructive shape yet because the citation rotted BEFORE THE CYCLE THAT PRODUCED IT HAD EVEN FINISHED.** The cycle's own hand-off described the production wiring of `effectiveCharIsUnsigned` as `src/program/compile_pipeline.cpp:585`. ✔TREE at the time of reading it was **`:613`** — a 28-line drift, inside the same uncommitted working tree, in the same session. Line 585 lands in an unrelated `strictAliasingOnDistinctTypes` / bit-field-strategy comment block that a reader could plausibly accept as "close enough" and then reason from. ⭐ **THE LESSON THIS INSTANCE ADDS: `FILE:LINE` rots fastest for the code a cycle is ACTIVELY EDITING — precisely the code its own hand-off is most likely to cite.** The rot is not a slow decay over cycles; it is same-session. The stable identifier was available and would not have rotted: `mirCfg.charIsUnsigned = effectiveCharIsUnsigned(target, format);` — a symbol plus an assignment, greppable forever. Cite the SYMBOL and let the reader's grep supply the line. | Trigger: **ALREADY FIRED, and fires every cycle** — treat this as a STANDING RULE on writing a row, not as scheduled work. The rule: cite a **STABLE IDENTIFIER** (symbol, rule name, config key, `TEST(...)` name) as the primary reference; a line number, if given at all, is a *convenience* and must be marked as of a date. A row that names `kSemanticsKeys` stays correct forever; a row that names `:4533` is correct for hours.
