the **backslash-escaped** form inside backticks **WORKS** (renders as one intact code span with literal separators, trailing cells preserved) — this row had that right; but **THE HTML-ENTITY FORM DOES *NOT* WORK INSIDE A CODE SPAN** — `&#124;` is NOT decoded there and renders as the literal six characters. ⚠ **So this row’s parenthetical “only the ESCAPED form (or an HTML entity) survives” must be read as: THE BACKSLASH ESCAPE IS THE ONLY FORM THAT SURVIVES INSIDE BACKTICKS. A check built on the entity advice would green-light rows the renderer mangles.** ⭐⭐⭐ **(C) THE ROOT CAUSE THIS ROW ASSERTS FOR THE OVER-CELLED ROWS — “written by quoting accurate technical content containing a logical-OR” — IS TRUE FOR ONLY A THIRD OF THEM. ✔MEASURED over all 67 over-celled TABLE-A rows: 23 (34%) are PURE content-separator rows; **36 (54%) CONTAIN NO CONTENT SEPARATOR AT ALL**; 8 (12%) are both.** ★★ **WHAT THE 36 ACTUALLY ARE, AND IT IS A DIFFERENT DEFECT ENTIRELY: THEY ARE AUTHORED TO A *FIVE-COLUMN* SCHEMA — `Anchor / Finding / Trigger / Closing work / Cross-refs` — INSIDE A FOUR-COLUMN TABLE.** ✔MEASURED by splitting cells: an unbroken block at lines 583–609 (the SQLite/harness rows) plus 495–524 and 246–290 all carry a distinct Trigger cell AND a distinct Closing-work cell, so **their CROSS-REFS are the cell GFM drops.** Three rows (100, 110, 143) go further to SIX cells — 143 is two complete sub-entries fused into one row. **These are NOT lost separators; they are a SECOND AUTHORING CONVENTION coexisting with the declared one, and no amount of escaping touches them.** ⚠⚠ **(D) THE METRIC IN THIS ROW CANNOT SEE A CORRECT REPAIR, WHICH MAKES IT UNFIT FOR THE CHECK. ✔MEASURED: escaping 45 content separators across 31 rows left the RAW separator-count histogram COMPLETELY UNCHANGED** (Table A still reads 5×507, 6×48, 7×17, …) **because `\|` still contains the character.** ★★★ **THE CHECK MUST COUNT *UNESCAPED* SEPARATORS — split on a separator only when it is not preceded by a backslash, which is exactly what cmark-gfm does and what was validated here against it on 263 rows. A raw character count both over-reports (11 already-correct Table-A rows looked broken by it) and is blind to every repair.** ✔**(E) OUTCOME, MEASURED BEFORE AND AFTER, in CELLS (the metric that matters):** TABLE A went from **518 correct / 8 under-celled / 67 over-celled** to **541 correct / 0 under-celled / 44 over-celled** — 8 short rows closed, 23 over-celled rows closed outright, and 8 more improved (their content separators escaped, e.g. one row 13 cells → 5) with a residual extra cell named. **Every repair was verified three ways per row: the row re-splits to the intended cell count; removing only the added backslashes reproduces the previous line BYTE-FOR-BYTE (so no citation was reworded); and cmark-gfm renders the row with the same text it had before.** ⛔ **(F) WHAT WAS DELIBERATELY LEFT, AND WHY THIS ROW STAYS OPEN — 44 TABLE-A ROWS AND ~212 TABLE-B ROWS.** The 36 five-column rows (+8 residual extra cells) were NOT repaired because closing them is a **SCHEMA decision, not a formatting one**, with two legitimate answers the operator must pick between: **(i) MERGE two cells per row** — 44 separate content judgements about which claim absorbs which, exactly the guessing this row forbids — or **(ii) WIDEN the main table header to five columns**, which costs the 541 correct rows only an empty trailing cell and would fix all 41 at once, but changes the file’s declared convention. ★ **A THIRD DEFECT FOUND IN PASSING, worth naming because it silently breaks the code-span heuristic any checker will reach for: [[D-HARNESS-TEST-SCOPE-FIDELITY]] carries an ODD number of backticks (57) — one span is unterminated, so backtick parity is wrong for the rest of the row.** ═══ ★★★★★★★ **CONVERGENCE PASS 2026-07-29 (operator-directed; USER DECISION: “one pattern, as close as possible to the current one, no pattern divergence and no data loss”) — TABLE A NOW HAS ONE SHAPE, AND THE DIRECTED TABLE-B PLAN IS REFUTED BY MEASUREMENT.** ✔**(G) TABLE A CONVERGED *DOWN* TO 4 COLUMNS, HEADER UNCHANGED: 584 of 587 rows now carry EXACTLY 4 cells.** ✔MEASURED before → after: **4-cell 543 → 584; 5-cell 41 → 0; 6-cell 3 → 3.** Widening to five columns was REJECTED on a decisive ground: the 5-column schema inserts `Finding` as column **TWO**, so under a widened header all 543 already-correct rows would render their Trigger under “Finding” and their Closing work under “Trigger” — **silently misplacing 543 rows to rescue 41.** ★ **AND THIS WAS DATA *RECOVERY*, NOT LOSS: each of those 41 rows was having its CROSS-REFS cell silently DROPPED by the renderer. If it DESCRIBES (a “Trigger:”, an “Open (…)”, an “Already fired”, an “Attributed”) it belongs with the Trigger, so cells 2+3 were joined — **22 rows**. If it PRESCRIBES (a “(closed)”, a “✅ FIX”, a “FIXED”, a “Closing work:”) it belongs with the Closing work, so cells 3+4 were joined — **18 rows**.** A descriptive cell was NEVER folded into a prescriptive one. Each merge was verified three ways: the row re-splits to 4 cells, the joined cell minus the seam equals the two originals concatenated (so nothing was lost), and cmark-gfm renders 4 cells. **The seam is ` ═══ `, already native to this file’s in-cell vocabulary, and it is now DOCUMENTED in the prose above the table so a reader meeting it knows it is a joint in the prose and not a column boundary.** ⭐ **ONE OF THE 41 WAS NOT AN EXTRA CELL AT ALL, AND ONLY LOOKING AT THE SEAM CAUGHT IT: [[D-FFI-PE-CRT-UCRT-MIGRATION]] read `SysVRegisterSave` — separator — `HomogeneousPointer`, an ALTERNATION between two config enum values. The defect is a property of the medium, it recurs for competent authors acting correctly, and it is caught by counting or it is not caught at all.** ⚠ **A MECHANICAL FINDING FOR WHOEVER BUILDS THE CHECK: THE FAILURE IS INVISIBLE IN THE RENDERED OUTPUT AND NEARLY INVISIBLE IN THE SOURCE. Measure the SHAPE of an accumulating leak, not its instantaneous size.** ⭐ **WHY IT EVENTUALLY BREAKS, not merely offends: ✔TREE `tests/test_support/scratch_dir.hpp:115-118` THROWS after 10000 collision attempts.** Names are `<pid>-<n>`, so as residue accumulates and PIDs recycle, the collision search lengthens — **unbounded accumulation from killed runs eventually turns a passing suite into a hard failure, at a moment unrelated to any code change.** ★★ **That is the shape worth naming: a resource leak whose symptom, when it finally arrives, will look like a mysterious harness bug rather than like a leak — and the run that triggers it will be blamed for it.** ⚠ **Note this cycle behaved CORRECTLY (its own run cleaned up); the leak is fed exclusively by INTERRUPTED runs, which is precisely the case a happy-path cleanup cannot cover.** | **(1) EITHER a reaper OR a documented cleanup — but DECIDE, and record which.** A reaper on suite start (remove `test-scratch/examples/` entries older than N hours, or not belonging to a live PID) covers the case that actually causes this — the killed run — because **no exit-path cleanup can ever run for a process that was killed.** **(2) If the choice is DOCUMENTED CLEANUP instead, it must be discoverable from the failure** — the `ScratchDir` throw must name the directory and say how to clear it. ★ **An undiscoverable manual remedy is not a remedy: the failure arrives months later, to someone who has never read this row.** **(3) Do NOT close this by raising the 10000 collision limit.** That is the same move as raising the timeout in [[D-TEST-EXAMPLES-SPAWN-TIMEOUT-FLAKY]] — **it enlarges the runway for an unbounded quantity instead of bounding it**, and the ceiling exists to catch a genuinely stuck name search. **(4) The reaper must not delete a LIVE run's directory** — a concurrent `ctest` is the normal case on this machine, so liveness (PID existence, or an age threshold well beyond the longest run) is a correctness requirement, not a nicety. ★ **Red-on-disable: seed the directory with a synthetic aged cohort and the reaper must remove exactly it, leaving a live run's directories untouched.** | created 2026-07-29 (TF-C84 fold). ✔MEASURED BY THIS PASS in the working tree (HEAD `7206e54`, TF-C84 uncommitted, a suite run in flight): 24 directories in 5 age cohorts spanning Jul 27 23:30 → Jul 29 10:26. ✔TREE `tests/test_support/scratch_dir.hpp:115-118` (the 10000-attempt throw). Parent: [[D-TEST-EXAMPLES-SPAWN-TIMEOUT-FLAKY]] ✅ (whose killed runs produced several of these cohorts — **the flake and the leak were the same event seen twice**). Instrument-measurement law: [[D-GATE-CORPUS-MEASURE-INSTRUMENT-INVALID]] (do not quote a quantity that changes under observation). | `D-ENV-NO-GNU-COREUTILS-ON-MAC` | P5 | 🟠 OPEN | 🔴 **OPEN — created 2026-07-29 (TF-C84 fold). AN ENVIRONMENT CONSTRAINT THAT COSTS *INVESTIGATION* TIME, RECORDED SO THE NEXT AGENT DOES NOT PAY IT AGAIN.** ✔MEASURED BY THIS PASS: **neither `timeout` NOR `gtimeout` is on PATH on this box** — GNU coreutils is simply absent — and it bit the TF-C84 agent mid-investigation, in the middle of root-causing an unrelated defect. ⭐⭐ **✔SCOPE ESTABLISHED BY THIS PASS, AND IT IS NARROWER THAN IT FIRST LOOKS: NO SHIPPED SCRIPT DEPENDS ON IT.** A repo-wide search at `7206e54` across `*.sh`, CI YAML and `scripts/` finds **zero** uses of `timeout`/`gtimeout`, and zero uses of `readlink -f` / `realpath` / `gsed` / `gdate` / `nproc`. **So this is NOT a live defect in the shipped harness — it is a constraint on ad-hoc investigation, plus a guard against the first script that reaches for one of these.** ★ Saying that precisely is the point: **a row that overstated this as "the harness is broken on macOS" would send a cycle looking for a bug that is not there.** ★★★ **THE CLASS, AND WHY IT IS NOT JUST THE BSD-SED ROW AGAIN: GNU-TOOL ASSUMPTIONS ON macOS FAIL IN *TWO DIFFERENT WAYS*, AND THE SECOND IS THE EXPENSIVE ONE.** **(i) PRESENT BUT DIFFERENT** — BSD `sed`, and `/bin/bash` at 3.2 — which fails with a *syntax* or *behaviour* error, usually near the tool, and is the mode [[D-HARNESS-SELFTEST-BSD-SED-PORTABILITY]] ✅ closed. **(ii) ABSENT ENTIRELY** — `timeout` — which fails as **"command not found", a message that reads like a TYPO rather than like a platform fact.** ⭐ **Mode (ii) is more expensive precisely because it is less alarming:** it arrives mid-investigation, it looks like the operator mistyped, and it costs a debugging detour into a question that has nothing to do with the defect under investigation. **The tool that is missing does not announce that it is missing FOR A REASON.** ⚠ **Note this is the third macOS-environment constraint this project has paid for** (BSD sed, bash 3.2, now absent coreutils) — **a recurring tax that is currently recorded only in prose and memory, which is exactly why it keeps being paid.** | **(1) Record the constraint where a SCRIPT AUTHOR will meet it, not only where an investigator will** — a short portability note listing what this platform does NOT have (GNU coreutils entirely; BSD `sed`/`date`/`readlink`; `/bin/bash` 3.2), sited with the existing harness portability guidance rather than in a new document. **(2) Any future script that needs a bounded wait must NOT reach for `timeout`** — use the language-level facility the tests already have (`runBinary`'s budgets, see [[D-TEST-EXAMPLES-SPAWN-TIMEOUT-FLAKY]]) or a portable shell construct, **never a GNU binary assumed onto PATH.** **(3) If a GNU-only tool is ever genuinely required, it must be DETECTED AND FAIL LOUD WITH THE REMEDY NAMED** — never invoked bare, because a bare invocation degrades into "command not found" at the worst possible moment. ★ **The rule that generalises past this one tool: a script may DEPEND on a tool or DETECT it, but it must not ASSUME it.** **(4) The check belongs in the existing self-test guard**, not in a new mechanism — [[D-HARNESS-SELFTEST-BSD-SED-PORTABILITY]] established that the self-test is what protects the first run on a NEW HOST, and "the tool is missing" is exactly a new-host failure. ⚠ **Do NOT close this by installing coreutils on this machine.** That fixes one box and leaves the assumption in the code, where it will fail on CI or on the next developer's machine — **and it would convert a visible constraint into an invisible one.** | created 2026-07-29 (TF-C84 fold, after `timeout` bit the agent mid-investigation). ★ **Cross-referenced rather than duplicated, per the operator's instruction:** [[D-HARNESS-SELFTEST-BSD-SED-PORTABILITY]] ✅ is the SAME FAMILY but a DIFFERENT MODE (present-but-different, in the sqlite driver's self-test) and is CLOSED; this row is the ABSENT-ENTIRELY mode and is open. Related macOS-host constraints: [[D-HARNESS-TCL-VERSION-AGNOSTIC]], [[D-TEST-MACOS-HOST-SPAWNS-FOREIGN-BINARY]], [[D-HARNESS-ARM64-LEG-HOST-ARCH-HELPER-SO]]. FILED AS A LINKED SET:** [[D-PROGRAM-PROJECT-WIDE-PARSE-GATE-MASKS-CENSUS]] · [[D-DIAG-TWO-CODE-RENDERINGS]] · [[D-DIAG-MAXPERCODE-SILENT-COALESCE]]. ⭐ **WHY THEY BELONG IN THIS ROW AND WHY THEY EXTEND IT RATHER THAN JUST ADDING TO THE TALLY.** Instruments (1)–(5) were cases of a REAL CHECK WHOSE GREEN WAS READ AS BROADER THAN ITS SCOPE — the check saw what it saw and a reader over-generalised it. ★★ **These three are the INVERSE: the instrument's own FIELD OF VIEW was truncated by the SUBJECT, silently, in ways no statement of scope by the instrument's author could have covered — because the author did not know. A census cannot state a blind spot it does not have.** **(6) `D-PROGRAM-PROJECT-WIDE-PARSE-GATE-MASKS-CENSUS`** — one TU's parse error deletes the semantic-and-later phases for the whole project, so entire diagnostic FAMILIES are absent and their absence is indistinguishable from non-existence. **(7) `D-DIAG-TWO-CODE-RENDERINGS`** — one code has two rendered spellings, so a pattern that matches one is STRUCTURALLY BLIND to the other; ⟲RELAYED, 133 of 189 runs were invisible to the pattern every grep in the arc used. **(8) `D-DIAG-MAXPERCODE-SILENT-COALESCE`** — an unannounced cap turns a total into a floor, with roundness as the only tell. ⭐⭐ **THE COMPOUND LESSON THIS ROW NOW OWNS, AND IT IS THE MOST TRANSFERABLE THING IN IT: STATING AN INSTRUMENT'S SCOPE IS NECESSARY AND NOT SUFFICIENT. AN INSTRUMENT MUST ALSO BE ABLE TO REPORT WHETHER ITS OWN VIEW WAS TRUNCATED BY WHAT IT WAS MEASURING — "did a gate stop me", "did a cap fire", "was there a form I could not parse" — AND SAY SO IN-BAND. A number with no truncation-report is not a measurement; it is a lower bound of unknown tightness.** ★ **✔MEASURED at `5093341`: `scripts/corpus-census.py` is the first instrument in this repo to act on that — it isolates per-TU to defeat (6), parses BOTH renderings and reports WHICH it matched to defeat (7), and knows the per-code cap so it can flag a figure as a floor for (8). It is the model to copy, and its LIMITS are equally the point: it fixes the measurement, not the compiler, and every non-census consumer still inherits all three.** ⚠⚠ **★★★★ BLIND SPOT (c) SCORECARD, ✔MEASURED — AND IT HAS A SECOND, WORSE MODE THAT THIS PASS IS THE FIRST TO OBSERVE.** TF-C86's pre-flagged prefix-resolution hazard did NOT materialise at CITATION time: all 9 `src/` citations of [[D-CSUBSET-STDARG-F001A]] used the full name. ⭐ **BUT IT BIT AT *NAMING* TIME IN THIS VERY PASS: the commissioned name `D-DIAG-CODE-RANGE-0x5xxx-DOUBLE-ALLOCATED` would have been truncated by the guard's uppercase-only regex to the generic stem `D-DIAG-CODE-RANGE-0`, and was uppercased to `0X5XXX` before filing** — see [[D-DIAG-CODE-RANGE-0X5XXX-DOUBLE-ALLOCATED]]. ★★★ **THE DISTINCTION IS THE FINDING, AND IT INVERTS THE SEVERITY EVERYONE ASSUMED. AT *CITATION* TIME THE BLIND SPOT IS SURVIVABLE: the guard runs over `src/`, so a bad citation at least has a MECHANISM that can notice it. AT *NAMING* TIME THERE IS NO MECHANISM AT ALL — the defect is baked into the ROW KEY, in a file the guard only ever reads as a haystack, and NOTHING in the system ever examines whether a registry row's own name is well-formed under the regex that will later have to match it. ★★ A row whose key cannot be matched in full is a row that will silently resolve by a too-generic stem for the rest of its life, and the collision it eventually causes will surface as a mysterious "wrong row" rather than as a guard failure.** ⭐ **SO THE CLOSING WORK GAINS A LIMB: THE GUARD SHOULD LINT *ITS OWN REGISTRY'S ROW KEYS* — every `D-*` row key must be fully matched by the same anchor regex the guard applies to `src/`, and any key that is a strict PREFIX of another key must be flagged. Both checks are trivial, both are self-contained in `.plans/`, and neither exists.** ★★ **TALLY: hit once, dodged twice, across two cycles — every time by a HUMAN OR AGENT REMEMBERING, and the third save came from an agent that had just written the paragraph warning about it. That is the definition of an unenforced convention — see item (b) of [[D-GATE-SCRIPT-PS1-PAIRING-UNCHECKED]], the sibling this row is already paired with.** ═══ ★★★★★ **UPDATED 2026-07-29 (TF-C89 close-out anchor pass, at HEAD `13f8b4d`) — INSTRUMENTS (9) AND (10). ★ (9) IS A GENUINELY NEW *SPECIES* AND MUST NOT BE FILED AS A NINTH SCOPE GAP, WHICH IS WHY IT GETS ITS OWN ROW:** [[D-GATE-ANCHOR-GUARD-READ-UNSTABLE]]. **(9) THE ANCHOR GUARD — THE SAME INSTRUMENT AS (2), BUT THE DEFECT IS *NON-DETERMINISM*, NOT SCOPE.** ✔MEASURED: run while `.plans/` was being rewritten it reported **2 missing anchors at 19:35:13 and 6 missing seconds later**, and ✔ALL FIVE PHANTOMS WERE PRESENT IN `.plans/` THE ENTIRE TIME (re-verified by direct grep at `13f8b4d`, 1–14 occurrences each). ✔MECHANISM, read from the script: `SRC_ANCHORS` is grepped ONCE, then the resolve loop runs **a FRESH `grep -qrF` over `.plans/` PER ANCHOR, sequentially** — so N anchors are answered against N different file STATES. ⭐⭐⭐⭐ **WHY IT IS THE WORST MEMBER OF THIS ROW'S FAMILY TO DATE, AND THE POINT GENERALISES BEYOND ANCHORS: INSTRUMENTS (1)–(8) EACH PRODUCE A WRONG ANSWER *CONSISTENTLY*, WHICH IS DETECTABLE BY COMPARISON — two readings that disagree tell you one is wrong. (9) PRODUCES A *DIFFERENT* WRONG ANSWER EACH RUN, SO COMPARISON YIELDS NOISE RATHER THAN A CONTRADICTION, AND NO READING OF THE OUTPUT REVEALS THE INSTRUMENT RATHER THAN THE DATA.** ★★★ **THEREFORE THIS ROW'S CENTRAL PRESCRIPTION GAINS A THIRD TIER: stating an instrument's SCOPE is necessary and not sufficient (established); reporting whether its own view was TRUNCATED is necessary and not sufficient (added by (6)–(8)); ★ and an instrument must ALSO be able to report whether its INPUT WAS STABLE WHILE IT LOOKED — "did the thing I measured change under me". A number taken from a moving subject is not a lower bound of unknown tightness, it is not a measurement at all.** ⚠⚠ **AND THE RETROSPECTIVE CONSEQUENCE, which none of (1)–(8) had: every guard reading taken while `.plans/` was being edited on 2026-07-29 is untrustworthy AFTER THE FACT. | `D-PLANS-PARALLEL-AGENT-STASH-HAZARD` | P4 | 🟠 OPEN | 🔴 **OPEN — ★ a MULTI-AGENT PROCESS-SAFETY CLASS, created 2026-07-29 (TF-C85). ★★★★ THE INCIDENT: A PARALLEL IMPLEMENTATION AGENT RAN `git stash push -u` TO RE-MEASURE A BASELINE, AND SWEPT A CONCURRENTLY-RUNNING ANCHOR AGENT'S UNCOMMITTED `.plans/**` EDITS OUT OF THE WORKING TREE FOR ROUGHLY 15 MINUTES.** `git stash pop` restored them CLEANLY this time, and **the implementer SELF-REPORTED it** — which is the only reason it is recorded at all. ⭐⭐⭐ **DO NOT FILE THIS UNDER "no harm done". THE FAILURE MODE IS *TOTAL SILENT LOSS OF ANOTHER AGENT'S UNCOMMITTED WORK*, AND IT IS INVISIBLE TO EVERY GUARD THIS REPO HAS.** ✔The anchor guard checks that `src/` citations resolve INTO `.plans/`; it cannot notice that `.plans/` briefly ceased to contain them, and it would have passed just as happily against the swept tree. ✔`ctest` has no opinion about markdown. ✔The pre-commit hooks run at COMMIT time, and the whole exposure window is BEFORE any commit exists. **There is no instrument in this repository whose green would have changed — which puts this squarely in [[D-GATE-INSTRUMENT-SCOPE-UNSTATED]]'s family, as a defect NO instrument's scope covers rather than one whose scope was over-read.** ★★★ **WHY A STASH IS THE PARTICULARLY DANGEROUS TOOL HERE, and the reason generalises past agents to any two people sharing a checkout: `git stash push -u` is WHOLE-TREE BY CONSTRUCTION. It has no notion of "the part of the tree I am working on", so an agent reasoning correctly about ITS OWN files still takes everybody else's. The blast radius is not a function of the operator's care; it is a property of the command.** ⚠ **AND THE RECOVERY IS NOT GUARANTEED, WHICH IS WHY "pop restored it" IS NOT THE MORAL: a `pop` can CONFLICT if the other agent wrote to the same file during the window, and a conflicted pop on a 2.4 MB single-line-per-row markdown table is a MERGE the other agent never asked for. Here the anchor agent happened not to write during the window. That is luck, and luck is not the control.** ⭐⭐ **✔RE-VERIFIED INDEPENDENTLY BY THIS PASS at the start of the close-out, against the restored tree — the content did survive: **806** anchor rows, TF-C85 material present, and the **8** pre-existing malformed 3-cell rows still exactly 8** (that last one matters because a bad restore is far more likely to show up as a structural anomaly than as missing prose). **Verification of a restore has to be MEASURED, not assumed from a clean `pop` message.** | 🔴 **CLOSING WORK — ONE RULE, AND IT IS ALREADY ACTIONABLE.** **(1) A BASELINE RE-MEASURE MUST USE A `git worktree` OR A COPY OF THE TREE, NEVER A WHOLE-TREE STASH, WHENEVER ANOTHER AGENT HOLDS UNCOMMITTED STATE.** A worktree at the baseline sha gives the same measurement with ZERO interaction with the live checkout, and it is the shape this repo's own tooling already supports. **State it as a prohibition on the COMMAND rather than as advice about care, because the hazard is in the command's scope, not in the operator's attention.** **(2) ★ THE HARDER HALF, AND IT SHOULD NOT BE SKIPPED TO CLOSE THIS ROW FAST: an agent CANNOT CURRENTLY TELL whether another agent holds uncommitted state.** Rule (1) is conditional on a fact nothing publishes. The honest options, to be adjudicated rather than assumed: make the condition unconditional (never stash the whole tree in a multi-agent session, full stop — the cheapest and most robust); or introduce an explicit ownership announcement at session start; or have the orchestrator hand each agent a worktree by default. **(3) RED-ON-DISABLE, and it is genuinely awkward, so say so rather than inventing a weak one:** a pre-stash hook that refuses when another agent's marker is present is enforceable ONLY once (2) produces the marker. **⚠ UNTIL THEN THIS ROW IS A CONVENTION WITH NO MECHANISM, which is exactly the state [[D-CONFIG-COMMENT-CLAIM-ROT]] warns about — record it as unenforced rather than letting the prohibition read as a control.** ★ **What DID work is worth institutionalising in its own right: the implementer SELF-REPORTED the sweep. Self-reporting is not a mechanism, but it is the only signal that existed here, and a process that discourages it removes the last one.** | created 2026-07-29 (TF-C85 anchor pass, at HEAD `7db3435`, written by the anchor agent whose files were swept). **SIBLING PROCESS ROWS:** [[D-PLANS-CYCLE-COMMITTED-WITHOUT-ANCHOR-PASS]] (the mitigation for which — launching the anchor pass IN PARALLEL with the implementer — is precisely what CREATED the concurrency this row is about, so the two must be read together and the fix to this row must not be "stop running them in parallel"), [[D-GATE-INSTRUMENT-SCOPE-UNSTATED]] (no instrument covers this), [[D-PLANS-REGISTRY-MALFORMED-ROW-CELLS]] (the structural anomaly used as the restore check), [[D-TEST-SCRATCH-DIR-LEAK]] (the other row about state left behind by an interrupted run). ✔MEASURED BY THIS PASS on the restored tree: 806 anchor rows, 8 malformed 3-cell rows, TF-C85 content intact. |
| `D-ENV-MACOS-GATEKEEPER-ADMISSION-IRREDUCIBLE` | P5 | 🟠 OPEN | ⛔ **NEGATIVE RESULT — ESTABLISHED, NOT ASSUMED. Recorded 2026-07-29 (TF-C85 close-out) SO NOBODY RE-INVESTIGATES IT. ★★★★ THE FINDING: THE FIRST EXEC OF ANY NEWLY WRITTEN EXECUTABLE ON macOS COSTS ~260 ms OF *OS ADMISSION*, INCLUDING ONE HTTP/3 ROUND TRIP TO APPLE, AND *NOTHING DSS CAN PUT IN THE BINARY REMOVES IT*.** MECHANISM: `syspolicyd` performs a Gatekeeper scan (`GK performScan`) before `main()` runs; for an ad-hoc signature it ALWAYS fails with **`-67018`**, and the exec proceeds anyway — **the round trip is paid to reach a foregone conclusion.** ⭐⭐⭐ **EVIDENCE, and the A/B is a ONE-BYTE PATCH so nothing else can explain the result: the CodeDirectory flags word was patched IN PLACE with page hashes recomputed and `codesign -v` passing on every arm.** DSS `CS_ADHOC` **264.4 ms, 12/12 scanned** vs DSS `+LINKER_SIGNED` **261.4 ms, 12/12 scanned**; **ld64** arms **256.4 ms and 258.8 ms, 10/10 and 10/10 scanned.** The FULL controllable flag space was then swept — `CS_ADHOC`, `+LINKER_SIGNED`, `+RUNTIME`, both together, and CD `platform=1` — for **30/30 scanned.** ⭐⭐⭐⭐ **★ THE ld64 ROWS ARE THE DECISIVE FACT AND MUST NEVER BE DROPPED WHEN THIS RESULT IS SUMMARISED: APPLE'S OWN TOOLCHAIN OUTPUT PAYS THE IDENTICAL COST. THIS IS NOT A DSS DEFICIENCY, AND ANY FUTURE "our binaries are slow to start" INVESTIGATION THAT DOES NOT FIRST MEASURE AN `ld64` BINARY IS MEASURING THE OS AND BLAMING THE COMPILER.** ★★ **DOCUMENTED, not merely observed — the flag could not have exempted anything even in principle:** `Security.framework/Headers/CSCommon.h:298` defines `kSecCodeSignatureLinkerSigned` as a PROVENANCE marker, saying it *"should be ignored in any new signing operation"*. **It is a note about who produced the signature, never a policy or trust flag.** ⭐⭐ **THE ONE LEVER THAT DOES MOVE THE NUMBER, AND WHY IT IS NOT A FIX: cdhash REUSE at a fresh inode cuts admission 262 → 104 ms — but a TRANSACTION COUNT shows 6 execs producing 6 network round trips in BOTH phases. THE HOP IS PER *NEW FILE*, not per new HASH.** ★★★ **So reproducible builds would HALVE the local cost and NOT remove the dependency on somebody else's server. A 60% saving that leaves the failure mode intact is a performance change, not a fix — and conflating the two is how a flaky suite gets re-declared healthy.** ⚠ **TWO DEAD ENDS, closed by measurement: copying a SYSTEM binary to borrow Apple's trust is `SIGKILL`ed; and `com.apple.provenance` is present on DSS output and clang output ALIKE, so it is not a differentiator.** ⚠⚠ **★ TWO THINGS WERE *NOT* TESTED, AND THE ROW SAYS SO RATHER THAN IMPLYING A COMPLETE SWEEP: (i) OFFLINE FAST-FAIL — the user's networking was DELIBERATELY not disabled, so whether the scan short-circuits with no route is UNKNOWN; and (ii) THE `Privacy & Security → Developer Tools` GRANT — a per-machine user/MDM grant that CANNOT be committed to a repo, and which Apple does not document as a Gatekeeper exemption.** **Neither is a loose end to be tidied; (i) is genuinely open and cheap, (ii) is out of scope for anything a repo can carry.** | ⛔ **NO CLOSING WORK — THERE IS NOTHING TO FIX. The row exists to STOP the investigation from recurring, and its closing work is the SET OF CONSEQUENCES it imposes on other rows.** **(1) `kAdmissionBudget{30000}` (`tests/test_support/run_binary.hpp`) BOUNDS AN EXTERNAL PARTY'S NETWORK AND MUST STAY GENEROUS.** ★★ **It must NEVER be described as the fix, and it must never be tightened on the evidence of a fast local run** — the quantity it bounds is not under this project's control, which is precisely why [[D-TEST-EXAMPLES-SPAWN-TIMEOUT-FLAKY]] split it from `kRunBudget` in the first place. **(2) TF-C84's UNTIMED WARM-UP EXEC IS THE CORRECT LONG-TERM SHAPE, and this row explains WHY in a way the fix itself does not: it EXCLUDES admission from the timed window rather than pretending to eliminate it.** A budget cannot make an unbounded cost bounded; only moving the cost outside the assertion can. **(3) IF (i) ABOVE IS EVER MEASURED, record the offline behaviour HERE rather than opening a new row** — an offline fast-fail would change CI advice, not the finding. ★ **MANDATE CHECK, ✔RE-MEASURED BY THIS PASS AND RECORDED BECAUSE THE SUBJECT MATTER INVITES THE SUSPICION: DSS COMPILATION INVOKES ZERO EXTERNAL TOOLS.** Greps for `system(`, `popen(`, `posix_spawn`, `exec*` and `fork(` across all of `src/` return **FIVE matches, ALL OF THEM INSIDE COMMENTS** (four prose mentions of `execve()` in the ELF/format layer, one "dispatch fork" phrase in `lir_text.cpp`) — **no call site exists.** DSS emits its OWN ad-hoc signature in `src/link/format/macho_codesign.cpp` over its OWN SHA-256 (`src/core/crypto/sha256.hpp`); **every `codesign` string in `src/` is a ground-truth comment naming the tool the format was verified against, never an invocation of it.** | created 2026-07-29 (TF-C85 close-out; the measurements are the operator's, made while gating TF-C85 and pre-scoping TF-C86). **OWNED BY / SUCCESSOR TO** [[D-TEST-EXAMPLES-SPAWN-TIMEOUT-FLAKY]] (TF-C84 — the cycle that NAMED the mechanism; this row is the follow-through that establishes it is IRREDUCIBLE). **CONSUMER:** [[D-LK7-CODESIGN-LINKERSIGNED-FLAG]] (TF-C86 — the flag whose MEASURED-zero effect on admission is half of this evidence, and whose rationale must therefore be fidelity-only). **SIBLINGS:** [[D-LK7-ADHOC-CODESIGN-MACHO]] (the signature DSS emits), [[D-TEST-MACOS-HOST-SPAWNS-FOREIGN-BINARY]], [[D-ENV-NO-GNU-COREUTILS-ON-MAC]] (the other environment-fact row), [[D-GATE-INSTRUMENT-SCOPE-UNSTATED]] (a budget over an unbounded quantity is an instrument whose scope was unstated). ✔TREE: `tests/test_support/run_binary.hpp` (`kAdmissionBudget` / `kRunBudget` and the warm-up exec) · `src/link/format/macho_codesign.cpp` · `src/core/crypto/sha256.hpp`. ✔DOCUMENTED: `Security.framework/Headers/CSCommon.h:298`. ═══ ★★★ **CROSS-LINKED 2026-07-29 (TF-C89 close-out anchor pass, at HEAD `13f8b4d`) — THIS ROW NOW HAS A SIBLING, AND TOGETHER THEY DEFINE A CLASS WORTH NAMING: [[D-TEST-SPAWN-CHMOD-ENOENT-AS-SUCCESS]].** ✔MEASURED in TF-C89: one `integrated_tests` run failed on `shipped_struct_timeval` with `chmod(<temp>/main, 0755) failed: errno=2` — **while the THREE assertions immediately preceding it PASSED for that same example (compile exited 0, artifact existed at that exact path, artifact non-empty). The file vanished BETWEEN TWO SYSCALLS in the shared `/var/folders/…/T` tree**, and the spawn path chmods with NO RETRY and reports the `ENOENT` as "spawn succeeded". ⭐⭐ **THE CLASS, STATED ONCE FOR BOTH ROWS: AN ENVIRONMENTAL FAILURE WEARING A COMPILER-DEFECT COSTUME.** This row's macOS admission/notarization delay and that row's reaped temp artifact are the same shape — **an outcome the project does not control, surfaced in the vocabulary of an outcome it does.** ★★ **AND THE COST IS THE SAME IN BOTH CASES, AND IT IS NOT THE LOST RUN: it is that the next reader spends the cycle looking in `src/`.** ★ **THE SHARED PRESCRIPTION: an environmental precondition must be reported AS an environmental precondition — named, distinguished from a compiler result, and never collapsed into a generic failure.** Note the asymmetry worth preserving: THIS row's quantity is genuinely IRREDUCIBLE (outside the project's control, hence a budget), whereas the sibling's is FULLY FIXABLE (a per-run temp path plus a retry) — **so they share a reporting defect and NOT a closing strategy, and neither should be closed by citing the other.** ⚠ Sibling's shared-scratch root: [[D-TEST-INTEGRATED-FIXED-TEMPPATH-COLLISION]] · [[D-TEST-SCRATCH-DIR-LEAK]]. |
| `D-HOST-DEFAULT-CLANG-IS-ANDROID-NDK` | P3 | 🟠 OPEN | 🔴 **OPEN — created 2026-07-28 (TF-C74). ★ A HOST-ENVIRONMENT LANDMINE, not a compiler defect: the first `clang` on this host's `PATH` is the **Android NDK** build, which compiles Darwin objects happily and then CANNOT LINK them.** ✔TREE-verified on this host (Darwin 25.5.0 arm64), not relayed: `which -a clang` resolves **first** to `/opt/homebrew/share/android-ndk/toolchains/llvm/prebuilt/darwin-x86_64/bin/clang` — `Android (13989888, +pgo, -bolt, +lto, -mlgo, based on r563880c) clang version 21.0.0`, InstalledDir `…/AndroidNDK14206865.app/…` — and only **second** to `/usr/bin/clang`, `Apple clang version 21.0.0 (clang-2100.1.1.101)`. MEASURED failure: a link through the NDK build dies with **`ld64.lld: library not found for -lSystem`**. Every clang measurement in this cycle had to spell `/usr/bin/clang` explicitly. ⭐⭐ **WHY IT IS A TRAP RATHER THAN AN INCONVENIENCE: both builds report `Target: arm64-apple-darwin25.5.0`, and the NDK one COMPILES Darwin sources without complaint.** It fails only at the LINK step, and only because `-lSystem` is absent from its sysroot. So `clang -fsyntax-only`, `clang -c`, and above all **`clang -dM -E`** — the exact invocation this cycle used to establish ABI facts — all SUCCEED against the wrong toolchain. Anything that measures without linking gets a plausible answer from a compiler nobody intended to consult. ★★ **SCOPE — VERIFIED, AND THE NEWS IS GOOD, WHICH IS WHY THE PRECISE ANSWER IS RECORDED RATHER THAN A WARNING:** the concern was raised that this might reach the sqlite harness's reference build (`$SQLITE_DIR/bld-dss/testfixture`) — the ORACLE the whole failure-attribution story depends on for deciding whether a corpus failure is DSS's fault. **It does not, by default.** ✔TREE: the harness never invokes a bare `clang` (`grep -n clang real-examples/c/sqlite/*.sh` finds exactly ONE hit, `build-and-test.sh:1518`, and it is a COMMENT about `-isysroot`). The host leg's compiler is **`${CC:-cc}`** (`build-and-test.sh:230`, via `add_leg`), and the cross leg uses `aarch64-linux-gnu-gcc` (`:232`). On this host `which -a cc` → **`/usr/bin/cc` ONLY**, `which -a gcc` → **`/usr/bin/gcc` ONLY**, both Apple clang 21.0.0 — and the NDK bin directory ships **`clang` and `clang++` only, no `cc` and no `gcc`** (measured: zero matches). **The NDK build therefore cannot shadow the harness's compiler, and past attribution results are NOT retroactively suspect.** ⚠ **BUT THE EXPOSURE IS ONE ENVIRONMENT VARIABLE WIDE, and that is the actual finding:** the host leg honours **`$CC`**, so a perfectly ordinary `CC=clang ./build-and-test.sh` — or any CI image, shell profile or wrapper that exports `CC=clang` — silently selects the NDK build, and the reference fixture then fails to link. Given `build-and-test.sh:817` explicitly warns that corrupting the reference fixture “corrupts … the ORACLE the whole failure-attribution story rests on”, and `:841` **TOLERATES** a reference build that “did not fully link”, the failure would be **degraded-but-quiet** rather than loud. That combination — a tolerated link failure plus a `$CC` that can point at a non-linking toolchain — is what makes this worth a row. | Trigger: **ALREADY FIRED** for interactive measurement (every `clang` invocation this cycle needed an absolute path). | `D-PLANS-ANCHOR-EXPECTATION-BECOMES-SPEC` | P4 | 🟠 OPEN | 🔴 **OPEN — ★ a PLANNING-DISCIPLINE CLASS, created 2026-07-28 (TF-C74) after the SAME mistake was measured TWICE in one cycle. A plausible-sounding expectation written into an anchor's “closing work” stops being a guess and becomes a SPECIFICATION — and a later cycle implements the specification instead of measuring reality.**  ⭐ **THE TWO MEASURED INSTANCES, both the same shape:** **(1)** [[D-PROGRAM-TARGET-LOAD-ORDER]] demanded a witness asserting “EXACTLY ONE `D_SchemaLoadFailed` and ZERO header `#error`s”. Correct behaviour emits **TWO** — `{C_InvalidFormatName, D_SchemaLoadFailed}`. In both cases the count was never measured; it was inferred from “one mistake, one error”, which is intuitive and wrong — the config tier reports WHAT it could not find and the driver reports WHAT THAT MEANS, and both are wanted. ⭐⭐ **WHY IT IS DANGEROUS RATHER THAN MERELY UNTIDY, and the reason it earns a row: the anchor is read by someone who was not there.** An implementer treats the registry as authoritative — that is the entire point of the registry — so a confident sentence in the closing-work cell is followed, not questioned. The failure then has TWO possible endings, and the bad one is likelier: the implementer writes the specified assertion, it goes RED against CORRECT code, and the cheap resolution is to **change the compiler to match the anchor** — suppressing a diagnostic the system was right to emit. The registry's authority is what makes a wrong sentence in it expensive. ★ **RELATION TO THE SIBLING CLASSES:** [[D-TEST-REDONDISABLE-DECAY]] covers tests that cannot catch (or that pin a defect); this covers the PLAN text that tells someone what to assert in the first place — one tier upstream, and it can manufacture the species-(5) problem from scratch. [[D-MEASURE-FIRST-FAILURE-MASKS-RESIDUAL]] is its numeric twin (never quote an unmeasured error count); this is the diagnostic-set form. ★★★ **INSTANCE (3), added 2026-07-28 (TF-C75 fold) — AND IT CARRIES A NEW WRINKLE THAT WIDENS THE CLASS BEYOND ANCHORS.** [[D-CSUBSET-CONST-EVAL-CHAR-SIGNEDNESS]] stated as fact that `intKindInfo(Char) = {32,false}` makes `(char)-1 < 0` fold WRONG on x86_64. An end-to-end probe against `/usr/bin/clang` **REFUTED the signedness half** — that comparison folds correctly in every reachable position — while confirming a DIFFERENT, narrower defect (the WIDTH half, and only in the static-initializer path). The claim had never been measured; it was inferred from reading the constant `{32,false}` and assuming both fields were reachable. ⭐ **THE WRINKLE: this one did not originate in an anchor. It reached the anchor because a COORDINATOR RELAYED A PLAN AGENT'S INFERENCE AS FACT.** So the hazard is NOT only "anchors become specs" — it is **any confident-sounding upstream claim**, from any role, that arrives without the words "measured" attached. A relayed inference is indistinguishable from a measurement once it is written down in an authoritative place, and the registry is an authoritative place. **RULE (v), added by this instance: attribute every load-bearing claim to its EVIDENCE, not to its SOURCE.** "The brief says X" and "the anchor says X" are the same epistemic weight — zero — until someone ran it. When relaying, mark unmeasured claims as unmeasured IN THE RELAY; when receiving, verify before writing, and file the REFUTATION in the row rather than silently dropping the claim, or the next reader re-proposes it as news. ★ Reading the source is a HYPOTHESIS about behaviour, never a measurement of it: this instance's whole error was treating a constant's value as proof of its consequence, when in fact one wrong field (width) MASKED the other (sign) so thoroughly that the second was not even observable. ★★★ **INSTANCE (4), added 2026-07-28 (TF-C76) — AND IT IS ITS OWN SPECIES: NOT A WRONG EXPECTATION ABOUT A REAL DEFECT, BUT A DEFECT ASSERTED TO EXIST THAT DID NOT EXIST AT ALL.** The claim, relayed by the coordinator from a prior agent: *`src/link/object_format_schema_json.cpp` accepts `"kind": "unknown"` and lets it load clean.* **MEASURED AT BASELINE `657c3a9`: FALSE, four ways.** ✔TREE **(a)** `ObjectFormatData::validate()` already rejected `ObjectFormatKind::Unknown` with `C_MalformedJson` at `/format/kind` (`src/link/object_format_schema.cpp:64-68` — present at baseline, verified with `git show`); **(b)** `loadFromText` runs `data.validate()` before returning (baseline `object_format_schema_json.cpp:2284`, ✔TREE `:2308` post-change) and returns `unexpected` if anything failed; **(c)** every entry point routes through `loadFromText`; **(d)** a test pinning exactly this — `ObjectFormatSchemaLoader.UnknownSentinelKindRejectedByValidate` — **already existed at baseline and PASSED**. ⭐⭐ **THE IMPLEMENTING AGENT DID NOT EDIT CODE TO MATCH THE CLAIM. It measured, REFUSED THE PREMISE, and reported — and that is the behaviour this row exists to produce.** Had it complied, it would have "fixed" a defect that was not there, and the fix's red-on-disable would have been measuring something else entirely. ★★ **THE GENERALISABLE RULE, RULE (vi): VERIFY THAT A REPORTED DEFECT *REPRODUCES AT BASELINE* BEFORE FIXING IT.** A fix for a non-existent defect is **unfalsifiable**: there is no red state to return to, so nothing can ever demonstrate the fix was needed or that it works. Reproduce first, then fix, then re-measure — and if it does not reproduce, FILE THE REFUTATION (as here) rather than quietly dropping it, or the next reader re-proposes it as news. ⚠ **AND THE HONEST OTHER HALF, recorded so this correction is not itself misread: a load-site rejection WAS added — on INDEPENDENT grounds, and it closes NO silent fallback.** ✔TREE `object_format_schema_json.cpp:191-214` now rejects the sentinel at the load site for two reasons that stand on their own: **(1) UNIFORMITY** — all name-accepting object-format sites now behave identically ([[D-CONFIG-OBJECT-FORMAT-SENTINEL-ACCEPTED-AT-NAME-SITES]]), so no future reader has to rediscover why one was special; **(2) MEASURED CASCADE ELIMINATION** — a `kind:"unknown"` beside an `elf` identity block ALSO emitted *"identity block 'elf' … fix the block name or the format.kind"*, advice that points at perfectly good blocks when the single actual defect is the kind. `validate()`'s arm was **KEPT**, deliberately: it guards a HAND-BUILT `ObjectFormatData` that never set the field (the zero default IS the sentinel), which no JSON path can reach. **Two guards, two distinct reachable populations — this is not duplication, and neither may be deleted as such.** | Trigger: a standing discipline, not implementable work — treat as SATISFIED for a cycle when its rows obey the rules below, VIOLATED when a row states an unmeasured expectation as a requirement. **THE RULES: (i)** write **MEASURED** expectations only; if it was not run, say **“expected (UNMEASURED — measure before asserting)”** in the row itself. **(ii)** phrase closing-work as the PROPERTY, not the literal assertion — “target-tier diagnostics ALONE, asserted as a set” survives a second code appearing; “exactly one `D_SchemaLoadFailed`” does not. **(iii)** prefer **set equality over counts** everywhere: a set adapts to the true answer and still catches everything else, a count encodes a guess as a constraint. **(iv)** when an anchor-specified assertion goes red against code you believe is correct, **suspect the anchor first** — re-measure and CORRECT THE ROW; do not adjust the code to satisfy a plan sentence. **(v)** (added by instance (3)) attribute every load-bearing claim to its **EVIDENCE**, not to its SOURCE — "the brief says X" and "the anchor says X" carry the same weight, zero, until someone ran it; mark unmeasured claims as unmeasured IN THE RELAY. **(vi)** (added by instance (4)) **verify a reported defect REPRODUCES AT BASELINE before fixing it** — a fix for a non-existent defect is unfalsifiable, and if it does not reproduce, FILE THE REFUTATION rather than dropping it silently. | created 2026-07-28 by TF-C74 from two measured instances, extended 2026-07-28 by TF-C75 with a third and by TF-C76 with a fourth (the defect-that-did-not-exist species, + rule (vi)); | `D-TEST-CORPUS-MULTI-TARGET-ROWS-NOT-ALL-RUN` | P3 | 🟠 OPEN | 🔴 **OPEN — ★ a TEST-COVERAGE CLASS, created 2026-07-28 (TF-C74). A multi-target corpus example declares expectations for four targets and, on any given host, EXECUTES roughly one of them — so “the example is green” means “one row ran and three compiled”, which is a much weaker claim than it reads as.** ⭐ **THE MEASURED INSTANCE:** `examples/c/char_value` (ctest **#294**) declares `x86_64:pe64-…-windows`, `x86_64:elf64-…-linux`, `arm64:elf64-aarch64-linux` and `arm64:macho64-arm64-darwin`. On this host (Darwin arm64) **only the darwin row actually RUNS** — there is no Windows, no Linux, and no `qemu-aarch64`. The other three are COMPILE-verified: real objects are produced and any codegen crash would be caught, but **no exit code is ever observed**, so their `exitCode` expectations assert nothing at runtime on this machine. ⭐⭐ **WHY THAT MATTERS RIGHT HERE, AND WHY IT IS NOT A HYPOTHETICAL:** this example's `arm64:elf64-aarch64-linux` expectation of **131** is corroborated only by CLANG FACTS (`__CHAR_UNSIGNED__` is defined for `aarch64-linux-gnu`), not by execution — while its `macho64` sibling, the one row that DOES run, asserts **131** and is **WRONG** ([[D-TARGET-CHAR-SIGNEDNESS-PER-PLATFORM]]). So the single executing row is the incorrect one and the correct row is the unexecuted one: the configuration that would most obviously have caught the defect — **running both arm64 rows and seeing them disagree** — is exactly the configuration this host cannot produce. That is not bad luck; it is the class doing what it does. ★ **THE HONEST CLAIM TO MAKE, and the phrasing matters because the current one over-promises:** a green multi-target corpus run means *“every declared target COMPILED, and the host-native target(s) RAN”* — not *“the behaviour is verified on four targets.”* Any argument that leans on a non-native row's exit code is leaning on an expectation, not an observation. This bears directly on the cross-target runtime-closure discipline already tracked in the plans. | Trigger: a standing coverage caveat, and it FIRES whenever someone cites a green corpus as cross-target RUNTIME evidence. Closing work, in increasing order of cost: **(1)** make the gap VISIBLE FIRST — have the runner REPORT, per example, which rows ran and which were compile-only, so the distinction is in the output instead of in this row (cheapest, and it stops the over-claim immediately); **(2)** mark compile-only rows explicitly in the manifest so an unexecuted `exitCode` is self-evidently an expectation; **(3)** close the gap where a runner EXISTS — `qemu-aarch64` covers the arm64-linux row on a Linux host and Wine/CI covers pe64 — and record per-host which legs are genuinely runnable. ⚠ **LABELLED SUSPECT (untested):** that every non-native row is currently compile-only on every host was measured for THIS example on THIS host only; the runner may already execute some legs elsewhere (the sqlite harness does use `qemu-aarch64`). Enumerate per-host runnability before quoting a coverage number. | created 2026-07-28 by TF-C74; ✔TREE `examples/c/char_value/expected.json` (four declared target rows, one runnable on this host) · ctest #294; the defect whose detection it bears on [[D-TARGET-CHAR-SIGNEDNESS-PER-PLATFORM]]; sibling test-honesty classes [[D-TEST-REDONDISABLE-DECAY]] (esp. species (5)) · [[D-TEST-CORPUS-C-NOT-VALIDATED-AGAINST-A-REAL-COMPILER]] · [[D-HARNESS-TEST-SCOPE-FIDELITY]] · [[D-MEASURE-FIRST-FAILURE-MASKS-RESIDUAL]] |

| `D-MEASURE-FIRST-FAILURE-MASKS-RESIDUAL` | P3 | 🟠 OPEN | 🔴 **OPEN — ★ a MEASUREMENT-METHODOLOGY CLASS, created 2026-07-28 (macOS-arm64 predefine-probe cycle, post-TF-C73). Not a code defect — a standing correction to how every error-count estimate in this registry must be read and written, filed as a row so other rows can CITE it.** ⭐ **THE MECHANISM:** DSS **abandons a TU at its first hard parse failure**. A corpus error count is therefore a count of the errors **REACHED**, never of the defects **PRESENT**. Clearing an early blocker does not only remove its errors — it **EXPOSES** later defects in the same TU that the compiler never got far enough to report. ⭐⭐ **THE TWO WAYS THIS MISLEADS, both MEASURED (RELAYED) this cycle on the 189-TU sqlite build:** **(1) a drop is not a fix-count.** Architecture + toolchain predefines took the total **213 → 80**. That is **NOT 133 defects fixed**: 136 `P001E` went to 0, and the residual 80 is a **newly-exposed population** whose composition was unknown until separately measured. **(2) a real, correct fix can make the total go UP.** Toolchain macros ALONE took **213 → 216** — not because anything regressed, but because entering `TargetConditionals.h`'s GNU branch reached 3 more errors than the branch it replaced. A cycle judging itself on the headline number would have reverted a correct change. ★ **THE DISCIPLINE THIS IMPOSES ON EVERY ROW IN THIS FILE:** (a) any claim of the form “closing X clears N errors” MUST carry this caveat explicitly; (b) a closing witness MUST pin the SPECIFIC error class it targets going to zero (e.g. `P001E` → 0), never the total going down; (c) the residual after a fix MUST be reported as NEWLY-EXPOSED and re-measured, never subtracted to imply a fix-count; and (d) an INCREASED total is not by itself evidence of regression — attribute it by class before judging. ★ The same caveat is recorded in this file's PREAMBLE so it is seen without needing to find this row. | Trigger: this row does not “close” by being implemented — it is a standing rule. Treat it as SATISFIED for a given cycle when that cycle's rows and report obey (a)–(d) above; treat it as VIOLATED (and correct the offending row) whenever a bare error-count delta is presented as a defect count. Closing work, if any: the durable fix is to make the measurement itself honest — an error-recovery mode that CONTINUES past the first hard parse failure so a corpus run reports the true defect population per TU rather than the first-blocker population. ⚠ **LABELLED SUSPECT (untested):** that per-TU parse error-recovery is feasible without producing cascade noise worse than the abandonment it replaces is a HYPOTHESIS — it must be prototyped and measured before being adopted; until then this row stays a discipline, not a feature. | created 2026-07-28 (macOS-arm64 predefine-probe cycle) as a CITABLE CLASS; cited by [[D-CSUBSET-TOOLCHAIN-IDENTITY-PREDEFINES]] (the 213 → 80 and 213 → 216 measurements that motivated it) · ★ CORRECTION 5 of [[D-CSUBSET-APPLE-SDK-HEADER-SURFACE]] (whose CORRECTION 1 over-attributed 136 where 24 was the arch-only truth); sibling measurement/test-honesty classes [[D-TEST-IGNORE-LIST-IS-A-LICENSE-TO-DROP]] · [[D-HARNESS-TEST-SCOPE-FIDELITY]] · [[D-TEST-CORPUS-C-NOT-VALIDATED-AGAINST-A-REAL-COMPILER]]; also recorded in this file's preamble. ═══ ★★★★ **UPDATED 2026-07-29 (TF-C87 anchor pass) — ★★ THIS ROW HAS A *STRICTLY LARGER* SIBLING THAT WAS NEVER SEPARATELY NAMED, AND CONFLATING THE TWO COST AN ENTIRE ARC: [[D-PROGRAM-PROJECT-WIDE-PARSE-GATE-MASKS-CENSUS]].** ⭐ **THE DISTINCTION, STATED ONCE, PRECISELY, BECAUSE IT IS THE WHOLE POINT: THIS ROW IS *PER-TU* MASKING — DSS abandons a TU at its first hard failure, so THAT TU's later defects go unreached. THE NEW ROW IS *PROJECT-WIDE* MASKING — `program.cpp` returns before `compileOneTarget` if ANY TU has a parse error, so ONE TU's failure deletes the semantic-and-later phases for EVERY OTHER TU. Different axis, larger blast radius, different fix.** ⚠⚠ **WHY THIS UPDATE EXISTS RATHER THAN A BARE CROSS-REF: for an entire arc, EVERY project-scope measurement anomaly was read through THIS row — the only masking row that existed — and was therefore mis-explained. ★★ Two anchored items, [[D-CSUBSET-UINT128-TYPE]] and [[D-CSUBSET-SYNC-BUILTIN-BARRIER]], were recorded as BLOCKED by [[D-CSUBSET-STDARG-F001A]] when they were merely UNMEASURED, and a cycle was ordered around that false dependency. ⟲RELAYED-MEASURED: compiling `src/util.c` and `src/mutex_unix.c` IN ISOLATION yielded 5 `S0006` + 1 `S0001` that the whole-project run was completely silent about.** ★★★ **THE RULE THIS ROW NOW CARRIES: WHEN A DIAGNOSTIC IS ABSENT, ASK *AT WHICH SCOPE* IT WAS MASKED BEFORE ATTRIBUTING IT. "Not reached in this TU" and "this phase never ran for any TU" produce IDENTICAL SILENCE and demand OPPOSITE responses — the first says fix the earlier blocker in that file, the second says your instrument never looked.** ⚠ **AND THERE ARE NOW THREE MASKING MECHANISMS, NOT TWO: the third and fourth axes are [[D-DIAG-TWO-CODE-RENDERINGS]] (a rendering the pattern cannot see) and [[D-DIAG-MAXPERCODE-SILENT-COALESCE]] (a cap that reports a floor as a total). ★ Before quoting ANY corpus figure, check all four — this row, the project-wide gate, the two renderings, and the silent cap. The arc's four incomparable numbers were all of them operating at once.** ═══ ⭐⭐⭐⭐ **★★★★★ UPDATED 2026-07-29 (TF-C87) — A *MEASURED COUNTEREXAMPLE* TO THIS ROW'S USUAL DIRECTION, AND IT SHARPENS THE LAW RATHER THAN WEAKENING IT. RECORDED AS A NEGATIVE RESULT BECAUSE NEGATIVE RESULTS ARE THE ONES THAT OTHERWISE GET RE-INVESTIGATED.** ⭐ **THE PREDICTION AND THE OUTCOME.** The standing expectation — the operator's own — was that closing [[D-PP-INCLUDE-REENTRY-GUARD-AWARE]] would EXPOSE the `__has_builtin` / `TargetConditionals.h` wall, exactly as this row's usual direction implies. ⛔ **IT DID NOT. ⟲RELAYED-MEASURED across all three legs at 189/189: `F001A` `4`→`0` and `P0016` `1`→`0`, with NO NEW EXPOSURE ANYWHERE — pe `13`→`13`, elf `8`→`8`, macho `54`→`49` (a pure subtraction).** ⭐⭐ **★★★★ THE REASON IS CHECKABLE, NOT HAND-WAVED, WHICH IS WHY IT BECOMES A LAW INSTEAD OF AN ANECDOTE: THE RE-ENTERED COPY IS *GUARD-DEAD*. Permitting re-entry added NO PROGRAM TEXT — the guard's controlling name was already defined in the shared macro table, so the re-spliced body read DEAD and contributed nothing for a later phase to choke on. macho's `P0001`/`P0009` were already at baseline and stayed there.** ★★★ **THE REFINED LAW, WHICH THIS ROW NOW OWNS AND WHICH SHOULD BE APPLIED *BEFORE* PREDICTING ANY EXPOSURE: CLEARING A BLOCKER EXPOSES RESIDUAL ONLY WHEN THE BLOCKER WAS SUPPRESSING ***PROGRAM TEXT***, NOT MERELY A ***DIAGNOSTIC***.** ⭐ **SO THE PREDICTIVE QUESTION IS ONE LINE: *does the fix cause more code to be COMPILED?* If yes, expect exposure and expect the total to be able to RISE. If the fix only stops a complaint about text that was already there — or was already dead — expect a PURE SUBTRACTION and treat a rise as a REGRESSION SIGNAL rather than as this row's usual effect.** ⚠ **THAT DISTINCTION ALSO MAKES THIS ROW *FALSIFIABLE*, which it was not before: "the total may go up" is unfalsifiable as stated, and an unfalsifiable caveat degrades into an excuse for any number. With the program-text test, a cycle can now COMMIT to a direction in advance and be WRONG — which is what makes the prediction worth making.** ★★ **THIRD AXIS RECORDED THE SAME DAY: [[D-PP-FATAL-RECURSION-UNBOUNDED-HANG]] is this row's masking effect operating on CONTROL FLOW — a wrong rule was suppressing a code PATH, and removing it exposed an unbounded recursion nobody knew was reachable. ⭐ So masking has THREE observed axes now — DIAGNOSTICS (this row), PROGRAM TEXT (the law above), and CODE PATHS — and only the first was ever named.** |

A misattribution is worse than an unclassified failure — it spends debugging effort AND lends false credibility to the classifier's other verdicts. Contrast the correct behaviour on the same run: 10 real confounds (recoverfault/walsetlk/zipfile) WERE recognised. So the classifier is not broken in general; it simply has no notion of "the environment could not have run this test at all". ═══ Already fired. | `D-TEST-HARNESS-REIMPLEMENTS-PRODUCTION-PIPELINE` | P3 | 🟠 OPEN | 🔴 **OPEN — ★ a TEST-SUBSTRATE / DUPLICATION CLASS, created 2026-07-28 (TF-C78) with **THREE** MEASURED INSTANCES — and instance 3 fired **ONE CYCLE** after instance 1 was documented, in the very file the warning was written into. A test harness that REIMPLEMENTS a production pipeline drifts SILENTLY, and every parameter added to production is a fresh chance for it to diverge — the divergence is invisible because the harness still COMPILES and its tests still PASS.** ⭐ **INSTANCE 1 — THE HARNESS THAT STOPPED MID-ARGUMENT-LIST.** ✔TREE `tests/mir/test_mir_lowering_c_subset.cpp` contains FOUR private `lowerToMir` harnesses that hand-assemble the same call `src/program/compile_pipeline.cpp:634-645` makes. Three of them stopped at `&hir->typedefVlaOriginBySymbol`, silently omitting `synthRecipeMap` and `returnsTwiceMap` — maps that had been in production for two prior feature arcs — as well as TF-C78's new `noInlineMap`. **Nothing failed.** The trailing parameters carry `= nullptr` defaults (a deliberate and correct source-compatibility choice at the production signature), so an out-of-date harness compiles cleanly and simply lowers a DIFFERENT program from the one the shipped compiler lowers. Every unit test in the file was therefore running against a pipeline that had quietly been behind for two features, and no test could report it, because the thing being tested was the harness's own idea of the pipeline. TF-C78 realigned **three** of the four. ★ **THE FOURTH IS STILL DIVERGENT AND IS DELIBERATELY LEFT NAMED HERE RATHER THAN QUIETLY FIXED, because it is this row's live witness:** ✔TREE `tests/mir/test_mir_lowering_c_subset.cpp:11150` (the setjmp/`returns_twice` FFI-carrier harness) passes `/*synthRecipeMap=*/nullptr, &hir->returnsTwiceMap` and stops — it does NOT pass `noInlineMap`. So the drift this row describes is not historical; one instance of it is in the tree right now, at a known line. ⭐ **INSTANCE 2 — THE SAME SPELLING IN THREE PLACES, FOUND AT GATE TIME.** ✔TREE the MIR-text keyword `"noinline"` shipped as a BARE STRING LITERAL in **three** places in `src/mir/mir_text.cpp` — the printer, the parser, and the error message that lists the accepted attribute names — sitting in **two separate anonymous namespaces hundreds of lines apart** (the file's own docblock at `:33` notes that the emitter and parser are deliberately separated). Its neighbours `binding` and `visibility` never had this problem for a structural reason worth stating: they route through the `symbolBindingName` / `symbolVisibilityName` NAME TABLES, so there was an obvious thing to call. `noInline` is a plain `bool` with no table to borrow, **which is exactly how the literal crept in** — the absence of a chokepoint is not neutral, it actively invites duplication. Folded onto one `kMirTextNoInlineAttr` constant (`mir_text.cpp:44`) at gate time. ★★★ **RECORD THE DISTINCTION EXPLICITLY, BECAUSE INSTANCE 2 WILL OTHERWISE BE MIS-FILED AS AN AGNOSTICISM VIOLATION — AND THE TF-C78 IMPLEMENTER INITIALLY MIS-FILED IT EXACTLY THAT WAY. "I initially called this a hard bar violation and that was wrong"; the corrected reasoning is recorded here so the next reader does not re-litigate it in EITHER direction.** `.dssir` is **DSS's OWN TEXT FORMAT**, and that format is **compiler-defined** — DSS is the only producer and the only consumer of its keywords — so its vocabulary legitimately lives in code. **The identity-branch veto is about SOURCE-LANGUAGE / TARGET-PROCESSOR / OBJECT-FORMAT identity, which must come from config** (plan-00's operative test: config-driven is the ABSENCE OF AN IDENTITY BRANCH, not the absence of an algorithm). Nothing about `"noinline"` in `mir_text.cpp` branches on any of those three axes. ★ **AND THE C-SIDE SPELLING REMAINS CONFIG-DECLARED, WHICH IS THE HALF THAT ACTUALLY MATTERS:** the source-language attribute name lives in `src/dss-config/sources/c.lang.json` (the `attributeSemantics.effects` row + both `linkageSpecifierIgnoredNames` occurrences), NOT in `src/`. The two spellings are independent by design — a different source language could spell the attribute anything and still lower to the same `.dssir` keyword. **So instance 2 is a plain DUPLICATION defect, not an agnosticism defect; the fix is a chokepoint, and the reason to record it under this row is that it is the same disease as instance 1 (N copies of one fact with nothing coupling them), one tier smaller.** ⭐⭐⭐ **INSTANCE 3 — THE TRAP FIRED AGAIN, ONE CYCLE AFTER IT WAS DOCUMENTED, AND A WRITTEN WARNING IN THE ADJACENT CODE DID NOT PREVENT IT (2026-07-28, TF-C81, [[D-CSUBSET-ALWAYSINLINE]]).** TF-C78 added a comment at `tests/mir/test_mir_lowering_c_subset.cpp` describing this exact failure mode — “any map added after this point was invisible to every unit test in this file while working in the real compiler”. **TF-C81 then added `alwaysInlineMap` and reproduced it verbatim:** the attribute worked end to end through the real CLI (probed: the over-threshold callee's `bl` vanished from the release binary) while **EVERY unit test in the file read a `nullptr` map and saw the flag as absent.** ★★ **WHY IT WAS CAUGHT AT ALL, AND THIS IS THE PART TO GENERALISE: because the tests assert the APPLIED FACT.** A test that only checked “it compiled” or “the attribute parses” would have PASSED against a dead harness and HIDDEN the gap instead of exposing it — the harness defect is only observable through an assertion that the flag CHANGED THE OUTPUT. ★★ **AND THE PROCESS FINDING, WHICH IS NOW A PATTERN RATHER THAN AN ANECDOTE: A WRITTEN WARNING IN THE ADJACENT CODE WAS NOT SUFFICIENT.** That is now **TWICE** in this arc — the `.bss` vacuity trap was the first, this is the second. **A comment describing a hazard does not prevent the hazard; only a shape that makes the hazard fail LOUDLY does.** This is the argument for closing-work item (3) below (make ABSENCE something that must be WRITTEN) rather than for writing a third, more emphatic comment. ✔TREE all FOUR `lowerToMir` harnesses now thread the trailing maps identically (`:207`, `:552`, `:618`, `:11484`), and the TF-C78 warning comment has been extended in place with the recurrence and an explicit “THERE ARE FOUR CALL SITES … update all four”. | Trigger: **ALREADY FIRED THREE TIMES (TF-C78 ×2, TF-C81 ×1) — and instance 3 fired ONE CYCLE after the warning was written, which is the evidence that item (3) below is the real fix.** ★ **STATUS UPDATE 2026-07-28 (TF-C81): ITEM (1) IS NOW DISCHARGED — the sentence “instance 1 still has a live divergent site at a named line” is FALSE as of `9699f99`.** ✔TREE all four harnesses thread the same trailing maps (`:207`, `:552`, `:618`, `:11484`); the fourth's one remaining `nullptr` is written EXPLICITLY as `/*synthRecipeMap=*/nullptr` — deliberate for that FFI harness, and note that this is already item (3)'s “absence must be WRITTEN” shape appearing by hand at one site. ⚠ **Items (2) and (3) remain OPEN, and the row stays 🔴 for that reason: realigning the four harnesses fixed the INSTANCE, not the CLASS — the fifth map added to production will drift exactly the same way.** ── Original closing work, in order: **(1)** ✅ realign `tests/mir/test_mir_lowering_c_subset.cpp` (was cited as `:11150`; the site is now `:11484` — [[D-PLANS-LINE-CITATION-ROT]]) with `compile_pipeline.cpp` — the last of the four; **(2)** sweep `tests/` for the general shape: any harness that hand-assembles a production call whose trailing parameters are defaulted (✔TREE start from the `lowerToMir(` call sites in `tests/asm/`, `tests/lir/`, `tests/opt/`, `tests/mir/`, several of which pass far fewer maps than production and may or may not intend to); **(3)** make it MECHANICAL rather than repeating the sweep every cycle — the durable options are (a) a single shared test fixture that calls the SAME helper production calls, so there is one call site and it cannot drift, or (b) a struct/options parameter at the production signature so a new field is a named initializer rather than a positional argument a harness can silently omit. ★ **Option (b) is the one that removes the class**: the defect is that "omitting an argument" and "deliberately passing nullptr" are indistinguishable at a call site, and only a shape where absence must be WRITTEN can tell them apart. ★ Test: whichever route, the pin must show that a NEW production map is visible to the harnesses without editing them — assert the PROPERTY, not that the file compiles ([[D-TEST-IGNORE-LIST-IS-A-LICENSE-TO-DROP]]'s rule, one tier over). ★★★★ **INSTANCE 4 FIRED 2026-07-30 (TF-C92) -- THREE CONSECUTIVE CYCLES NOW (TF-C78, TF-C81, TF-C92), WHICH MAKES IT A PATTERN, NOT BAD LUCK, AND THE ROW'S "remember to" MITIGATION IS THEREFORE REFUTED.** MEASURED: `tests/mir/test_mir_lowering_c_subset.cpp` has FOUR `lowerToMir` call sites that must each be updated whenever a HIR side-map is added. The `no_sanitize_thread` implementer's five form-cases ALL read 0 while the real CLI lowered the flag perfectly -- i.e. the harness, not the product, was wrong, and a less careful cycle would have "fixed" working code or shipped a vacuous green. No anchor pass was launched. ⭐ **It differs from (1) and (2) in the one way that matters for scoring the fix, and in one way that matters for the record:** it was caught at cycle START rather than after commit, so it produced NO bad commit and the `.plans/` work IS being done in parallel as designed — this row is being written by the pass it is about. **That is a better outcome, and it is not evidence the remedy works; it is evidence that a human noticed.** ⭐⭐ **WHY IT IS THE MOST INFORMATIVE OF THE THREE:** instances (1) and (2) are readable as forgetting the rule, which makes "remember harder" a plausible remedy. **In (3) the rule was actively recalled, written down, and announced — and still not executed.** Announcing an intention and issuing a tool call are separate acts, and nothing in the cycle connects them. **This is the third consecutive refutation of diligence as the remedy, and the first one that refutes it at the level of INTENT rather than MEMORY** — the failure mode has now been demonstrated at every layer it can occupy: forgotten (1), forgotten (2), remembered-but-unissued (3). ★★★ **THE CONSEQUENCE, STATED AS A CONSTRAINT ON THE FIX: THE PARALLEL LAUNCH MUST BE MECHANICAL — emitted by the same step that launches the implementer, not chosen alongside it.** A mitigation that has to be re-elected every cycle has now failed three times in five cycles, twice by omission and once despite being said out loud. Any remedy phrased as a habit, a reminder, or a checklist item the operator reads is refuted by (3) before it is proposed. ★★ **WHY THE EXISTING GUARD CANNOT CATCH IT, WHICH IS THE LOAD-BEARING PART:** `scripts/check-anchor-registry/check-anchor-registry.{sh,ps1}` validates that every `D-*` anchor CITED IN `src/` RESOLVES INTO `.plans/`. It is a check on the src→plans DIRECTION only. **It has nothing to say about whether a cycle updated its OWN row**, and — worse for this failure mode — a cycle that correctly writes `D-CSUBSET-ALWAYSINLINE` into its source comments and never writes the matching row would trip the guard, while a cycle whose anchor already exists (TF-C79's did) sails through with its row untouched and its evidence lines falsified. **A green guard is therefore NOT evidence that a cycle was anchored.** ★★★ **THE MECHANISM, STATED HONESTLY, BECAUSE IT IS NOT FORGETFULNESS AND WILL NOT YIELD TO TRYING HARDER:** in both instances the implementation agent's report was thorough enough that the cycle *FELT* finished, and the commit was taken on the strength of that report rather than on the checklist. A thorough report is precisely what makes the omission invisible — the better the implementer, the more complete the cycle appears at the moment the anchor pass is due. ⭐ **AND THE CORRELATION THAT NAMES THE FIX:** every cycle that launched its anchor pass **IN PARALLEL WITH the implementation agent, at cycle START** — TF-C74 through TF-C78, and TF-C80 — got anchored. **Both cycles that DEFERRED the anchor pass until after the implementation report arrived — TF-C79 and TF-C81 — were missed.** The variable is not diligence; it is WHEN the pass is scheduled relative to the report that creates the false sense of completion. ★★ **INSTANCE (3) NARROWS THIS CORRELATION AND WEAKENS THE CLAIM IT SUPPORTED — restate it in the corrected form, because the original wording now over-claims.** TF-C82 INTENDED the parallel launch and still produced no pass, so "scheduled at cycle start" is NECESSARY BUT NOT SUFFICIENT: the correlation holds over cycles that ACTUALLY ISSUED a parallel launch, and (3) locates the remaining gap between the SCHEDULE and its EXECUTION. **Corrected statement: every cycle that ISSUED the parallel launch got anchored; all three that did not issue it — by deferring (1, 2) or by intending-without-issuing (3) — were missed.** Written the original way, mitigation (1) reads as sufficient; written this way, it is visibly a scheduling property that still needs an ISSUER. ★ Filed as a sibling of [[D-PLANS-ANCHOR-EXPECTATION-BECOMES-SPEC]] and [[D-PLANS-LINE-CITATION-ROT]]: all three are ways the PLAN TIER decays while the CODE tier stays green, and all three are invisible to ctest by construction. | Trigger: **ALREADY FIRED THREE TIMES (TF-C79, TF-C81, TF-C82); OPEN because no MECHANISM has landed — only this record.** ★★ **INSTANCE (3) RE-RANKS THE CLOSING WORK BELOW AND DEMOTES OPTION (1) — read this before the original ordering.** Option (1), the zero-cost "launch the pass in parallel" habit, was **stated in the same turn it was skipped**, so it is no longer a candidate CLOSURE for this row; at best it is a candidate improvement, and it must never again be recorded as the cheap fix that makes (2) optional. **The revised ordering is: (2) the mechanical check is the only closing work that can close this row** — plus, cheaper and available immediately, **(1′) make the parallel anchor-pass launch a FIXED STEP of the cycle skill's implement phase (`dss-cycle`), so it is EMITTED by the workflow rather than decided per cycle.** (1′) is what (1) should have been; (1) as written was a note to self, and this row now contains three data points on notes to self. Closing work, in order of cost: **(1)** the zero-cost mitigation, adoptable immediately and the one the measured correlation supports — **launch the anchor pass IN PARALLEL with the implementation agent at cycle START, never as a step after the report arrives**; the pass then has to be actively abandoned rather than passively skipped. **(2)** a MECHANICAL check, which is what actually closes this row: a cycle whose commit touches `src/` and cites a `D-*` anchor in the diff, but touches NO `.plans/` file, is flagged — note this is the plans→cycle direction the existing guard does not cover, so it is a NEW check beside `scripts/check-anchor-registry/check-anchor-registry.sh`, not a widening of it. ★ **Design constraint learned from instance (1): the check must NOT be satisfiable by touching any `.plans/` file — TF-C79's failure was that a row's EVIDENCE LINE went stale, which a whitespace edit elsewhere would have masked.** Prefer flagging the specific anchors named in the diff and requiring each to appear in the same commit's `.plans/` delta. **(3)** if (2) proves too noisy for cycles that legitimately touch no anchor, degrade it to a REPORTING step rather than deleting it — a silent check is this row's own disease. ★ Test/red-on-disable: whatever lands, verify it by replaying `f9333fc` and `9699f99` through it — **both must be flagged, and a cycle like TF-C80 (which anchored correctly) must NOT be** — otherwise the check is either vacuous or noise. ★ **Instance (3) contributes NO commit to that replay SET, and that is a finding rather than a gap: it never reached one.** The replay SET therefore stays exactly `{f9333fc, 9699f99}` positive and `{TF-C80's anchoring commit}` negative — **do not inflate it with (3)**. ⚠ **And note what (3) proves about option (2)'s ceiling: a post-hoc, commit-shaped check could not have caught (3) at all**, because (3)'s omission happened at LAUNCH and was corrected before any commit existed. **(2) catches the damage; only (1′) catches the cause.** Ship (2), but do not let its greenness be read as coverage of this instance's shape. ★ Do not state an expected COUNT of flagged cycles anywhere in this work; state the SET of commits replayed ([[D-PLANS-ANCHOR-EXPECTATION-BECOMES-SPEC]] rule (iii)). | created 2026-07-28 (TF-C81 follow-up anchor pass) as the OWNING row for a failure previously recorded only inside the row it damaged; | `D-PLANS-ANCHOR-NAME-SEGMENT-COUNT-GATE` | P4 | 🟠 OPEN | 🟢 **DOCUMENTED 2026-07-28 (TF-C81) — a CONVENTION that was load-bearing and UNWRITTEN. Not a defect in the guard; a gap in what the guard TOLD anyone.** ✔TREE `scripts/check-anchor-registry/check-anchor-registry.sh:26-29` defines `ANCHOR_REGEX='\<D-[A-Z0-9_]+(-[A-Z0-9_]+){2,}'` — i.e. **`D-` followed by THREE OR MORE `-`-separated uppercase/digit segments is treated as a DEFERRED-WORK ANCHOR and MUST resolve to a `.plans/` row; a TWO-segment `D-*` name is treated as an informal feature label and is ignored by the guard.** The script's own comment states the rule (“Two-segment names like `D-OPT` are treated as informal; the registry contract enforces ≥3 segments”), but it is stated **only inside the script**, where nobody choosing an anchor name is looking. ★ **HOW IT ACTUALLY BIT, which is why it is worth a row rather than a footnote:** TF-C81 first named its anchor `D-CSUBSET-ALWAYS-INLINE` — four segments, therefore a registry-bound anchor — and TRIPPED the guard, because the row did not exist yet. It was renamed to **`D-CSUBSET-ALWAYSINLINE`** (three segments), which both satisfies the contract and matches its sibling `D-CSUBSET-NOINLINE`. ⭐ **THE TRAP GENERALISED: hyphenating a COMPOUND FEATURE NAME silently changes the identifier's CLASS.** `ALWAYS-INLINE` and `ALWAYSINLINE` are the same word to a human and different categories to the guard, and the failure surfaces as a guard error about a MISSING ROW — which reads as “you forgot the row”, not as “you spelled the name in a way that changed what the name MEANS”. That misdirection is the actual cost. **RULE OF THUMB: spell a compound feature word as ONE segment (`ALWAYSINLINE`, `NOINLINE`, `RETURNSTWICE`) and reserve hyphens for genuine name COMPONENTS (`D-CSUBSET-…`, `D-TEST-…`, `D-HARNESS-…`); when in doubt, match the sibling anchor already in the registry.** | ✅ **NO CODE CHANGE OWED — the guard is correct; only its discoverability was missing.** Closing work: this row IS the documentation, plus the note now added to this registry's header schema section (where an anchor name is actually chosen). ★ If a future cycle wants belt-and-braces, the cheap improvement is to make the guard's own FAILURE MESSAGE say it — when an unresolved anchor has ≥3 segments AND a 2-segment or differently-hyphenated variant of the same name already resolves, say so, because that is exactly the ALWAYS-INLINE/ALWAYSINLINE case and the message currently sends the author to write a duplicate row instead of to fix the name. **That is a message-quality improvement, not a contract change; do not alter the ≥3-segment rule itself — many existing rows depend on it.** | created 2026-07-28 (TF-C81 follow-up anchor pass); ✔TREE `scripts/check-anchor-registry/check-anchor-registry.sh:26-33` (`ANCHOR_REGEX` and its comment) · `scripts/check-anchor-registry/check-anchor-registry.ps1` (the PowerShell twin — keep the rule identical in both or the two CI legs disagree); triggering case [[D-CSUBSET-ALWAYSINLINE]] (renamed from `D-CSUBSET-ALWAYS-INLINE`); sibling naming precedent [[D-CSUBSET-NOINLINE-PER-FUNCTION-SINK]]; process siblings [[D-PLANS-CYCLE-COMMITTED-WITHOUT-ANCHOR-PASS]], [[D-PLANS-LINE-CITATION-ROT]] |

The pass did not need a build; it needed to re-read a file it had already produced.** ★★ **AND THE ROOT IS NOT LAZINESS, WHICH IS WHY A RESOLUTION TO TRY HARDER WOULD NOT FIX IT: a count is a SUFFICIENT reconciliation for a row that tracks a TOTAL, and an INSUFFICIENT one for a row that tracks MEMBERS — and nothing in the sweep distinguishes those two row shapes. So "was it always on the list?" is not a valid shortcut either.** ★★★★★ **UPDATED 2026-07-29 (TF-C91 close-out anchor pass) — THIS IS NO LONGER ONE INCIDENT. THREE CLAIMS MINTED IN A SINGLE SESSION WERE REFUTED BY EVIDENCE THAT WAS ALREADY ON DISK, AND TWICE THE REFUTING TEXT SAT IN A FILE THE PASS HAD ALREADY OPENED FOR OTHER REASONS. THREE INSTANCES IN ONE SESSION IS NOT BAD LUCK; IT IS A MISSING STEP.** **(a)** step (4)'s `time_t` "cannot be distinguished without a build" — the disproving census was sitting in TF-C90's OWN base-census directory (the instance already recorded above). **(b)** a "CI has never run for this branch" defect, escalated to the user as the cycle's HEADLINE. **It is not a defect:** [[D-LK-MACHO-STATIC-LINK-RUNTIME]] — a ✅ CLOSED row in THIS VERY FILE — has recorded since 2026-07-18 that the `Run Pipes` gate is a user-chosen, cost-motivated trade-off ("rather than block on premium CI"), and the user re-affirmed it verbatim when asked. The surviving, much narrower defect was re-filed as [[D-PLANS-CLOSEOUT-GREEN-FIGURE-LEG-UNQUALIFIED]]. **(c)** `D-FFI-DESCRIPTOR-ZERO-VARIANT-SILENT-ABSENCE`, minted and then **DELETED OUTRIGHT in this same pass — the name appears here ONLY as the audit trail; there is deliberately NO ROW for it and none should be re-created, so a future grep that finds this string has found its refutation, not its row** — ✔MEASURED refuted on four independent grounds already present in the loader plus two `$comment`s that anticipate the exact hypothetical: the ILP32 zero-variant absence is DELIBERATE and its rationale is written into `src/dss-config/shippedLibs/{stdint,stddef}.json` citing [[D-FFI-STDINT-PTR-WIDTH-ILP32]]; absence-then-use is LOUD (TF-C90's own cohort produced 32 `S0006`s); a typo'd `when` KEY fails loud via `rejectUnknownKeys(...)` in `matchVariantWhen` (`src/ffi/shipped_lib_descriptor.cpp`, whose comment names "a typo'd \"ach\"" verbatim); and a typo'd `when` VALUE fails loud via `dataModelFromName`, whose comment names "making the entry vanish on every target" verbatim. The general class was ALREADY recognised and closed as [[D-CONFIG-KEY-ACCEPTS-INVALID-THEN-SILENTLY-FALLS-BACK]]. **The row was removed rather than reframed, because a row that contradicts a closed decision or a shipped guard is WORSE THAN NO ROW: it re-opens a settled question under a new name, and the next pass has to re-refute it.** ⛔ **The deletion is deliberate and is NOT a lapse in this file's never-delete-a-row rule — that rule protects HISTORY; this row was never history, it was a mistaken claim four hours old, and its lesson is preserved here, which is where it belongs.** ⭐⭐ **THE UNIFYING ROOT, AND IT IS NOT LAZINESS EITHER: MINTING IS CHEAP AND REFUTING IS EXPENSIVE, SO THE PROCESS HAS NO STEP THAT FORCES THE CHEAP CHECK FIRST.
