Audit existing count-based diagnostic assertions for the same masking. ⚠ Do NOT resolve this by deleting the latch: it is correct for the driver; the TEST is what cannot see it. ~~~~ **THIS ROW'S STATED GROUNDS WERE WRONG AND ARE RETRACTED -- corrected 2026-07-30, SAME CYCLE, by the TF-C93 code review. The correction makes the finding BIGGER, not smaller.** The row claimed a production-vs-harness CONTRAST: latch "LOAD-BEARING IN PRODUCTION and INVISIBLE TO THE TEST HARNESS", on the basis that the driver sets `dedupWindow = 0` at eight sites. **That contrast does not exist.** MEASURED: `S_AttributeIgnoredForDeclarationKind` reports into `s.reporter` = `EngineState::reporter` (`semantic_analyzer.cpp:232`), a **DEFAULT-CONSTRUCTED** `DiagnosticReporter` (window 4) that `analyzeImpl` never re-configures -- there is no `reporter =` assignment anywhere in the TU -- so the window is **4 in EVERY path, CLI included**. The eight `dedupWindow = 0` reporters (`compilation_unit.cpp:176,185,340,688,801`; `program.cpp:520,1157,1250`) are driver/CU-scratch reporters that receive ALREADY-DEDUPED semantic diagnostics. ★ MY ERROR, recorded as the method lesson: I verified that `dedupWindow = 0` EXISTS at eight sites and then INFERRED that the diagnostic under discussion flows through one of them. It does not. Measuring an adjacent fact is not measuring the claim. ~~ **THE CORRECTED, LARGER FINDING:** the semantic tier's dedup window is 4 **in the shipped compiler**, so (a) the latch is currently DEFENSIVE rather than load-bearing -- dedup already collapses the 3-declarator case, which is why demo (iv) could not go red anywhere, harness or CLI; and (b) **every** identical-key semantic diagnostic beyond the 4th is dropped from REAL USER OUTPUT, not merely from tests. A file with five identical semantic diagnostics shows four. That is a user-facing truncation with no marker (unlike the global cap), and it silently bounds any "fires N times" assertion at 4. ⇒ Closing work is now TWO things: (1) keep the latch -- it is correct and becomes load-bearing the moment the message gains per-declarator detail, which changes the dedup key (that is the sole valid justification and must carry the whole comment); (2) audit whether a 4-deep silent truncation of user-visible semantic diagnostics is intended at all, and if so make it announce itself. ~~~~ **THE REPLACEMENT CLAIM WAS *ALSO* WRONG. Third statement, corrected 2026-07-30 by the pre-commit self-audit, and this one is READ FROM THE CODE rather than reasoned about.** The retraction above replaced a false production/harness contrast with "a file with five identical semantic diagnostics shows four". That is FALSE too. MEASURED at `diagnostic_reporter.cpp:207-241`: `isRecentDuplicate` **returns early** on a hit, and `recent_.push_back(key)` happens **ONLY on the accept path** -- so the FIRST report is kept and every later identical `(code, buffer, span, ruleContext, actual)` report is DROPPED while that key remains in the 4-deep `recent_` deque, i.e. until **4 other ACCEPTED diagnostics** evict it. N identical consecutive reports therefore yield **ONE**, not four. Confirmed end-to-end: `__attribute__((noinline)) int q1,...,q8;` -> **1** warning. Note also that "identical" requires the same SPAN, which narrows the affected scenario to precisely the multi-declarator shape the latch handles. ⇒ The "larger finding (b)" above (a 4-deep truncation of user-visible diagnostics) is WITHDRAWN -- there is no such truncation, and its prescribed audit is not owed. **What REMAINS true and is the whole of this row:** the latch cannot be shown red-on-disable, because dedup already collapses the repeat; the latch is kept because it is correct and becomes load-bearing the moment the message gains per-declarator detail (which changes the dedup key). ★★ THE METHOD LESSON, now the most valuable thing in this row: this single mechanism was stated wrongly THREE TIMES in one day -- (1) a production/harness contrast that does not exist, (2) "shows four", (3) only then the measured behaviour -- and each wrong version was arrived at by REASONING from an adjacent fact (that `dedupWindow = 0` appears at eight driver sites; that a window implies a cap). MEASURED: the `.sh` verdict named a compiler commit the run did not use.** The Linux corpus run printed `compiler : …/dss-code-prime @ 5093341d` while the actual HEAD was **`fb555bc0`** — a commit from two days and ~20 cycles earlier. ⓘ **THE RUN'S RESULT WAS STILL VALID, and that was verified rather than assumed:** the WSL source tree carried the cycle's work (`_byteswap_ushort` rows = 3, `Bswap` hits = 4 in `mir_opcode.hpp`) and the BUILT BINARY compiled a `_byteswap_ushort` probe at **rc=0**, which a pre-TF-C106 binary cannot. Only the REPORTED PROVENANCE was wrong. ★ **CAUSE — OUR GATE PROCEDURE, NOT THE HARNESS'S ARITHMETIC.** The Windows-side gate rsyncs the working tree into `$HOME/src/dss-code-prime` with `--exclude=/.git` (deliberately: the rsync exists so a cycle can gate UNCOMMITTED work, which a `git checkout` cannot do). The result is a HYBRID — current SOURCES over a stale `.git` (measured mtime 2026-07-29). `build-and-test.sh` then reports `git rev-parse HEAD`, which is exactly right for the real checkout its own header documents (*"the checkout at ~/src AS-IS on its CURRENT branch"*) and meaningless for the hybrid. **The harness has no way to detect the mismatch, so it reports a plausible lie with full confidence.** ⚠ **SCOPE, measured and NARROWER than first stated:** the `.ps1` reads the REAL Windows checkout and reported `@ fb555bc0` CORRECTLY in the same session — so this is specific to the rsync'd WSL tree, NOT a general harness-provenance defect. ⓘ Two cheap-looking fixes are both wrong: syncing `.git` costs **959 MB** and still misreports during a pre-commit gate (sources newer than HEAD by design), and replacing the rsync with a real `git checkout` destroys the ability to gate uncommitted work — which is the rsync's whole purpose. ★ Same FAMILY as the `.ps1` swallowed-update defect fixed in this cycle (a verdict naming a version the run did not get); the difference is that one was a swallowed FAILURE and this is an unverifiable INPUT. | Make the verdict report provenance it can actually VERIFY instead of a SHA it cannot. Preferred: have both drivers detect and STATE the condition rather than assert a commit — e.g. report `git rev-parse HEAD` **plus** whether the working tree diverges from it (`git status --porcelain` non-empty, or a source-tree digest that disagrees with HEAD), and say `@ <sha> +local-modifications` or `@ <sha> UNVERIFIED (tree does not match HEAD)`. ⓘ A cheaper partial: have the GATE script stamp the true SHA into the synced tree (e.g. a `.dss-gate-provenance` file the harness prints when present), so the hybrid announces itself. ⚠ Whatever is chosen must keep working when the tree IS a clean checkout — the common case for a user cloning and running the script — and must never turn a legitimate dirty-tree probe into a hard failure. Trigger: FIRED (measured, and it already produced a wrong verdict line in a recorded run). Priority: normal — no wrong test results, but every `.sh` verdict taken from this workstation carries an unreliable compiler SHA, which is exactly the kind of citation a later cycle will quote as fact. | the `.ps1` swallowed-update fix in the same cycle (same family: a verdict naming a version the run did not get) · [[D-GATE-INSTRUMENT-SCOPE-UNSTATED]] · [[D-PLANS-LINE-CITATION-ROT]] (a citation that looks authoritative and is not) |

The closing work below is therefore RIGHT but must be applied to the `.sh` for item (1), not the `.ps1`, and the sed portability fix must go the other way. ── **WAS (kept as the audit trail — its driver attribution is the part refuted above; the mechanism it describes is real and still live in the `.sh`):** 🔴 **OPEN — normal — created 2026-08-03 (TF-C111).** ✔MEASURED from the code: `real-examples/c/sqlite/build-and-test.sh:1160-1166` de-duplicates translation units by BASENAME, first-wins, iterating `for f in "${!TU[@]}"` — `TU` is an ASSOCIATIVE array, so that iteration is **bash hash-bucket order**, not sorted order (the `sort` at `:1166` runs AFTER the winner has already been chosen, so it makes the OUTPUT stable while leaving the CHOICE arbitrary). Which alias of a generated file wins — e.g. `bld/opcodes.c` vs `bld/tsrc/opcodes.c` — is therefore arbitrary today; ⚠ and the part that actually bites is that it is **not stable across a CHANGE to the key set**: adding keys can resize the table and reorder iteration, silently flipping which copy of a generated `.c` gets compiled, with no diff anywhere to point at. ⓘ Benign so far only because the aliased copies are believed identical — an assumption nothing checks. | Trigger: **fires the moment a SECOND artifact's TUs enter the same map** — i.e. immediately, on [[D-SQLITE-CLI-BUILT-ON-NO-LEG]]. Closing work: (1) `sort` `${!TU[@]}` BEFORE the first-wins loop so the winner is deterministic by path, not by hash bucket; (2) derive any second artifact's TUs into its OWN arrays rather than mutating the shared `TU`/`TU_BASENAME` maps; (3) ⚠ while there, make the identical-alias assumption CHECKED rather than assumed — if two paths share a basename and differ in content, `die` naming both, instead of picking one. | `D-TEST-CROSS-ARCH-SKIP-YIELDS-NO-VERDICT` | P3 | 🟠 OPEN | 🟢 **LEDGER + STRICT GATE LANDED 2026-08-03 IN BOTH HARNESSES — one item of the closing work below did NOT land and the row stays open for it; see the ★★ block in the closing-work cell. *(Was: 🔴 OPEN — ★★ created 2026-08-03.)* A SKIP THAT WAS INDISTINGUISHABLE FROM A PASS, IN BOTH RUNNERS, OVER 449 OF 557 MANIFESTS ON THE x86_64 CI LEGS. TRIGGER: FIRED.** ✔MEASURED, both harnesses: when a target arm's arch differs from the host arch and EITHER the manifest declares no `emulator` OR the declared emulator is not on PATH, the arm is marked `ArmStatus::SkippedCrossHost` and the runner **returns with NO assertion of any kind** — `tests/examples/examples_runner.cpp:817-836` (the two skip branches, both `GTEST_LOG_(INFO)` only) feeding `:890-892` (`if (baseline.status != ArmStatus::Ran) return;`), and the subprocess equivalent `integrated_tests/runner.cpp:672-698`, whose `[SKIP]` prints at `:685-696` increment **neither the pass nor the fail counter**. The ctest entry is GREEN. ⭐ **THE MEASURED SCALE, and it is the CI legs that matter, not the dev host:** `.github/workflows/pipeline-pr.yml` installs **no qemu at all** (✔MEASURED: zero matches for qemu, binfmt or aarch64 anywhere in the file), so on both `ubuntu-latest` x86_64 legs every one of the **449 of 557** manifests that declare an arm64-linux arm with a non-empty `emulator` is skipped — silently, and counted as a pass. ⚠ **There is NO accounting anywhere: no skip ledger, no per-leg runnability table, and no assertion that a DECLARED arm ever produced a verdict.** ★★ This is the project's recurring *instrument reports a pass over work it did not do* class in its purest form — the other members over-read a green whose scope was merely unstated; here a green is emitted for a unit that was never measured at all. | ⚠ **Do NOT close this by installing qemu on the CI legs — that changes WHICH units run, not whether an unrun unit can pass.** The structural fix is A VERDICT FOR EVERY DECLARED ARM: **(1)** both runners keep a SKIP LEDGER — per manifest, per target, per arm, with the reason — and print it in the summary beside the pass/fail counts, so `771 passed` can never again be read as `771 verified`; **(2)** the expected skip SET is pinned per (host-os × host-arch) and an unexpected skip is a RED, not a shrug — that is what makes the ledger a gate rather than a log; **(3)** red-on-disable: delete the ledger or the pin and something must go red. ★ Sequence this BEFORE any qemu-in-CI work — with the ledger in place, adding an emulator becomes a MEASURABLE DELTA instead of an unverifiable claim. ⚠ Both runners must land it together or the capability-pairing rule is broken again. ★★ **WHAT LANDED 2026-08-03, AND THE ONE ITEM THAT DID NOT.** ✔**(1) DONE, both harnesses:** the single `ArmStatus::SkippedCrossHost` is GONE, replaced by a shared closed verdict vocabulary in the new `tests/test_support/arm_verdict_ledger.hpp` — `Ran` / `ExpectErrorAsserted` (verified) · `SkippedByRunOn` / `SkippedNoEmulatorDeclared` (STRUCTURAL: manifest + host decide, no machine can change them) · `SkippedEmulatorMissing` (ENVIRONMENTAL) · `NotSelectedByRunner` (the harness limitation of [[D-TEST-CLI-HARNESS-BINDS-FIRST-MATCHING-TARGET]], ledgered honestly instead of being absent) · `Poisoned`. EVERY declared arm is recorded, including the optimizedPipelines arms a skipped baseline never compiles. Both runners print the counts line; the subprocess runner's Results line now reads `N passed, M failed, K of T declared target arms NOT verified`. ✔**(3) DONE — red-on-disable MEASURED, not asserted:** a scratch manifest (outside the repo) declaring `arm64:elf64-aarch64-linux-exec` with `runOn:["windows"]` gives the Windows leg a live environmental skip; default mode → WARNING + exit 0, `DSS_STRICT_ARM_VERDICTS=1` → `[ FAILED ]` + exit 1, a malformed value (`ture`) → REFUSED + exit 1, and a STRUCTURAL no-emulator skip stays green even under strict. Plus 12 unit tests in `tests/test_support/test_arm_verdict_ledger.cpp`. ⚠ **(2) NOT DONE — the expected skip SET is still not pinned per (host-os × host-arch).** The user's decided design replaced it with the opt-in strict gate, which is a DIFFERENT property: strict says "no arm the manifest asked for was silently dropped ON THIS MACHINE", it does NOT say "this leg skipped exactly the set it is supposed to skip". A leg that starts skipping a STRUCTURAL class it never skipped before (a manifest edit narrowing `runOn`, a target arm deleted) still reds nothing. ⇒ the pin remains the open half of this row. ★ Strict mode is opt-in via env `DSS_STRICT_ARM_VERDICTS=1`, wired through the CMake cache option of the same name (`dss_strict_arm_verdicts()` stamps the ctest ENVIRONMENT for the 557 `examples/*` entries + `integrated_tests`) so the out-of-repo gate scripts get it from a one-time `-DDSS_STRICT_ARM_VERDICTS=ON` configure rather than a recipe edit. Default OFF ON PURPOSE: strict on a qemu-less Linux box reds 449 manifests, and a developer must still get a usable green suite. ⚠ Deliberately NOT enabled in `.github/workflows/pipeline-pr.yml` — those legs have no qemu, so flip CI in the SAME change that installs it. | ✔MEASURED 2026-08-03 by direct read plus a JSON walk over all 557 `expected.json` manifests. ✔TREE `tests/examples/examples_runner.cpp` · `integrated_tests/runner.cpp` · `.github/workflows/pipeline-pr.yml`. **Class siblings, and NEITHER subsumes this row:** [[D-TEST-CORPUS-MULTI-TARGET-ROWS-NOT-ALL-RUN]] owns the *compile-only vs ran* over-read, measured for ONE example on ONE host, and explicitly leaves "enumerate per-host runnability before quoting a coverage number" as a LABELLED SUSPECT — this row DISCHARGES that suspect (449/557 declared-runnable arm64 arms, zero qemu in CI) and adds the half that row does not claim: the skip is not merely under-reported, it is UNCOUNTED and unassertable. [[D-TEST-EXAMPLES-CROSS-HOST-RELEASE-ARM-NEVER-COMPILED]] owns the SAME `:890-892` early return on the RELEASE-ARM axis (what is never COMPILED); this row owns the ACCOUNTING axis (what never produces a verdict). · [[D-GATE-INSTRUMENT-SCOPE-UNSTATED]] (the generalisation) · [[D-TEST-CLI-HARNESS-BINDS-FIRST-MATCHING-TARGET]] (the subprocess runner's second, independent way of never reaching an arm64 arm) · [[D-CI-ARM64-EXAMPLES-NEVER-SANITIZED]] · [[D-TEST-MANIFEST-ARM64-ARM-WITHOUT-EMULATOR]] (the one manifest for which this skip is permanent). |
| `D-TEST-QEMU_LD_PREFIX-AMBIENT-ONLY` | P3 | 🟠 OPEN | 🟢 **ITEM (1) LANDED 2026-08-12 — the spawn chokepoint now DERIVES AND OWNS the guest loader search root instead of inheriting it from the operator's shell. ⚠ THE ROW STAYS OPEN: item (2) is still only HALF discharged and the remaining half is named in the closing-work cell; it is NOT stamped closed to make a number look better. *(Was: 🔴 OPEN — ★★ created 2026-08-03.)*** ⭐ **THE TRIGGER FIRED A SECOND TIME WITH AN IDENTICAL SIGNATURE, and that is what settled it as a HARNESS defect rather than an operator error.** ✔MEASURED 2026-08-12 on this project's WSL leg: a raw `ctest` with `QEMU_LD_PREFIX` unset reported **468 of 826 tests failing**; with `QEMU_LD_PREFIX=/usr/aarch64-linux-gnu` exported and NOTHING else changed, **826 of 826 passed**. Every one of the 468 was the same single cause — `qemu-aarch64: Could not open '/lib/ld-linux-aarch64.so.1': No such file or directory` on raw stderr — surfacing to the reader ONLY as **`baseline exit-code mismatch (expected=42; OS=255)`**. (2026-08-03: ~450 failures, same cause, same message.) ✅ **WHAT NOW EXISTS**, all of it in `tests/test_support/run_binary.hpp:170-483` and called from `detail::spawnAndWait:605-617` BEFORE any handle exists, i.e. at the ONE chokepoint both corpus harnesses share: **(a)** `qemuUserGuestArch:243` derives the guest arch from the LAUNCHER'S OWN NAME (`qemu-<arch>`; a trailing `-static` is stripped as `qemu-user-static` PACKAGING, not arch; `qemu-system-*` and every non-qemu launcher are excluded because they do not read this variable and must not be handed its remedy). **(b)** `elfInterpreterPath:276` reads **PT_INTERP out of the artifact's own bytes** and never infers it from the arch — the loader's spelling is a TABLE (`ld-linux-aarch64.so.1` vs `ld-linux-x86-64.so.2` vs `ld-linux-armhf.so.3`), and a table here is exactly the host-identity ladder the agnosticism veto forbids. **(c)** The candidate sysroots are the `<arch>-*` directories the FILESYSTEM reports under `/usr` (the Debian/Ubuntu multiarch cross layout), sorted for reproducibility, and each is accepted only if it actually carries the interpreter the artifact asked for — the answer to "is this a sysroot for this binary?" is always a probe, never an assumption. **(d)** An operator-set value is honoured absolutely, through a snapshot taken at the FIRST EMULATED spawn, so the harness can never mistake its own earlier write for the operator's intent (a process spawning two guest arches would otherwise serve the second one the first one's sysroot). **(e)** When nothing on the host provides that interpreter the spawn is REFUSED, with one sentence naming the variable, the interpreter, the launcher, where it looked and the package that fixes it. ★ **THE REFUSAL IS A FAILURE, NOT A SKIP.** `spawned == false` maps to `ArmVerdict::Poisoned` in both runners and deliberately NOT to the `SkippedLauncherPrerequisiteMissing` class that exists in the ledger (`tests/test_support/arm_verdict_ledger.hpp:205`) and still has no producer anywhere: a skip is not a red, and converting a cross-arch failure into one is precisely the masking this row's closing work forbids. ★ **NOTHING IS AUTO-SET THAT COULD MASK A REAL FAILURE.** A static guest (no PT_INTERP), a host root that already carries the loader, a non-ELF64-LE image, a non-qemu launcher and an operator-supplied prefix ALL fall through untouched and spawn exactly as before; the only two outcomes the mechanism can add are "a correct binary now runs" and "one legible refusal". ★ **THE ORIGINAL FINDING, unchanged, and still why the row exists:** ✔MEASURED 2026-08-03 repo-wide, **nothing** in `tests/`, `integrated_tests/`, `.github/`, `scripts/` or the CMake test wiring either SET or READ `QEMU_LD_PREFIX` — the only occurrences in the whole tree were the sqlite driver `real-examples/c/sqlite/build-and-test.sh:323,1967`. OS=255)`. *(ii)* THE DERIVATION'S ACTUAL ANSWER on that host, printed from the production defaults against a real DSS-emitted `arm64:elf64-aarch64-linux-exec` artifact: `interp = /lib/ld-linux-aarch64.so.1`, `exported = /usr/aarch64-linux-gnu`; | `D-GATE-SQLITE-CORPUS-DENOMINATOR-UNPINNED` | P3 | 🟠 OPEN | 🔴 **OPEN — ★★ created 2026-08-04 (TF-C113). THE GATE THIS PROJECT TRUSTS ABOVE ctest REPORTS A PASS AGAINST AN UNSTATED DENOMINATOR. TRIGGER: FIRED.** The pe64 sqlite corpus verdict is the single gate that DECIDES a codegen change (a green 3-leg ctest is necessary and NOT sufficient — phase P2 of the UCRT arc went 720/720 green and the testfixture still died at `vtabH-3.5` with 0xC0000409). Its verdict line is `N errors out of M tests`, and the driver classifies GREEN on **N==0 alone**: ✔MEASURED at `real-examples/c/sqlite/build-and-test.ps1:1183` the summary regex is `(\d+) errors? out of (\d+) tests` and at `:1603` the denominator is a computed UNION, `$totalTests = $sumTests + $derTests` over the segments that happened to run. **M is never compared against anything.** ⇒ a run that silently executed a THIRD of the corpus would print `0 errors out of 110000 tests` and be classified GREEN, indistinguishable from a full pass. ⭐ **AND M DEMONSTRABLY DRIFTS — measured from this registry's own rows, every one of which quotes its own number as though it were THE number:** `330,935` (`D-FFI-PE-CRT-UCRT-MIGRATION`), `330,937` (`D-SQLITE-PE64-ALL-TIER-OOM-FILE-HANDLE-LEAK`, `-FULL-TIER-STACK-DEPTH`), `330,958` (`D-CSUBSET-INTRINSIC-BSWAP`), `330,993` (TF-C111), `330,991` (TF-C112), **`330,971` (TF-C113, this cycle)** — a spread of **58**. Tonight's run moved **−20 from the immediately preceding one while printing `excluded: (none — the full tier ran)`**, i.e. the driver asserted totality and the total changed. ⚠ **SUSPECT, LABELLED, NOT TESTED:** that the drift is benign and comes from segmentation/resume changing which segments contribute to the union (the `:1603` union is real; that it EXPLAINS the drift is INFERRED and unverified). Do NOT close on this hypothesis — it is equally consistent with units silently failing to generate. ★ Same class as [[D-HARNESS-SELFTEST-ASSERTION-ACCOUNTING]] (`OK (20 assertions)` over 46) and [[D-TEST-CROSS-ARCH-SKIP-YIELDS-NO-VERDICT]] (449 skips counted as passes): an instrument reporting success over work it did not enumerate. ⓘ NOT a correctness red — every recorded run had N==0; the defect is that N==0 is being read as coverage. | ⚠ **Do NOT close this by hardcoding an expected integer** — M legitimately moves as the corpus and the tier evolve, so a scalar pin would either rot into a rubber stamp or red on every real change; that is the `EXPECT_GE` inert-pin trap in a new costume. The fix is to make the DENOMINATOR ACCOUNTED rather than asserted: **(1)** derive the expected unit SET (test files × tier) from the staged tree the run is about to execute, and reconcile executed-vs-expected at the end, naming every unit with no verdict — the skip-ledger shape, applied to the sqlite corpus instead of the examples corpus; **(2)** make an unexplained denominator move a LOUD classification, not a silent field: report the delta against the previous run for the same (tier × target) and require it to be attributable; **(3)** first, settle the labelled suspect — instrument one run to emit its per-segment contributions and establish whether the −20 is segmentation or lost units. ★ Red-on-disable: force a segment to drop and the run must RED on the missing units, not print a smaller M and a green verdict. Priority: HIGH — this is the gate that decides codegen changes, and it currently cannot tell a full pass from a partial one. | ✔MEASURED 2026-08-04: the six divergent counts above, each grepped from its own registry row; the classifier regex at `build-and-test.ps1:1183`; the union at `:1603`; and this cycle's run printing `0 errors out of 330971` together with `excluded: (none — the full tier ran)`. ★★ **2026-08-04 — A PRIME SUSPECT FOR THE DRIFT IS NOW MEASURED, AND IT IS NOT A COUNTING BUG: THE CORPUS ITSELF MOVES BETWEEN RUNS.** `clone_or_update` (`build-and-test.sh:611-634`) does `git checkout --quiet master; git pull --rebase --quiet` on the upstream SQLite clone on EVERY RUN (reflog-confirmed: `2026-08-03 15:33:44 pull --rebase: Fast-forward f034d515b4 -> 17cf702308`). So two runs a day apart legitimately execute DIFFERENT upstream test corpora, and **no quoted figure in this project records the upstream commit it was measured at** — the ~330,964 veryquick and 1,061,995 full numbers were taken at `f034d515b4` (2026-07-31/08-01) while the artifacts now in the tree are `17cf702308`. ⇒ **there is no single "the sqlite baseline"; the existing figures are not comparable to each other at the source level, and re-quoting a bare number is itself the error.** ⚠ LABELLED SUSPECT, NOT YET CONFIRMED: the mechanism is MEASURED, but nobody has shown that the specific drift 330,993 / 330,991 / 330,971 / 330,964 maps to four distinct upstream commits — an alternative (or additional) cause inside the counter is NOT excluded. THE TEST THAT SETTLES IT: record the upstream commit alongside every corpus figure, then re-run twice at a PINNED commit — if the count is stable there and moves only across commits, the counter is exonerated; if it drifts at a fixed commit, the counter is the defect. Either way this makes M meaningful, which is the row's whole point. Closing work therefore GAINS an item: **stamp every corpus figure with its upstream commit** (and pin the commit for any run used as codegen evidence), alongside the original "check M, not just N==0". [[D-HARNESS-SQLITE-STAGED-TREE-MIXED-VINTAGE]] is the same harness behaviour seen from the staging side. ✔TREE `real-examples/c/sqlite/build-and-test.ps1` (+ the `.sh` sibling — capability-paired, so a fix lands in BOTH or the pairing rule is broken again). **Class siblings:** [[D-GATE-INSTRUMENT-SCOPE-UNSTATED]] (the generalisation) · [[D-HARNESS-SELFTEST-ASSERTION-ACCOUNTING]] · [[D-TEST-CROSS-ARCH-SKIP-YIELDS-NO-VERDICT]] · [[D-GATE-FAILING-TU-ERROR-COUNT-IS-A-FLOOR]] (the numerator's matching under-report — together they mean neither side of `N errors out of M` is currently trustworthy as an absolute). |

| `D-GATE-MTIME-PRESERVING-RESTORE-SKIPS-REBUILD` | P3 | 🟠 OPEN | 🔴 **OPEN — HIGH — created 2026-08-04. ★★ THIS DEFECT FABRICATES RED-ON-DISABLE EVIDENCE, AND IT DOES SO IN THE *OVER*-CLAIMING DIRECTION — the one that makes a guard look STRONGER than it is, which is the worst direction available. TRIGGER ALREADY FIRED (caught in flight, not shipped).** ✔**MEASURED during this cycle's red-on-disable work on the static-data overlap refusal:** PowerShell `Copy-Item` **PRESERVES the source file's `LastWriteTime`**, so restoring `src/asm/asm.cpp` from a backup left the RESTORED file's mtime OLDER than the `.obj` built from the MUTATED version. Ninja compared mtimes, found nothing to do, SKIPPED the recompile — and the next test run therefore measured the **stale mutated binary** while the pristine source sat on disk looking restored. The agent came one step from recording *"the restored pin is still red"*, i.e. crediting a guard with catching a mutation it had never actually been shown. Caught ONLY by comparing the `.obj`/DLL timestamps against the source; fixed by touching the file. ★ **THE GENERALISATION IS THE DURABLE HALF: any mtime-preserving restore breaks incremental-build causality, so a red-on-disable protocol must VERIFY that the artifact rebuilt, never ASSUME it.** The mutate→build→observe-red→restore→build→observe-green loop silently drops its SECOND build, after which both observations come from the same binary — a protocol that can no longer distinguish "the guard fired" from "I measured one artifact twice". ⚠ **This is the Windows-native, SELF-INFLICTED sibling of the `/mnt/c` clock-skew rule** (rsync stamps Windows mtimes OLDER than the WSL objects → `make` skips a changed `.cpp` → stale `.o` → false reds; the standing mitigation is to `touch` every changed file after an rsync). ⓘ **That rule lives in MEMORY.md and has NEVER had a registry row** — grep-verified 2026-08-04: zero occurrences of the clock-skew rule anywhere under `.plans/`. So the CLASS has been carried as single-leg folklore, which is precisely why it re-appeared on the other leg under a different mechanism. ⓘ The two differ in DIRECTION OF HARM and both are bad: the `/mnt/c` form manufactures FALSE REDS (costly but self-announcing); this form manufactures FALSE CONFIRMATION (silent, and it certifies a test as strict when it may be vacuous — see the vacuity ledger in [[D-TEST-REDONDISABLE-DECAY]], of which this is a NEW species: the pin may be perfectly good, it is the MEASUREMENT that is void). ═══ ⚠⚠ **SECOND MEASURED OCCURRENCE — 2026-08-12 — AND IT IS WHY THIS ROW IS STILL OPEN RATHER THAN CLOSED BY THE PASS THAT RECORDED IT.** ✔MEASURED, same host and same mechanism: `Copy-Item` preserved the SOURCE's `LastWriteTime`, so a file restored from a backup was left OLDER than its own `.obj`; ninja reported **`no work to do`**; the resulting **stale binary produced 8 FALSE REDS**. ★★ **THE NEW INFORMATION IS THE DIRECTION, AND IT INVERTS THIS ROW'S OWN FRAMING.** The row above argues that this Windows-native form manufactures FALSE CONFIRMATION while the `/mnt/c` form manufactures FALSE REDS — a distinction it draws between the two MECHANISMS. This occurrence produced FALSE REDS **from the Windows-native mechanism**, so both directions are now measured on the SAME mechanism. ⇒ **THE DIRECTION OF HARM IS NOT A PROPERTY OF THE MECHANISM AT ALL — it is decided by what you happened to be measuring when the rebuild was skipped.** Neither direction may be assumed, and "this form fails loudly" is not available as comfort. ★ **HOW IT WAS CAUGHT IS ALSO NEW, AND IT WAS NOT THIS ROW'S INSTRUMENT:** the first instance was found by comparing `.obj`/DLL timestamps against the source; this one was found because **the pass/fail PATTERN was impossible for the restored source** — the evidence was the SHAPE of the result set, not any instrument. That is luck wearing the costume of diagnosis, and it is an argument for remedy (c), not for confidence. ⚠ **THE MITIGATION APPLIED WAS REMEDY (a) — `touch` after any restore-from-backup — WHICH THIS ROW'S OWN CLOSING WORK ALREADY RATES AS *"cheapest, but still mtime-based — a discipline, not a guarantee"*. THE ROW THEREFORE STAYS OPEN AT HIGH.** Closing it on the strength of the remedy it itself labels insufficient is exactly the *"a guard that is weakened every time it fires ends up asserting nothing"* failure, and this is the second firing. ⓘ **RECORDED BECAUSE IT WAS A NEAR MISS:** this recurrence was briefed to the registry pass as a NEW, BORN-CLOSED row (`D-BUILD-RESTORED-FILE-MTIME-DEFEATS-NINJA`). It was folded in HERE instead — a duplicate would have split the evidence for a HIGH open defect across two owners, converted a SECOND firing into a first, and closed the class on a discipline while the real remedy stayed unbuilt. The symptom-vocabulary grep that caught it (`Copy-Item`, `LastWriteTime`, `ninja`, `mtime`) is the reason the rule *"grep the SYMPTOM, not the title you have in mind"* exists. Trigger: FIRED TWICE (2026-08-04, 2026-08-12); re-fires on every red-on-disable measurement that restores by file copy rather than by `git checkout`, on any host. | Make the red-on-disable protocol PROVE the rebuild instead of assuming it: **(a)** `touch` the restored file (cheapest, but still mtime-based — a discipline, not a guarantee); **(b)** ASSERT that the `.obj`/DLL mtime ADVANCED past the restore before believing ANY measurement taken afterwards, failing loud otherwise; or **(c)** key the check on CONTENT — hash the source and compare against a hash the build records — so causality no longer rests on a clock at all. ★ Only (c) survives all three known mechanisms at once: a mtime-preserving copy, a host/guest clock skew, and a filesystem with coarse timestamps. Whichever ships, encode it in the dss-cycle red-on-disable step so it is ENFORCED rather than remembered — a protocol defect that only a careful agent catches is not fixed. ⚠ Second, non-optional half: give the `/mnt/c` clock-skew rule its own registry row (or fold it into this one explicitly) so the class is tracked on BOTH legs instead of surviving as memory-only folklore on one. | ✔MEASURED 2026-08-04: `Copy-Item` preserved `LastWriteTime` on the restore of `src/asm/asm.cpp`; the resulting `.obj` was NEWER than the restored source; Ninja performed no recompile; the subsequent run executed the mutated binary. Detected by direct obj/DLL-vs-source timestamp comparison; remedied by `touch`. ✔MEASURED 2026-08-04 (grep): the `/mnt/c` clock-skew rule has zero occurrences under `.plans/` — it exists only in MEMORY.md. ✔TREE `src/asm/asm.cpp` (the restored file) · `build-dbg/` (Ninja objects + `bin/dss/libdss-code-prime.dll`). **Cross-refs:** [[D-TEST-REDONDISABLE-DECAY]] (the red-on-disable vacuity ledger — pointer added there; this is a new species, an invalid MEASUREMENT rather than an inert pin) · [[D-GATE-CORPUS-MEASURE-INSTRUMENT-INVALID]] · [[D-GATE-INSTRUMENT-SCOPE-UNSTATED]] · [[D-MEASURE-FIRST-FAILURE-MASKS-RESIDUAL]] (the instrument-validity family) · [[D-HARNESS-WSL-TREE-PROVENANCE-UNVERIFIABLE]] (the rsync'd-tree sibling: a run that measures a tree other than the one it names) · the `/mnt/c` clock-skew rule in MEMORY.md, unrowed · the dss-cycle red-on-disable step. |
| `D-GLOB-BASE-DIR-HOST-CASE-SENSITIVITY` | P3 | 🟠 OPEN | 🟠 **OPEN — normal — created 2026-08-04 (TF-C114 §A.7, found while closing D-PP-HEADER-CASE-INSENSITIVE-PE). A `--project` SOURCE GLOB IS MATCHED HALF BY THE OS AND HALF BY DSS, SO IT IS HOST-CASE-DEPENDENT IN THE SAME WAY `#include` WAS.** ✔**READ FROM THE CODE (`src/core/types/glob_match.cpp:162-254`, `expandGlob`):** the pattern is split at the last `/` before its first metacharacter; the LITERAL BASE DIRECTORY is handed to `fs::recursive_directory_iterator` (so whether `Src/**/*.c` finds `src/` is decided by the HOST filesystem — it does on NTFS/APFS, it does not on ext4), while the GLOB TAIL is matched by DSS's own `matchSegments`, which compares BYTE-EXACTLY. One pattern, two different case rules, and which one you get depends on where the metacharacter falls. ⚠ NOT simply "apply `headerNameMatching` here": a project's source-file glob is not a header name, it names files in the USER'S tree, and the closest honest analogue would be the host-tree convention, not the TARGET's. Closing work: DECIDE the rule (byte-exact everywhere is the defensible default — it is what git and every build system assume — with a loud diagnostic when the base dir exists only under a different case), then make BOTH halves obey it, reusing `asciiToLower` rather than minting a second folder. **Trigger:** open now; a mis-cased base dir in a `.dss-project.json` on a case-insensitive host silently resolves and then fails on the Linux leg. ROOT-CAUSED to the STAGED SOURCES, not the compiler: `~/src/sqlite/bld-dss/sqlite3.c` carries `SQLITE_SOURCE_ID "2026-07-06 16:26:30 7f49a7a9…"` (mtime Jul 6) while `~/src/sqlite/bld-dss/sqlite3.h` — the header `shell.c:161` includes — carries `"2026-08-03 15:05:05 0f873f56…"` (mtime Aug 3). `shell.c`'s own startup check compares `sqlite3_sourceid()` against the compile-time `SQLITE_SOURCE_ID` and refuses; a gcc build of the same pair would refuse identically. ★ THE REAL HAZARD IS ATTRIBUTION: the failure is a clean compile followed by a runtime refusal, i.e. exactly what a miscompile looks like from the outside, so a future CLI cycle can burn a day inside the code generator. ★★ **ROOT-CAUSED 2026-08-04 (MEASURED, and it CORRECTS this row's first draft — the tree was FIVE-way, not two-way): the harness causes it.** `clone_or_update` (`build-and-test.sh:611-634`) runs `git checkout --quiet master; git pull --rebase --quiet` on the upstream clone EVERY RUN while `bld-dss` is REUSED, and `make` refreshes only the requested target's prerequisites. The dangerous INVERSE is a deleted config, a removed bad test, or a retired golden LINGERING in the WSL tree, in which case **the leg goes GREEN on state the commit does not contain** — a gate certifying a tree that will never exist again. **A no-delete rsync cannot distinguish those two cases; which one you get is decided by what you happened to delete.** So the reassuring half of this instance is not evidence about the mechanism. ✅ **FIX APPLIED THIS CYCLE (and it does NOT reach for `--delete`):** enumerate the deleted paths from `git status --porcelain` — the staged/worktree deletion codes, anchored at line start: `D ` , ` D` and `AD` — and remove exactly those paths in the WSL tree before building. The shared part is only the idempotent base. That is sound engineering and the wrong call here — §A.7 lists "a flaky test" as a trigger and is explicit that thin evidence, out-of-scope-ness and "I'll remember it" are exactly the conditions that make anchoring MANDATORY rather than optional. **Thin evidence argues for a LOW priority and an honest confidence label; it never argues for silence.** An un-anchored flake is invisible to the next cycle, to the anchor guard and to the plan sweep, so it is lost by construction — and a flake in the GATE is worse than an ordinary one: it can red a clean cycle, or be waved away as "just the flake" on the run where it is real. ⓘ A separate row was proposed for this occurrence under the name `D-TEST-NATIVE-PROBE-ABI-CONFORMANCE-FLAKES-UNDER-PARALLEL-CTEST` and **deliberately NOT minted — it would have been a duplicate of this row**, same test, same symptom, same suspect family; recorded here so a future search for that phrasing lands on the right row rather than creating one. ── **WAS:** 🟠 **OPEN — normal — created 2026-08-04 (TF-C115 §A.7). A SINGLE NON-REPRODUCING FAILURE, ANCHORED RATHER THAN DISMISSED — this project's rule is explicit that `exit 8` is ctest's failed-test return and NOT a flake signature, so "it passed on the re-run" is a reason to record, never to move on.** ✔MEASURED, all four arms on the same tree: Windows `build-dbg` ctest `-j 4` run #1 → **786/787, `core/test_bitfield_abi_conformance` FAILED**; the same test ALONE → **passed, 1.56 s**; Windows `-j 4` run #2 → **787/787, rc=0**; WSL x86_64 + qemu-arm64 → **787/787, rc=0**. So: Windows-only, parallel-only, one occurrence in two runs. ⚠ **MECHANISM IS A LABELLED SUSPECT, NOT A FINDING — and I cannot strengthen it, because the evidence was destroyed (see below).** The suspect: `tests/core/native_c_probe.hpp` compiles and RUNS a probe with the real NATIVE toolchain, and **THREE separate test binaries depend on it** — `test_bitfield_abi_conformance`, `test_packed_abi_conformance`, `test_coff_object_reader` — with **NO caching of toolchain discovery anywhere** (grep for `static`/`call_once`/`cache` in that header returns nothing relevant). Each independently runs `vswhere`, then `vcvars64.bat`, then a `toolchain_check.c` compile+run, into its own `ScratchDir{Location::Temp, tag}`. Under `-j 4` up to three can do that simultaneously, and `vcvars64.bat` manipulates environment and writes under `%TEMP%`. ★ THIS IS THE SAME SHAPE AS THE ONLY PREVIOUSLY ROOT-CAUSED FLAKE IN THIS PROJECT (TF-C84: macOS Gatekeeper/syspolicyd issuing a per-code-signature scan that serialized under `-j`) — an EXTERNAL toolchain or OS service contending under parallelism, surfacing as a test failure that vanishes in isolation. TF-C84 was fixed STRUCTURALLY, never by serializing, and the same standard applies here. ⚠ NOTE the scratch-path collision class is NOT the suspect: this header already uses the collision-safe `ScratchDir` that TF-C98/C99 introduced, and it does not use `runBinary`, so TF-C114's drain fix is not implicated either — both were checked rather than assumed. **Trigger:** ⚠ **FIRED TWICE (2026-08-04 TF-C115, 2026-08-05 TF-C120) — the "never seen twice" clause below is now FALSE and is retained only as the audit trail.** RESTATED so it is unambiguous: it fires again on a THIRD occurrence, **OR on any gate run in which an ABI-conformance test fails with `NATIVE-PROBE-COMPILE-FAILED`**. Priority: normal — held there deliberately, because two occurrences in ~7 observed full runs is a real but LOW rate; ⓘ the rate is the thing to measure next, not the mechanism to guess. ── **WAS:** **Trigger:** FIRED (once). Priority: normal — it has never been seen to cost a real red twice, but an uncoordinated external-toolchain dependency in three tests is a standing hazard. | Cache the native-toolchain discovery ONCE per ctest invocation rather than once per test binary — the discovery result is a property of the HOST, not of the test, so recomputing it three times in parallel is both the hazard and pure waste. Then re-run the full Windows suite under `-j 4` repeatedly (≥10) and report the failure count, so "non-reproducing" becomes a measured rate rather than an absence of evidence. ★ AND FIX THE INSTRUMENT (below) FIRST, or the next occurrence will be just as unanalysable. | `tests/core/native_c_probe.hpp` (`:97` the status string, `:299-335` why `CompileFailed` excludes "no toolchain", `:312` the per-process `build_probe.bat`); `tests/core/test_bitfield_abi_conformance.cpp` (the ONLY binary seen to fail, twice); `tests/core/test_packed_abi_conformance.cpp` (passed CONCURRENTLY with the second failure); `tests/link/test_coff_object_reader.cpp`; `tests/test_support/scratch_dir.hpp:80`, `:107-112` (why the shared-scratch hypothesis is REFUTED); with the TF-C84 precedent, [[D-GATE-SHARED-DLL-RELINK-UNDER-RUNNING-CTEST]] and ★ [[D-BUILD-CONCURRENT-CONTENTION-YIELDS-TRUNCATED-OBJECT-TREATED-AS-CURRENT]] — **the same family: this repository has now been bitten twice by parallelism damaging a shared artefact, which is an argument that the CLASS is real even where this INSTANCE's evidence is thin** |
| `D-GATE-CTEST-OUTPUT-TRIMMED-DESTROYS-ONE-SHOT-FAILURE-EVIDENCE` | P3 | 🟠 OPEN | 🟠 **OPEN — normal — created 2026-08-04 (TF-C115 §A.7). AN OPERATOR-SIDE DEFECT, recorded against myself because it cost a real diagnosis THE SAME HOUR IT WAS COMMITTED.** The Windows gate command pipes ctest through `Select-Object -Last 30` (and the WSL sibling through `tail -25`). `--output-on-failure` prints the failing test's captured output BEFORE the summary block, so a trailing-N trim keeps the summary and **discards exactly the part that explains the failure**. ✔MEASURED 2026-08-04: `core/test_bitfield_abi_conformance` failed once under `-j 4`; the assertion text, the expected-vs-actual layout, and the native-toolchain output were all trimmed away, leaving only the name. Trimming for context economy is the right instinct applied at the wrong layer — the fix is to keep everything on disk and trim only what is READ. **Trigger:** FIRED. Priority: normal. | Always `Tee-Object` / `tee` the FULL ctest output to a file and trim only the console view — then grep the file for the failure block. Cheap, and it converts "the failure was unanalysable" into a class that cannot recur. | scratchpad `wsl_gate.sh`, `clang_asan_leg.sh`, and the Windows gate invocation; with [[D-TEST-NATIVE-PROBE-TOOLCHAIN-ACQUISITION-UNCOORDINATED]], [[feedback_verify_before_asserting_2026_07_26]] |

The CLI was hardened for this on 2026-08-05 (`--prereq-mode link-line --always-make 1 --token-scope recipe`); THIS call site was left on `whole-blob`/`all`, whose comment asserted "`make -n testfixture` runs with every prerequisite already built" and "there is no bootstrap in this recipe" — two unasserted assumptions about the state of `$BLD`, both FALSE on a cold tree. `make -n` prints WHAT REMAINS TO BE DONE, so the build directory is an INPUT to the derivation, and it was an unexamined one. ⚠ **THE FLOOR COMMENT DOCUMENTED THE BROKEN NUMBER:** it read "the fixture is ~192 TUs" citing a ✔MEASURED 2026-08-05 derive — that measurement was itself taken on a CONTAMINATED tree, so the poisoned count had been the written-down normal ever since. ⚠⚠ **TWO SUSPECTS WERE EXONERATED BY EXPERIMENT, and neither story survived contact:** (i) the reference `make sqlite3d` regenerating derived sources — refuted, running it did NOT reintroduce the tool lines; (ii) a `configure` re-run — refuted, NEITHER run took that path. The arm64 VPS, same driver, same commit, same upstream, derived CLEANLY. So the state that produced it is real, reproducible in its effect, and UNEXPLAINED in its origin. **Trigger:** FIRED. Priority: normal (fixed; the residual is the unexplained trigger). | ✅ DONE: (a) both drivers' fixture call sites moved to `--prereq-mode link-line --always-make 1 --token-scope recipe` — `-B` makes the derive DETERMINISTIC and the link-line span excludes the bootstrap STRUCTURALLY (probed: the `-o testfixture` line carries 139 unique `.c` and ZERO `tool/` sources, while `make -B -n` prints FOUR tool sources into the blob — lemon, lempar, mkkeywordhash, mksourceid — so `whole-blob` was a latent trap that fires on any cold tree, not a one-off); (b) a CAUSE-AGNOSTIC belt in the shared core `dss_bh_emit_recipe` refuses ANY harvested TU under `tool/` or named `lempar.c`, for every caller and every mode, ordered with the other NAMED causes above the floors. Belt AND braces precisely BECAUSE the trigger is unknown. Pinned by 3 selftest assertions (2 negative + 1 positive control, 41 passed / 0 failed), red-on-disable demonstrated FAIL-CLOSED: witness unique, mutant differs by `cmp` at IDENTICAL line count 954, witness absent, `bash -n` clean, both guard assertions RED and the control GREEN. ⏳ RESIDUAL: if a cold-tree derive ever produces this shape again ON the hardened path, that is NEW information and this row is where it goes. ⏳ Also sweep whether any OTHER `dss_bh_emit_recipe` caller is still on `whole-blob`. | `real-examples/c/sqlite/base-harness.sh` (the guard + selftest 7b); `build-and-test.sh` and `build-and-test.ps1` (the fixture call sites); with [[D-HARNESS-SQLITE-STAGED-TREE-MIXED-VINTAGE]], [[D-HARNESS-SQLITE-DERIVED-SOURCES-NOT-MADE-BY-THE-DRIVER]] (same class: upstream pulled every run while `bld-dss` is reused), [[D-GATE-SQLITE-CORPUS-DENOMINATOR-UNPINNED]] |

Every one of the 14 assertions failed with `rc=3221225781` = `0xC0000135` = **STATUS_DLL_NOT_FOUND**, and the gate — correctly, by its own rules — reported `FAIL (CHARGED TO DSS) — 0/14, 14 charged to DSS`. With the transport byte-identity already proven (`ceea405f…`, 5,799,936 bytes both ends), that reads exactly like a cross-host emission defect. ★ IT WAS KILLED BY DIFFING THE IMPORT TABLES: the Mac-built and Windows-built binaries import the IDENTICAL set (`kernel32.dll`, `msvcrt.dll`, `ucrtbase.dll`, `zlib.dll`). The artifact was always fine; **the probe transported the executable without the leg's DECLARED runtime libraries**, which the harness stages beside the CLI (`zlib.dll`, `tcl86t.dll`) and a bare scratch directory does not have. Re-run with them carried: **14/14 PASS**. ★ **PRECISION THAT MATTERS FOR THE FIX, and it corrects the first draft of this row (operator error, caught in review):** of the four imports only **`zlib.dll` is third-party**. `kernel32.dll` / `msvcrt.dll` / `ucrtbase.dll` are OS libraries the Windows loader resolves from the system search path — they must **NOT** be copied, and copying them is worse than useless: a stale local copy shadowing the system one is its own defect class. The probe also carried `tcl86t.dll`, which the CLI does not import at all (it is the TESTFIXTURE's dependency) — harmless, unjustified, and a sign the transport was assembled by hand rather than from a declaration. ⇒ **the closure is per-ARTIFACT and non-system only**, and the harness ALREADY computes it: the manifest lines print `cli manifest: … resolveLibraries=1` (zlib) versus `manifest: … resolveLibraries=2` (zlib + tcl). Nothing needs deriving — only exposing. ⚠⚠ **THE GENERALISATION IS THE DANGEROUS PART, AND IT IS NOT HYPOTHETICAL:** the earlier `VPS builds elf64-x86_64, run it on WSL` cell passed 14/14 from the same one-file probe — but only because THAT leg's `libtcl8.6`/`libz` happened to resolve from the destination's system paths. On a host without them it would have failed identically, and there would have been no reason to suspect the probe rather than the compiler. ⇒ **a round-trip result from a one-file probe is only as trustworthy as the destination's accidental library set.** **Trigger:** FIRED. Priority: HIGH. | Expose the closure the harness ALREADY has — a `--runtime-closure LABEL --artifact {cli,fixture}` mode reading the same `resolveLibraries` set the manifest prints — so a transport carries it BY CONSTRUCTION instead of by the prober remembering. ⚠ It must emit the NON-SYSTEM set only: the OS libraries are resolved by the target loader and shipping them is a shadowing hazard, so "everything in the import table" is the WRONG answer even though it is the easy one. Then every round-trip probe consumes it, and the answer is declared in ONE place rather than re-derived per probe. Pin it by transporting an artifact to a directory with NO libraries present and asserting the closure list is non-empty and sufficient (red-on-disable: drop the closure and the smoke goes 0/14 with `0xC0000135`, which is a genuinely strong negative). ⚠ **NAMED BLOCKER, and it is why this is anchored rather than fixed on the spot per §A.7(c). ⓘ THE BLOCKER CHANGED IDENTITY 2026-08-07 and is restated rather than left stale:** it was "a delegated agent owns `harness_legs.py`" — that agent has landed. It is now "**a live Windows harness run reads `harness_legs.py` on every leg decision**", so editing it mid-run would make that run's leg plan inconsistent with its own start. Same file, different holder, still real. Close it when the in-flight host runs finish — not next cycle. ⚠ Restating a blocker instead of quietly inheriting the old wording matters: a stale blocker is how an anchor keeps its OPEN status long after the thing that justified it went away. | `real-examples/c/sqlite/harness_legs.py` (the new mode); the round-trip probes in the session scratchpad; with [[D-HARNESS-PE64-HAS-NO-SAME-PLATFORM-ORACLE]], [[D-GATE-SCRIPTS-LIVE-ONLY-IN-THE-SCRATCHPAD]] |

Nor were "8-only" markers used, because no reachable machine has a Tcl 9 to measure them against, and a wrong 8-only set would turn the real 9.0-vs-8.6 case into a silent "cannot determine". ⇒ classification rests only on the arm with hard evidence. ✘ **STILL OPEN — the DERIVATION half**, for a MEASURED reason and not for effort: in BOTH drivers the header is selected BEFORE per-leg library resolution (`.sh` same step, header first — and `tcl_lib_names()` actually derives the `host-system` candidate names FROM the chosen `$TCL_VER`, a real dependency in that direction; `.ps1` stages the header inside the `$deriveScript` heredoc that runs **in WSL at Step 3+4**, two steps and one machine boundary earlier). Deriving would require materialising the libraries ahead of that staging in both drivers; doing it in the `.sh` alone would recreate the one-driver-capability defect class. ⇒ deferred with the reason recorded, not silently narrowed. Originally: 🔴 **OPEN — HIGH — created 2026-08-06 (TF-C122). Controlled before the run: same compiler binary, same staged sqlite tree, same recipe, same leg spec; **the only changed variable is the host OS.** ★ ROOT CAUSE: the harness selects the Tcl **HEADER** from the **HOST** (`tclsh` on PATH → its `tclConfig.sh` → `TCL_INCLUDE_SPEC`, `build-and-test.sh:2100-2151`), while **every leg's LIBRARY is pinned at 8.6 by its provider**. ✔MEASURED on the Mac: `arm64libs/libtcl8.6.so`, `harness-libs/macho64-arm64/libtcl8.6.dylib`, `harness-libs/macho64-x86_64/libtcl8.6.dylib` — `nm -g` finds **ZERO** of the 9.0-only names in all four — while this host's Homebrew `tcl-tk` is **9.0.3** (`tcl-tk@8` = 8.6.18 is also present, unused). sqlite's `tclsqlite.c` gates live code on `TCL_MAJOR_VERSION>8`, so a 9.0 header makes the fixture **REFERENCE** symbols an 8.6 library cannot supply. ★★ **THE ASSUMPTION IS WRITTEN DOWN AND IT IS HALF TRUE, WHICH IS WHY IT SURVIVED:** `build-and-test.sh:2078` states *"Headers are leg-INDEPENDENT: DSS parses the host tcl/zlib headers agnostically (ABI is irrelevant at parse)"*. True of **ABI**; false of **API SURFACE** — the header version selects WHICH SYMBOLS ARE REFERENCED, which is a **link-time, per-leg** fact, not a parse-time one. ★★ **THE HARNESS HAS THREE Tcl COHERENCE CHECKS AND ALL THREE ARE HOST-SCOPED:** interpreter-vs-staging, header-vs-tclConfig, recipe-vs-staging. ⚠ *Cited by their own diagnostic strings, NOT by line number: the numbers in this row's first draft (`:2126`/`:2165`/`:2180`) were **already stale by ~16 lines at the moment they were written** — which is exactly why the new in-code comment cites diagnostics instead.* **Not one compares the staged header against the library that leg will actually LINK.** On Linux the host `tclsh` is 8.6, so header and library agreed **BY ACCIDENT OF THE HOST** — which is why many green Linux runs never saw it, and why the first non-Linux host found it immediately. ⚠ **THE FACT WAS ALREADY IN THIS REGISTRY AND WAS STILL WALKED INTO:** [[D-HARNESS-MACHO-IMPORT-NAME-IS-LOADER-PATH-NOT-A-SYSTEM-PATH]] records *"this Mac's Homebrew Tcl is 9.0, which cannot satisfy the fixture's 8.6 symbols either"* — recorded about the **LIBRARY**. Nobody carried it across to **HEADER** selection. ⇒ a recorded fact filed under the wrong mechanism is not a guard. **Trigger:** FIRED. Priority: HIGH. | ✅ **DONE (TF-C122)** — the missing FOURTH, PER-LEG check now exists in both drivers as one shared `harness_legs.py --tcl-coherence` verb, with three red-on-disable breaks demonstrated and restored (neutered comparison → 5 named checks red; name-instead-of-bytes → the `libtcl9.0.so`-that-is-really-8.6 test reds; deleted `.ps1` invocation → the both-drivers test reds). ✘ **STILL TO DO — the derivation**, per the measured ordering constraint recorded above. ═══ ORIGINAL CLOSING WORK: Add the **missing FOURTH check, and make it PER-LEG**: compare the staged header's Tcl version against the version of the `libtcl` **each leg resolves**, and fail loud naming both — instead of leaving the compiler to emit N undefined-symbol errors that a human must reverse-engineer back to a version skew. THE FIFTH VACUITY SPECIES IN THREE CYCLES — and the first that is NOT a side effect of a newly-added rule. These tests were BORN vacuous and stayed that way.** THE SHAPE: a negative-load test asserts *"this fixture is rejected"*. The rejection is REAL, so the test is green and its assertion is not weak in form. But the fixture also omits a field required EARLIER in the pipeline, so the loader rejects it **before the rule the test is NAMED FOR ever executes**. ⇒ the named rule has never been exercised, on any run, since the test was written — **deleting that rule outright would not have reddened it.** ✔MEASURED in `tests/link/test_object_format_schema.cpp`, 5 instances, ALL PREDATING this cycle: `DuplicateRelocationNameRejected`, `ZeroKindRejected`, `DuplicateRelocationKindRejected` — every relocation row omitted `nativeId`, a format-only REQUIRED field, so the loader **DROPPED EVERY ROW** before the duplicate-name / zero-kind / duplicate-kind checks could see one (three tests, three distinct rules, zero coverage); plus `LibrarySynthesis.UnknownVehicleRejected` and `LibrarySynthesis.MissingLibraryPathRejected` — missing `dataModel` + `headerNameMatching`, both unconditionally required. ★ **WHY IT SURVIVES REVIEW:** the test is green; the assertion looks reasonable (`EXPECT_FALSE(load(…))` over a genuinely invalid fixture); and the defect is an **ABSENCE** — absences do not show up in a diff or a code read, and the fixture still *looks* like it exercises the rule. Only attributing the diagnostic to a JSON POINTER makes the substitution visible. ★★ **THE GENERAL LAW, worth more than the five fixes: "it was rejected" is NOT evidence that it was rejected FOR THE STATED REASON.** A negative test must pin WHICH rule fired — path, code, or message — never merely THAT something failed. (b) ★ **THE DURABLE FIX IS A HABIT, NOT A SWEEP:** make the attributed form the path of least resistance so the bare form stops being written — a shared helper per loader family, as was done for object-format loads. (2) When the out-of-tree regression guard lands ([[D-TEST-HELPERS-IGNORE-DSS-CONFIG-ROOT-OUT-OF-TREE]]), make it FAIL LOUD on this specifically — a preflight that computes the longest object path it will need and refuses with a message naming MAX_PATH beats discovering it as a phantom compile error 233 steps in. | `D-HARNESS-WSL-DETACHED-CHILD-DIES-AND-ITS-WATCHER-CANNOT-TELL` | P3 | 🟠 OPEN | 🔴 **OPEN — HIGH — created 2026-08-05 (TF-C119 §A.7), OPERATOR-AUTHORED from a live incident. TWO defects, and the second is the one that would have cost the day: a `nohup … &` inside `wsl.exe` runs NOTHING, and the watcher armed against it could not distinguish "never started" from "still working".** ✔MEASURED 2026-08-05. To keep a long sqlite corpus run out of a harness-side timeout the run was detached — `wsl.exe -e bash launcher.sh`, the launcher doing `nohup bash -c "bash units.sh > $OUT 2>&1; echo $? > $DONE" >/dev/null 2>&1 &`. It printed a plausible `LAUNCHED pid=751669` **and then nothing ran at all**: neither `$OUT` nor `$DONE` was ever created, and no `build-and-test` process existed seconds later. **The children do not outlive the launching `wsl.exe`.** ★★ **THE COMPOUNDING ERROR, which is the reusable half:** the watcher was `until [ -f "$DONE" ]; do sleep 10; done`. **A marker that never appears is INDISTINGUISHABLE from a job still working** — that watch would have burned its entire timeout reporting nothing, and "still running" would have been believed the whole way. ⇒ **A WATCHER MUST BE ABLE TO OBSERVE THE FAILURE IT IS WAITING THROUGH, not only the success.** ⓘ The direct form (`wsl.exe bash units.sh` as a tracked background job) works precisely because the harness holds `wsl.exe` open for the job's lifetime — the fix is not a better `nohup`, it is not detaching. ★ This is the **THIRD** distinct `wsl.exe` invocation defect beside the two already written down (the Git-Bash `$`-mangling that once became `rsync -a --delete / /`, and the PowerShell missing-`-e` default-shell hop that expands `$( )` locally). The class: **`wsl.exe` is a process boundary with its own lifetime AND its own shell, and every rule about it so far has been learned by losing a run.** **Trigger:** FIRED. Priority: HIGH. ★★ **SECOND MEASURED OCCURRENCE 2026-08-13, AND IT INVERTS THE RULE THIS ROW WROTE.** ✔MEASURED just after `e42ae5a5`: the WSL gate lane’s build watcher was `until grep -qE "^BUILD OK\|BUILD FAILED\|CONFIGURE FAILED" <log>; do sleep 5; done`, and it span until killed **over a build that had SUCCEEDED**. Its producer `tee -a`’d both FAILURE arms into the log but wrote the success line as a bare `echo "BUILD OK"` — **stdout only**. ✔`grep -c "BUILD OK" <log>` = **0**; the log’s last two lines are `[661/661] Linking CXX executable …` and `build rc=0`. ⇒ this row’s remedy (2) — *“never arm a watcher whose only observable is the SUCCESS marker”* — was **ONE-SIDED**: here SUCCESS was the unobservable state and the FAILURE arms were fine, and the watcher was exactly as blind. The general rule is **every terminal state a watcher waits through must be observable ON THE POLLED CHANNEL**, and the channel is a property of the WRITER, not of the script’s intent — `echo X` and `echo X \| tee -a "$LOG"` read identically at a glance and differ completely to a log-polling grep. ⓘ **No verdict moved**: the build was independently established (rc=0, `[661/661]`, binaries on disk, then 851 tests actually ran) BEFORE the watcher was killed — a watcher that cannot fire cannot manufacture a green, only fail to announce one. ⚠ **AND THE READY-MADE FIX WOULD ITSELF HAVE FAILED AS PRESCRIBED:** the lane’s post-mortem proposed `scripts/run-gate/run-gate.sh <log> ‘BUILD OK’ ninja …`, which `run-gate.sh` **correctly refuses** — `ninja` never prints "BUILD OK", and a caller-authored success string is precisely the evidence-free exit that tool exists to reject. The witness must be TOOL-EMITTED. | (1) Write the LIFETIME rule down beside the existing `-e` / `bash -c` rules in the WSL invocation notes — *to run something long in WSL, keep the `wsl.exe` process alive for its duration; a detached child is reaped* — and at every driver site that launches WSL work. (2) SEPARATELY, and this is the durable item: **never arm a watcher whose only observable is the SUCCESS marker.** A wait loop must also poll a LIVENESS signal (the PID it claims to have launched, or growth of `$OUT`) and fail loud the instant liveness is gone with the marker absent; otherwise a timeout is the only verdict it can ever render and it cannot say why. (3) Red-on-disable, cheap and exact: kill the child immediately after launch — the watcher must red within ONE poll, not at timeout. (4) ✔ADDED 2026-08-13 from the second occurrence — restate (2) **SYMMETRICALLY**, and mechanically rather than by care: a wait loop must be armed only against tokens **proven present on the channel it polls**, for EVERY terminal state, not just the one the author was worried about. Cheapest durable form: stop hand-authoring markers and poll a TOOL-EMITTED line. Route long gate builds through `scripts/run-gate/run-gate.sh` with a witness the tool actually produces — `‘ninja: no work to do\|^\[[0-9]+/[0-9]+\]’` for a ninja build, `‘100% tests passed’` for ctest — never a self-authored `BUILD OK`. | the incident's launcher + watcher are scratchpad-resident, which is itself [[D-GATE-SCRIPTS-LIVE-ONLY-IN-THE-SCRATCHPAD]]; with [[D-HARNESS-WSL-LAUNCHED-LEG-RUNDIR-IS-DRVFS]] (the other live `wsl.exe`-boundary row), [[D-GATE-CTEST-OUTPUT-TRIMMED-DESTROYS-ONE-SHOT-FAILURE-EVIDENCE]] (an instrument that discards the evidence of the run it is watching — same family, different loss); and [[D-GATE-EXIT-CODE-WITHOUT-EVIDENCE-OF-WORK]] — the tool built for this exact class, which the 2026-08-13 occurrence bypassed by hand-rolling a script (itself [[D-GATE-SCRIPTS-LIVE-ONLY-IN-THE-SCRATCHPAD]] again) |

The failure mode is not a bad build — it is that **every measurement taken on that box is attributable to the wrong commit**, and this project's whole corpus-count discipline rests on quoting a number WITH its commit. A reader who trusts the banner attributes a green run to `a3af1320`; a reader who distrusts it has no commit at all. **Trigger:** FIRED. | `D-CYCLE-CANNOT-ASSUME-IT-OWNS-THE-WORKING-TREE` | P3 | 🟠 OPEN | 🟠 **OPEN — normal — created 2026-08-05 (TF-C120), from a live incident in this cycle. A PROCESS defect whose worst outcome is a commit that contains someone else's unfinished work.** ✔MEASURED: `git status` was CLEAN at session start; mid-cycle `.github/FUNDING.yml` went dirty and `.temp/funding-sponsorship-and-consulting-plan.md` appeared at 14:07. Neither cycle agent had been briefed on funding — **another session was working in this same tree concurrently**, and it later committed that work itself as `37dc54f3`. No harm resulted, and that is exactly why it needs recording: the harmless outcome was luck, not design. ⛔ **THE HAZARD, stated concretely: a long cycle that runs `git add -A` at ANY point in the ~40 minutes before that commit would have swept a stranger's in-flight edit into a compiler commit** — a change nobody reviewed, attributed to a cycle that never touched it, in a repository whose whole review discipline assumes a commit's contents are its author's. It also breaks the base-commit assumption: this cycle's base moved from `d93736c9` to `37dc54f3` while it was running. ★★ **SECOND INSTANCE, 2026-08-05 (TF-C121) — AND IT IS SHARPER THAN THE FIRST, because the first was a STRANGER'S commit and this one is THE CYCLE'S OWN PARALLEL AGENTS.** ✔MEASURED during a `.plans`-only bookkeeping pass run concurrently with implementation: (a) the pass measured the working tree and found three shipped descriptors UNMODIFIED with no `linkName` key anywhere in `src/`, and recorded the remediation as unverifiable — **both statements true when taken and FALSE ninety minutes later**, because an implementing agent landed the fix mid-pass; (b) `scripts/check-anchor-registry/check-anchor-registry.sh`, which had passed at the start of the pass, went **FAIL** on `D-FFI-SHIPPED-SYMBOL-PER-TARGET-LINK-NAME` — an anchor cited in six `src/` files that **did not exist when the pass began**. ★★★ **THE FRAMING, verbatim, because it is the whole lesson: A MEASUREMENT OF A WORKING TREE IS A MEASUREMENT OF AN INSTANT.** ⇒ **PRACTICAL CONSEQUENCE: a bookkeeping or audit pass that runs concurrently with implementation MUST RE-MEASURE BEFORE IT REPORTS** — a finding taken at minute 5 and reported at minute 95 is a claim about a tree that no longer exists, and it will be read as current. ⓘ **The anchor guard is what made the drift VISIBLE**, which is an argument for running it at the END of every pass rather than only before a commit: it is the one instrument here that compares `src/` against `.plans/` at the moment it is asked. **Trigger:** FIRED (twice). Priority: normal. | (1) **STAGE BY EXPLICIT PATH, never `git add -A`/`git add .`** — a cycle knows its own file set, and any file outside it is by definition not this cycle's. (2) Consider a pre-commit check that the STAGED set equals the cycle's DECLARED file set, failing loud on any extra path; the declared set already exists in every cycle's own plan text, so this is cheap. (3) Re-read the base commit at commit time rather than trusting the one read at orient time. Red-on-disable: dirty an unrelated file mid-cycle and assert the commit refuses it. | the cycle discipline (`dss-cycle`) · with [[D-PLANS-CYCLE-COMMITTED-WITHOUT-ANCHOR-PASS]] (the sibling process row), [[D-BUILD-CONCURRENT-CONTENTION-YIELDS-TRUNCATED-OBJECT-TREATED-AS-CURRENT]] (the same "two sessions, one tree" premise, one layer down in the BUILD) |

The source then CHANGED (the audit-fix round), so run #2 was launched on the final tree. **Before committing, the operator read `wsl-gate.done` while run #2 was still in flight, saw `DONE rc=0` — run #1's marker — and treated it as run #2's verdict, then committed.** Run #2 finished afterwards: `CAPTURED=0`, 792/792, `Total Test time 222.71 s` — green, and provably a DISTINCT run (different wall-clock, marker mtime 17:06:04). ⇒ **`86e4ffc1`'s WSL leg IS green. The conclusion was right and the evidence was stale — the right answer was reached by luck, not by method**, and that distinction is the whole reason this row exists rather than being waved off by the green outcome. ★★★ **THE CLASS, and it is the INVERSE of a trap this registry already names.** [[D-HARNESS-WSL-DETACHED-CHILD-DIES-AND-ITS-WATCHER-CANNOT-TELL]] records that **a marker that never appears is indistinguishable from a job still working** — it fails OPEN-looking, and a watcher burns its timeout learning nothing. This is the mirror image and it is WORSE, because it fails **CLOSED-LOOKING: a marker from run N−1 is indistinguishable from run N's, so a re-run's verdict silently INHERITS its predecessor's.** A reader cannot tell a verified gate from an unverified one, and the failure presents as a pass. ⚠ **THE STANDING RULE THIS VIOLATED IS ALREADY WRITTEN DOWN — *"never read state a background job is writing"* — AND IT WAS VIOLATED BY THE PERSON ENFORCING IT.** Recorded unsoftened, because that is the useful part: the rule did not fail, the discipline did, and the REUSED PATH is what made the violation invisible instead of obvious (had the path been per-run, the read would simply have found nothing and the error would have been self-announcing). It was caught only because a late task-notification forced a re-read of the marker. **Trigger:** UNCONDITIONAL — it applies to every gate script that writes a fixed marker path and is ever run twice in one session, which is every long cycle. The fix is mostly WIRING AN EXISTING SIGNAL INTO THE RIGHT PLACE, not new machinery. **(b) DELETE the marker, rc and log at the START of a run**, so an in-flight run has NO marker at all and a premature read finds nothing instead of something wrong. **(c) PREFER PER-RUN PATHS OUTRIGHT** — a marker whose path encodes its run cannot be mistaken for another's, which removes the class rather than detecting it. Priority: LOW. | Pass **`encoding="utf-8", errors="replace"`** at each call — **matching the file's OWN `open()` convention**, so the fix is harmonisation with an existing decision rather than a new one. Then **sweep every `.py` tool for bare `text=True` / `universal_newlines=True`**; `cli-smoke.py:116` is already known. Cheap, and it removes a whole class rather than an instance. ⚠ Prefer `errors="replace"` over `errors="strict"` deliberately: a mojibake'd verdict is the failure mode to avoid, but a *crash* in the harness on one odd byte would trade a silent wrong answer for a dead run, and this harness's standing rule is that it must SURVIVE everything. Red-on-disable: feed a spawned tool's stdout a non-ASCII byte and assert the decoded string round-trips rather than being locale-mangled. | `real-examples/c/sqlite/harness_legs.py:583`, `:1005`, `:2143` (the bare `text=True` sites) · `:1130`, `:1192`, `:1454`, `:1788`, `:1905`, `:3922`, `:3977`, `:4011` (the explicit-UTF-8 convention they diverge from) · `real-examples/c/sqlite/cli-smoke.py:116` (the sweep's second hit) · with [[D-GATE-NON-ASCII-OUTPUT-KILLS-THE-GATE-ON-A-CP1252-CONSOLE]] (the cp1252 family, and the grep that found this) · ★ **and the INSTRUMENT family — this is another "a check inherits an environment default nobody declared"**, the same shape as `[Parser]::ParseFile` inheriting the checking shell's grammar ([[D-HARNESS-PS1-UNPARSEABLE-UNDER-WINDOWS-POWERSHELL-5-1]]) and the guard's rc inheriting `head`'s ([[D-GATE-ANCHOR-GUARD-RC-LOST-AFTER-A-PIPE]]) |

So 17 − 2 + 1 = 16. THIS ROW STAYS OPEN because the detector is not the fix — 30 measured divergences were recorded and none was repaired; **as of 2026-08-11 two are repaired and one newly found, so 29 remain (13 direction-B + 16 direction-A).** ⓘ **RE-MEASURED 2026-08-24 (P30) AND EVERY EARLIER FIGURE IN THIS ROW IS NOW HISTORY — the live numbers are 99 probes, 31 divergences, direction-A=18, direction-B=13, not-pinned=1, over a FULL 5-oracle roster** (msvc-cl 19.51 @202312 · win-gcc@Strawberry 13.2 @202000 · wsl-clang-19 @202400 · wsl-clang-18 @202311 · wsl-gcc-13 @202000). ★★ **THIS ROW WAS CARRYING THREE MUTUALLY INCONSISTENT PROBE COUNTS AT ONCE** — 84, 85, and 87 quoted from [[D-TEST-GATE-VERDICT-DEPENDS-ON-HOST-TOOLCHAIN]] — while instructing its own readers to re-measure rather than carry a number forward by hand. The instruction was right and the row did not follow it. ★ The count is independently checkable WITHOUT running anything: the corpus holds exactly one `@acknowledged-gap` line per divergence and the ratchet forces the two to be equal on a green run, so `grep -c` on the corpus is a second instrument. ⚠ The direction-A closing list below enumerates 17 rows and is therefore SHORT BY ONE: `a_nested_function_gnu` became the 18th when P30 removed a `varies` waiver that was hiding it. | **Trigger: any change to the C grammar, the predefined-macro set, or the semantic analyzer.** The harness is a RATCHET and turns one way only: a NEW divergence on a probe with no `@acknowledged-gap` line reds; CLOSING an acknowledged gap ALSO reds, naming the exact line to delete, because an acknowledgement list that keeps rows after they are fixed stops meaning anything. **Closing work, in priority order:** (1) the extern-library-realization family (4 probes) — delete the `{optional stringLiteralExpr}` from `/shapes/externDecl` and move realization to the reference-compiler spelling, a GNU asm label, which DSS already accepts (`a_asm_label_gnu` passes); (2) the grammar OVER-ADMISSION family (7 probes: elided enumerator / initializer element / struct-member declarator, `...` not last, duplicate array-param qualifiers, comma operator in a case label and in a block-scope array size) — each is a nullable-repeat or a full-`expression` slot where ISO C requires a constant-expression or a fixed position; (3) `va_start`/`va_arg`/`va_end` as reserved GRAMMAR KEYWORDS with `__builtin_va_list` injected unconditionally, so they compile with NO `<stdarg.h>`; (4) the format-gated `WINAPI`/`__stdcall`/`__forceinline` predefines that make a pe TU accept Win32 decorations with no `<windows.h>`; (5) the direction-A list, 17 rows — ~~`__typeof__` and `__alignof__` (the ISO twins `typeof`/`_Alignof` both PASS, so these are spelling holes in the form real headers use)~~ **DONE 2026-08-11, TF-C138: both were exactly the spelling holes this line predicted and both closed as pure keyword-table aliases, zero C++; the count is now 16 because closing them uncovered [[D-CSUBSET-ALIGNOF-VALUE-OPERAND]]** — statement expressions, `__builtin_expect`, `__builtin_types_compatible_p`, `__builtin_choose_expr`, `__builtin_offsetof` (which is what `<stddef.h>`'s `offsetof` EXPANDS TO on gcc and clang), `__real__`/`__imag__` (while `_Complex` itself is accepted — a complex value can be declared and not decomposed), `__label__`, case ranges, `__COUNTER__`, operand-constraint inline asm, `__attribute__((constructor))` and `__attribute__((alias))` (while packed/aligned/noreturn pass — per-ATTRIBUTE holes, not absent attribute support), `__VA_OPT__`, and `[[attr]]` on a PARAMETER (its LEADING-position twin passes). ★ EIGHT of those seventeen were found by EXERCISING the red-on-disable arms rather than reading them: one arm asserted DSS rejects `__builtin_clz`, the arm FAILED because DSS accepts it, and measuring nine replacement candidates surfaced eight real gaps. ⚠ EXPAND THE CORPUS with every future C feature: the harness only catches what it probes, and `requiredProbeIds()` — named rows, not a count — is what stops the corpus shrinking. **RED-ON-DISABLE: 11 arms EXERCISED, not read** (corpus mutated, real binary run, subject restored and re-hashed byte-identically): a new invented construct, an unacknowledged direction-A row, a stale acknowledgement, a wrong `@expect-ref`, a collapsed corpus, an unknown key, a direction-B probe declared strict, a direction-B probe narrowed to one family, a `varies` waiver with no note, a broken sanity-accept probe (every oracle then reports BROKEN), and a probe that `#include`s a header. All 11 red; baseline green. ⚠ KNOWN LIMIT, stated rather than buried: on a host with NO reference compiler the census GTEST_SKIPs, and ctest scores a skip as a pass — mitigated by the corpus-wellformedness and classifier-truth-table arms staying LIVE there, and by a guard that reds if a skip happens with an EMPTY candidate list, but not eliminated. | `tests/conformance/test_reference_conformance.cpp` · `tests/conformance/corpus/reference_conformance.probes` (every verdict, per oracle, with its `-std=`) · reuses `tests/core/native_c_probe.hpp` (`locateMsvcToolchain`, `captureCmd`, `ExecutedRows`) · [[D-TEST-NATIVE-ORACLE-INERT-ON-POSIX]] (the absent-oracle-reads-as-a-pass shape this harness is built against) · [[D-CSUBSET-EXTERN-LIBRARY-SYNTAX]] (the grammar rule behind the known instance) · `src/dss-config/sources/c.lang.json` `/shapes/externDecl`, `/shapes/enumBody`, `/shapes/braceInitList`, `/shapes/structMemberDeclarator`, `/shapes/paramList`, `/shapes/arrayDeclSuffix`, `/shapes/caseLabel` · [[D-LK-PE-OBJ-ARM-CARRIES-NO-UNWIND-INFO]] (why the `__try` row is a format-capability refusal and not a front-end gap) |
