| `D-BUILD-NINJA-RECORDS-ZERO-HEADER-DEPS-UNDER-CONCURRENT-BUILDS` | P3 | 🟠 OPEN | 🟠 **OPEN 2026-08-13 — ★★★ **THIS ATTACKS THE ONE TECHNIQUE THIS PROJECT USES TO PROVE ITS PINS ARE NOT VACUOUS.** ✔MEASURED: `ninja -t deps src/link/CMakeFiles/link.dir/format/elf.cpp.obj` reports **`#deps 0`** — ninja recorded ZERO header dependencies, so a header-only change does not rebuild its consumer. **10 of 403 objects in `build-dbg` are in that state.** Consequence, observed: a red-on-disable demonstration **reported GREEN over a live mutant, twice**, and green over a mutant reads exactly like earned confidence.  ★★★ **RE-MEASURED 2026-08-15 AND IT IS 5× WORSE THAN THIS ROW RECORDS — AND THE ROW’S OWN NAMED WITNESS WAS STILL BROKEN TWO DAYS LATER.** ✔MEASURED after a seven-lane concurrent cycle: **51 of 430** objects carried `#deps 0` (this row says 10 of 403), and `src/link/.../elf.cpp.obj` — the witness named above on 2026-08-13 — was **still in the broken state**. `.ninja_log` puts the stale objects’ previous build **41.8 hours** before the rebuild. ⚠⚠ **THE PART THAT CHANGES WHAT THIS ROW MEANS: 16 of the 51 were `src/` TUs COMPILED INTO THE SHIPPED DLL** — `linker.cpp`, `elf.cpp`, `elf_backend`, `pe_backend`, `spirv_backend`, `mir_merge`, `shipped_lib_descriptor`, `macho_codesign`, `ar`, the ELF/Mach-O object readers, `entry_trampoline`, `synth_seh_funclets`, `synth_threads_shim`, `wasm`, `optimizer_json`. ⇒ **ANY GATE RUN IN THAT BUILD DIRECTORY BEFORE THE REBUILD PROVED NOTHING ABOUT THE CURRENT SOURCE.** That is not a build-speed annoyance; it is a green ctest over a partly-stale compiler, i.e. the same class as the false-green red-on-disable mechanisms this project already tracks, arriving through the build system instead of through a test. ★ **HONEST LIMIT, stated by the lane that found it:** three of the sixteen were checked for a DIRECT include of a header changed that cycle and none had one — so what was established is that the pre-fix DLL was **UNVERIFIABLE**, not provably wrong. Rebuilding removed the doubt rather than proving a miscompile. ★ **HOW IT PRESENTED, so the next reader recognises it instead of chasing the wrong cause:** the link failed with `undefined reference` to the **OLD MANGLED NAMES** of two functions whose signatures had changed (`analyze` without its `TargetSchema const*`, `lowerToMir` without its descriptor pool). It reads exactly like *"a lane changed a signature and forgot to update callers"* — and the orchestrator diagnosed it that way and dispatched a lane to fix callers. **It was not that at all: every call site was already valid and simply let the new defaulted parameters default.** Mangling includes defaulted parameters, so a freshly-compiled caller could not have produced those names — that mismatch is the discriminator. Deleting the 51 dep-less objects and rebuilding gave rc=0 with **ZERO source edits** (`git diff` empty for all seven suspected files). ★ `cmake --build … -- -k 0` collected all six failing targets in ONE pass instead of stopping at the first, which is what made the clean rebuild an exhaustive proof rather than a hopeful one. ⚠ **14 sibling `build-lane-*` directories were live in-tree** during that cycle — exactly the concurrency this row names as the cause, and an argument that per-lane build dirs mitigate the symptom without addressing it.  ★★★ **NARROWED 2026-08-17 — THE GATE-INTEGRITY HALF IS CLOSED BY AN INSTRUMENT; THE ROOT CAUSE IS NOT, AND THE ROW STAYS OPEN FOR IT.** The harm this row actually carried was never *“ninja sometimes has to recompile”* — it was that **a gate could run over stale objects and nobody could tell**. `scripts/check-ninja-deps/check-ninja-deps.py` ends that half: it parses `ninja -t deps`, FAILS on any object recording `#deps 0`, prints the offenders and the `-k 0` rebuild command, and is wired into the cycle gate reference ahead of ctest, so a green suite can no longer be read over a partly-stale compiler without the gate saying so first. ★★ **TWO THINGS BUILDING IT MEASURED THAT CHANGE HOW THE ROW SHOULD BE READ:** (1) **`#deps 0` IS NEVER LEGITIMATE.** ✔MEASURED on a purpose-built two-target ninja project: a C file with **zero `#include` directives** compiled through a `deps = gcc` rule records **`#deps 1`** — gcc lists the source itself. So a zero count is ALWAYS a lost record, never “a header-free TU”, and the tool’s allowlist is empty **by proof rather than by not having found an exception yet**. Anyone tempted to allowlist an entry is asserting something the measurement says cannot happen. (2) ★ **AN HONEST LIMIT OF THE INSTRUMENT, STATED RATHER THAN DISCOVERED LATER:** `ninja -t deps` prints only objects that HAVE a record, so an object with NO record is invisible to the check rather than flagged. That is not the guarded failure mode (a record-less object does not exist yet, so it gets built), but `OK` means *“every object ninja knows about carries deps”*, not *“every object was verified”* — which is why the tool prints the TOTAL: a sudden drop in the object count is the signal that something else went wrong. ✔**RED-ON-DISABLE, END-TO-END rather than by self-test alone:** a real ninja project whose rule writes a prerequisite-less depfile produced `a.o: #deps 0, deps mtime … (VALID)` beside a healthy `b.o: #deps 2 … (VALID)`; the tool reported `FAIL … 1 of 2 objects` and exited 1 while the healthy sibling passed. Seven parser self-tests (`--self-test`) pin the verdict rule separately — including that **`(VALID)` does NOT rescue a zero count**, which is the exact misreading that would make the whole check vacuous. ✔ `build-dbg` measured CLEAN at the time of writing: **430 objects, 0 dep-less**. ⚠ **WHAT REMAINS OPEN, precisely:** *why* ninja loses these records is still unexplained. The leading suspect is a ninja process KILLED mid-write leaving a truncated `.ninja_deps` — which fits the history (lanes have been killed, and a worktree was once deleted under a running gate) — but that is a **LABELLED SUSPECT, NOT A MEASUREMENT**, and it has not been reproduced on demand. Until it is, detection is a guard rather than a cure. **Closing work:** reproduce the loss deliberately (kill a ninja mid-link and re-inspect `-t deps`), and if the suspect holds, either fix the write discipline or make the gate’s repair automatic and loud. | Trigger: **FIRED**, and it fires more the more lanes build concurrently. ⚠ Compounding it, the same lane’s grep-based build check reported “BUILD OK” over a link that had actually FAILED — two independent instruments agreeing on a false green. **Closing work:** make the gate assert the subject binary’s **mtime moved** (the criterion that lane switched to and which worked), and add a `ninja -t deps` sanity check so a `#deps 0` object is a loud gate failure rather than a silent one. ⚠ Related but NOT the same mechanism: [[D-BUILD-CONCURRENT-CMAKE-REGENERATE-IN-SHARED-BUILD-DIR]] and [[D-TEST-NATIVE-PROBE-COMPILE-FAILS-UNDER-CONCURRENT-LOAD]] — three distinct shared-build-dir defects now, which is itself the argument for per-lane build directories. | `build-dbg/` · [[D-GATE-RED-ON-DISABLE-MUTATION-CAN-SILENTLY-NO-OP]] |

| `D-CONF-CORPUS-NO-DIRECTION-FOR-A-C23-REMOVED-CONSTRUCT` | P3 | 🟠 OPEN | 🔴 **OPEN 2026-08-24 (P30 lane O, found while REPAIRING the corpus rather than while using it).** The differential corpus has exactly TWO directions — **A** (*a reference accepts, DSS rejects*) and **B** (*DSS accepts, NO reference accepts*) — and neither can express a **THIRD**: *DSS accepts and a reference AT THE DECLARED STANDARD LEVEL rejects*, i.e. a construct C23 REMOVED. ✔MEASURED 2026-08-24 that the class is real and LEVEL-DEPENDENT rather than a thought experiment: the K&R identifier-list DEFINITION `int f(a) int a; { return a; }` is ACCEPTED by gcc-13, clang-18 and clang-19 at `-std=gnu17` and REJECTED by clang-18/19 at C23 level, **while gcc 13.3 at `-std=gnu2x` (measured 202000L) still accepts it** — so the verdict is a function of the LEVEL, exactly the axis `@min-stdc` exists for; and a `()` declaration CALLED WITH ARGUMENTS is accepted at gnu17 by all three and rejected at C2x-and-later by all three. ✔TWO CANDIDATES DISCHARGED BY THE SAME MEASUREMENT so they are not re-tried: trigraphs are rejected at `gnu17` too (GNU mode disables them, so they carry NO level signal) and `_Noreturn` is accepted at every level (deprecated, not removed) — ⚠ which corrects the orchestrator's brief, that named THREE removed constructs where only TWO are measurable here. ★ **THE ANSWER IS CLEAN TODAY AND NOTHING MEASURES IT:** DSS rejects both (`error[P0009]` and `error[S0005]`) and already applies C23's `()` ≡ `(void)` — which is why [[D-CSUBSET-INCOMPATIBLE-REDECL-DIAGNOSED-AT-CALL-SITE-NOT-DECLARATION]] exists at all: that level-dependent behaviour was found BY HAND during UCRT-P4, not by this oracle. | **Closing work:** give a probe a DECLARED LEVEL of its own — the natural spelling is a `@max-stdc <n>` companion to `@min-stdc`, judged by oracles measuring *below* n, so the two keys BRACKET a probe to a level band — plus a third `Direction`/`Outcome` arm, seeded with the two measured constructs. ⚠ **REUSE `@min-stdc`'s mechanism, do not reinvent it**: the per-probe standard axis already exists and is already MEASURED per oracle rather than declared. ⚠⚠ **AND THE OBVIOUS WORKAROUND IS THE TRAP.** Filing a removed construct as `@direction B` with `@min-stdc 202311` would go GREEN — but direction B is documented as *"a union over ALL families"*, and under that gate the union covers the C23-level SUBSET only, so the row would silently redefine what every OTHER direction-B row asserts. Filing it `@direction A` with `@min-stdc 0` is worse: gcc at 202000 accepts, so if DSS also accepted, `pin()` returns **Agreement** and the corpus would CERTIFY a C23-removed construct as conformant. Do NOT close this by declaring *"DSS is C23, so removed constructs are simply direction B"* — that converts a MISSING direction into a WRONG one. **Red-on-disable:** a probe for the K&R definition must be RED if DSS starts accepting it while any C23-level oracle rejects it, and must stay GREEN when a pre-C23 oracle accepts it — an arm that cannot tell those two apart has not implemented the direction. | **Trigger: FIRES the moment a second C language document declaring a lower level (`c11`/`c17`) exists** — planned as plan-23 FC20 — because DSS today has ONE C document and *"at which level?"* has no answer to test against. Until then this is a real but unfireable hole. `tests/conformance/corpus/reference_conformance.probes` (`@min-stdc`, `@direction`, `@expect-ref`) · `tests/conformance/test_reference_conformance.cpp` (`pin()`, `corroborate()`) · [[D-CONF-REFERENCE-DIFFERENTIAL-ORACLE]] (parent) |

A GOVERNANCE CHECK RENDERS A VERDICT AND NOTHING CONSULTS IT.** ✔MEASURED: PR #52 merged 2026-08-14T01:57:31Z as `d4c2836` while `gh pr checks 52` reported **`DCO  fail`** and **`Every commit is signed off  fail`**. So the workflow is not configured as a REQUIRED status check, and a PR merges over its red. ★ **THE GAP IS BETWEEN THE WORKFLOW AND ITS OWN STATED CONTRACT, WHICH IS WHY THIS IS A DEFECT AND NOT A PREFERENCE.** `.github/workflows/dco.yml`'s header argues, in writing, that it is a SEPARATE workflow rather than a job in `pipeline-pr.yml` precisely because *“sign-off is not optional and must not be skippable by omitting a label”* — the design went out of its way to make the gate unskippable, and the repository setting then made it skippable by a route the workflow cannot see. It is hand-rolled rather than a third-party action for the same reason (a governance gate should not take a supply-chain dependency), so the care is real; only the enforcement is missing. ⚠ **THE COST IS TRAINED BLINDNESS, NOT THIS ONE MERGE.** A check that is red on every PR and blocks none teaches every future reader to scroll past it — including the day it reddens for a genuinely unsigned outside contribution, which is the only case the DCO exists for. Same class as a warning nobody reads. ⓘ Also ✔MEASURED: the workflow triggers on `pull_request` only, so **nothing examines what lands on `main`** — `d4c2836` carries no `Signed-off-by` although its parent `f3057f4` does. **NAMED BLOCKER — why this is OPEN rather than fixed in the cycle that found it:** closing it needs repository ADMIN settings (branch-protection required-checks), which a cycle cannot reach, and the choice between enforcing and retiring the certificate is a governance decision with legal content — not a code fix an agent may make. **TWO ways to close, both one operator action:** **(a) ENFORCE** — mark `Every commit is signed off` a required check on `main` and adopt `git commit -s` (the attestation stays the human's; nothing here proposes automating it); or **(b) RETIRE** — delete the workflow and the `CONTRIBUTING.md` claim, if the DCO is not in fact wanted. Doing neither is the third option and is the status quo this row exists to make visible rather than let it read as an oversight. **TRIGGER:** the operator picks (a) or (b). | (open) | .github/workflows/dco.yml · CONTRIBUTING.md · DCO · PR #52 merged `d4c2836` over a red DCO · predecessor [[D-CI-DCO-CHECK-RED-ON-EVERY-COMMIT-OF-THIS-BRANCH]]. |

| `D-SYNC-RSYNC-PRESERVED-MTIME-DEFEATS-THE-REBUILD` | P3 | 🟠 OPEN | 🟠 **OPEN — created 2026-08-17. `rsync -a` PRESERVES MTIMES, so a Windows→remote sync of a CHANGED source can leave `ninja` correctly reporting `no work to do` — and the “post-fix” run then silently measures the OLD binary.** ✔MEASURED on the arm64 VPS today: after the fix was synced, the subject binary's sha256 was **byte-identical** to the pre-fix one. The build system was not wrong; the inputs genuinely did not look newer. ★★ **THIS IS THE THIRD INSTRUMENT IN ONE CYCLE THAT DEFEATS A REBUILD BY PRESERVING A TIMESTAMP** — `shutil.copy2` (a restore that measured the mutant), `cp -p`, and now `rsync -a`. Different tools, one failure: **anything that preserves mtime silently converts “I changed it” into “I measured the old one”.** ⇒ the durable rule is not *“avoid `-a`”*, it is **a cross-host claim must show the SUBJECT BINARY'S HASH CHANGING** — the only check that does not care which tool ate the timestamp. ⚠ It was caught only by an explicit binary-hash comparison. Nothing in the repo would have caught it: **there is no shared sync tool in `scripts/`**, so every lane re-invents the sync and re-discovers this. ⚠⚠ **AND A FOURTH VARIANT OF THE SAME TRAP LANDED THE SAME DAY, THIS ONE DESTRUCTIVE RATHER THAN MERELY MISLEADING:** a restore helper opened the destination `open(dst, 'w')` BEFORE reading its snapshot, and a Git-Bash-vs-Windows `/tmp` path mismatch made the read fail — **truncating `runtime/platform/src/dirent.c` to ZERO BYTES**. The three earlier variants (`shutil.copy2`, `cp -p`, `rsync -a`) silently measure the OLD file; this one silently DESTROYS the new one. ⇒ the rule generalizes: **a restore must not open the destination for writing until it holds the bytes it intends to write**, and the same subject-hash check that catches the mtime variants catches this one too. The real question is a DATA-FLOW one — *does this subscript reach a set that discovery built?* ⇒ a mechanical sweep would produce a wall, not a gate. Every path the verb then computes (`abs="$repo/.worktrees/$name"`, the scratchpad gate's `pad`, the `rm`/`git worktree remove` targets) is rooted at whichever repository the caller happened to be standing in. ⓘ Disclosed rather than repaired in P51 on a stated ground I still accept: changing which tree that script calls its own changes the guard's SUBJECT, and ctest pins the CWD through `WORKING_DIRECTORY`, so the entry is sound as CI and the local gate run it. ⚠⚠ **WHAT P52 ADDS IS THAT IT IS NO LONGER THEORETICAL — IT SILENTLY REDIRECTED A WHOLE GUARD RUN AT ANOTHER REPOSITORY.** ✔MEASURED 2026-09-02: a POSIX probe of `test-lane-worktree.sh` was launched from the DRIVER's checkout while the test seeds its fixtures relative to its OWN location in the WSL leg clone. The test therefore created `.worktrees/padtest*/scratchpad/...` in the LEG repo while `lane-worktree.sh` looked for them in the DRIVER repo, and **five of nine assertions reported that the scratchpad gate had not fired** — a gate correctly reporting that the directory it was pointed at held nothing, about a tree nobody meant to ask about. ★ **THE ONLY REASON NOTHING WAS DESTROYED IS THAT THE LANE NAMES IT INVENTED DID NOT EXIST IN THE TREE IT WAS POINTED AT**, so the removals were no-ops; the driver tree was verified intact afterwards (1 worktree, no `padtest*` residue). A caller standing in a repo that DOES hold a lane of that name would have had it removed. ⇒ The failure direction is not "refuses"; it is "acts on the wrong tree and reports success", which is the class this project treats as worst. | **Closing predicate:** `_repo_root()` resolves the tree the SCRIPT LIVES IN, through the existing per-language owner — `leg_tree_driver_identity` in `scripts/leg-tree/leg-tree.sh` for `.sh`, `Get-RepoTreeIdentity` in `scripts/repo-tree/repo-tree.ps1` for `.ps1` — and **NOT a third resolver**, which is the whole reason those two exist. ★ **THE DESIGN QUESTION MUST BE ANSWERED IN THE ROW, NOT ASSUMED:** `lane-worktree` legitimately operates on the MAIN checkout, so "which tree is mine" has a genuinely different correct answer here than it does for a guard reporting on the tree under test — state it explicitly rather than porting the guard's answer across. ⓘ Consider whether an explicit `--repo <path>` should exist for a caller that means another tree, so the default becomes script-relative without removing the capability. **Evidence required to close:** a pin that drives the verb from a DIFFERENT checkout's cwd and asserts it still answers about its own tree (today that arm would pass while acting on the wrong repository, so it must be able to go red); the existing nine assertions still green on Windows AND on a POSIX host; and a REMOVE-direction mutant restoring the bare `rev-parse` that reds the new arm by name. ⚠ **A pin that only runs under ctest cannot see this defect at all**, because `WORKING_DIRECTORY` pins the cwd to the repo — the arm must set a different cwd deliberately, which is exactly what the P52 probe did by accident. | ✔Disclosed 2026-09-01 by lane `gw` (P51) as row 4 of its bash-candidate matrix, deliberately not repaired, and named as a P52 item in that cycle's handoff §0.3 · ✔MEASURED load-bearing 2026-09-02 while fixing [[D-GATE-LANE-GUARD-SELFTEST-REQUIRES-AN-EXECUTABLE-BIT-NOTHING-USES]]; the probe whose misdirection demonstrated it, with the diagnosis in its own header, is `scratchpad/p52/posix-lane-guard-probe.sh` · the seam: `scripts/lane-worktree/lane-worktree.sh`, function `_repo_root`, and every path derived from it in `cmd_remove` (`abs`, the scratchpad gate's `pad`) and `cmd_add` · the resolvers to reuse rather than duplicate: `leg_tree_driver_identity` / `leg_tree_driver_git` in `scripts/leg-tree/leg-tree.sh` (`.sh`) and `Get-RepoTreeIdentity` / `Enter-RepoTree` in `scripts/repo-tree/repo-tree.ps1` (`.ps1`) · the row that built the `.ps1` owner and closed the same defect class for the guards: [[D-SCRIPT-GUARDS-ASK-GIT-FROM-THE-LANE-WORKTREE]] (CLOSED P51) · the verb whose evidence-preservation gate is rooted on this value, so a wrong root silently disarms it: [[D-CYCLE-LANE-WORKTREE-REMOVE-DISCARDS-AN-UNPRESERVED-SCRATCHPAD]] (CLOSED P50) · [[feedback-an-instrument-that-answers-an-adjacent-question]] (`rev-parse` answers "what repo am I standing in?" while being asked "what repo do I belong to?") · [[feedback-a-partial-fix-reads-as-a-complete-one]] (P51 closed the guards' half of this class and left the verb's half, and the result read as closed) |

## Allowlist (code-internal pins, NOT deferred work)

Anchor-shaped strings **in any root the guard scans** that are **not**
deferred-work markers and should be **ignored** by the CI guard. Add a row
when an in-code constant, a diagnostic-message identifier, or a purely
test-local pin matches the `D-*` regex but isn't a deferred-work anchor.

⚠ **THE SCOPE SENTENCE ABOVE SAID "in `src/`" UNTIL 2026-08-14 (AP6 Lane G2), AND
THAT WAS THE STALE HALF OF THIS SECTION, NOT A COSMETIC ONE.** The guard's roots
are `src/`, `examples/`, `tests/`, `integrated_tests/` and `real-examples/`
(`scripts/check-anchor-registry/check-anchor-registry.sh` `_ROOT_SPECS`, `.ps1` `$RootSpecs` — one table
per driver, per-root floors, and the two MUST stay byte-comparable). Written as
`src/`-only, the preamble read as a REFUSAL to allowlist a test-side pin, which
is exactly the class the AP6 widening surfaced en masse — so the sentence would
have pushed seven honest test-local pins toward fake registry rows. ⓘ The
`Pattern` column is matched by the same substring resolve every other `.plans/`
citation uses: there is **no separate allowlist parser** in either driver, so a
row here resolves its name by BEING here. ⚠ That means an entry is a real
decision with real reach — it silences the name everywhere, permanently. Do not
add one to quiet a red; add one only when there is genuinely nothing to do.

| Pattern | Reason |
|---|---|
| `D-MERGE-GLOBAL-INITFUNC` | Cycle 25 Stage B code-internal INVARIANT pin (`src/mir/merge/mir_merge.cpp`), NOT deferred work. Cited in the fail-loud abort that fires if a merged global's `initFunc` MirFuncId was not cloned into the merged module (`plan.funcMerged` miss). The invariant always holds for valid input — a global's module-init function is a defined function in the same CU, which the merge always clones (an init function is never a cross-CU weak loser). The abort is defense-in-depth: a breach means the planning pass dropped a function a global still references (a structural contradiction), so failing loud beats emitting a global whose initializer points at a stale/absent func. No closing work — it is a permanent guard, like the other `mergeFatal` invariant aborts. |
| `D-FF1-PARTIAL-CORRUPTION-WAE-PIN` | Test-local PIN NAME for the `--warnings-as-errors` elevation arm of `F_BinaryReaderPartialCorruption`, NOT deferred work. Cited at `tests/ffi/test_binary_reader.cpp:866` (ELF arm) and `tests/ffi/test_binary_reader_macho.cpp:503` (Mach-O arm); both tests EXIST and pass. The contract they witness — the unsuppressable gate bypasses SILENCING (`--suppress` plus severity overrides) but must NOT bypass ELEVATION (`warningsAsErrors`) — is additionally pinned format-INDEPENDENTLY at the policy layer by `tests/core/test_unsuppressable_codes.cpp` (`UnsuppressableHonorsWarningsAsErrorsEvenWhenSuppressed`, plus the two silencing-bypass pins on the same code). No closing work. ⚠ ✔MEASURED 2026-08-14 (AP6 Lane G2): the Mach-O site's comment says it *"mirrors the ELF + PE WAE pins"* and **there is no PE WAE arm** — `tests/ffi/test_binary_reader_pe.cpp` contains ZERO `warningsAsErrors` occurrences. That is a stale COMMENT, not a coverage hole: WAE elevation is reporter-POLICY behaviour and is identical whichever reader produced the diagnostic, so a third reader-path copy would witness nothing the policy-layer pin does not already. Recorded here rather than fixed because the comment is outside Lane G2's file ownership. |
| `D-LIR-BUILDER-OPERAND-COUNT-GATE` | Test-local TRIPWIRE label on a single negative test, NOT deferred work. Cited once, at `tests/asm/test_lk10_entry_slice_a.cpp:359`. It records WHICH TIER rejects a stray immediate on a zero-operand opcode: `LirBuilder::addInst` (`src/asm/lir.cpp:210-227`) checks only that the opcode is non-zero and known — it does NOT enforce the target schema's `minOperands`/`maxOperands`, which `x86_64.target.json:8` `$reservedFields` itself declares *"NOT yet fully enforced"* — so the encoder's variant-guard match (`walker_util.hpp::operandsMatchGuard`) is the SOLE gate, and the test asserts the SPECIFIC diagnostic code that gate emits rather than a permissive `errorCount > 0`. ★ Nothing is unprotected: the malformed input IS rejected, loudly, one tier later. The anchor exists so that IF a builder-tier gate is ever added, the next reader knows this test will begin failing at `addInst` on a different code path, and that this signals an intentional substrate change rather than a regression. No closing work — it is a note about tier placement, not a request for one. |
| `D-LK6-14-PAYLOAD-PIN` | File-level LABEL naming an existing unit-test suite, NOT deferred work. Cited once, at `tests/link/test_macho_chained_fixups.cpp:1`, where it names the whole file's subject: 8 direct tests over `buildChainedFixupsPayload` (`src/link/format/macho_chained_fixups.hpp`), hoisted out of `macho.cpp` at the d312c1c audit fold. They pin the LC_DYLD_CHAINED_FIXUPS `__LINKEDIT` payload's byte structure — 24-byte header field offsets, the starts_in_image region, imports packing (libOrdinal in the low 8 bits, weak_import at bit 8, name_offset at bits 9..31), the symbols pool's leading-NUL sentinel, the empty-imports edge case and the 23-bit name_offset boundary — INDEPENDENTLY of the `encodeExec*` integration that consumes them, which is the point: a regression in the primitive would otherwise only surface through every chained-fixups binary at once. The suite SHIPS and is green. No closing work. |
| `D-LK6-14-SIZEOFCMDS-DELTA-PIN` | Test-local PIN NAME on one existing test, NOT deferred work. Cited once, at `tests/link/test_macho_writer.cpp:3475`, naming `MachOExecWriter.ChainedFixupsSizeofcmdsDelta` — which asserts that the chained-fixups path's `sizeofcmds` is exactly `kDysymtabCommandSize` (80) LESS than the legacy path's, LC_DYSYMTAB being dropped on the chained path. The two fixtures carry a deliberately IDENTICAL entry cluster (required by `validate()` on an MH_EXECUTE schema), so the pinned delta isolates the LC_DYLD_INFO_ONLY/LC_DYSYMTAB swap and nothing else — an arithmetic pin, which is why it is stated as a delta rather than as two absolute numbers that would both drift. The test EXISTS and passes. No closing work. |
| `D-TEST-DIAG-SEVERITY-EXACT-PIN` | Hoist-THRESHOLD note on a shared test helper, NOT deferred work. Cited once, at `tests/test_support/diagnostic_count.hpp:23`. It records that the `bool sawX` plus exact-severity plus exact-`errorCount()` assertion TRIO is a SINGLETON today (`tests/ffi/test_binary_reader.cpp`'s Elf64 partial-corruption test) and that a SECOND test combining the same trio is the trigger to hoist an `expectExactlyOneDiagWith(rep, code, severity, expectedErrorCount)` helper next to `countCode`. ✔MEASURED 2026-08-14 (AP6 Lane G2): zero occurrences of that helper name repo-wide outside the note itself, so the trigger has NOT fired. This is the project's standing *"hoist once N structurally-identical consumers exist"* convention recorded AT THE SITE — self-executing by whoever writes the second consumer, not a task anyone owes today. ★ The note also carries the NEGATIVE measurement that makes it safe to act on: the 97 other `bool sawX` sites use DISJOINT assertion combinations (payload fields, spans, multi-code) and must not be swept into the hoist. No closing work. |
| `D-TEST-LE-READ-SIGNED` | Hoist-THRESHOLD note on one local helper, NOT deferred work. Cited once, at `tests/link/test_pe_writer.cpp:57`. `readI16LE` is the only SIGNED little-endian reader in the link tests and is PE-only (the COFF symbol record's `SectionNumber` field is an `int16`, and a `.reloc` site reads it); the note says keep it local until a 2nd signed-LE consumer lands — the same convention that DID fire for the unsigned readers and closed [[D-TEST-LE-READ-HELPERS]]. ✔MEASURED 2026-08-14 (AP6 Lane G2): `readI16LE` has exactly one definition and every call site is inside `test_pe_writer.cpp`, so the trigger is unfired and hoisting today would promote a helper to a shared header for a single consumer — the partial-hoist mistake the sibling anchor's own history records as *"strictly worse than either consistent choice"*. No closing work. |
| `D-TEST-MULTI-PAGE-FIXTURE-INVARIANT` | Test-local FIXTURE-INVARIANT pin, NOT deferred work — the same permanent-guard shape as `D-MERGE-GLOBAL-INITFUNC` above. Cited once, at `tests/link/test_macho_writer.cpp:3564`, on the 513-extern fixture that trips the multi-page `__DATA_CONST` guard (4 KiB page divided by the 8-byte `__got` slot = 512 fit, 513 spills). It pins the two boundaries the fixture must NOT cross incidentally: all 513 externs share libSystem, so `libOrdinal` stays 1 and never approaches the 127 ceiling, and the symbols pool stays far under 8 MiB, so it never trips the name_offset overflow guard instead. ★ THE POINT IS THE FAILURE MODE IT PREVENTS: without the invariant, a later fixture edit — a second dylib, longer symbol names — would silently RE-ROUTE the test onto a different rejection surface while still going green, so the test would keep passing for the wrong reason and the multi-page guard would go unwitnessed. Permanent by nature: it is a standing constraint on the fixture, to be re-verified whenever the fixture is touched. No closing work. |
